# Changelog

The running list of what changed in every version of the Ants remake, newest first. The version number lives in
`include/ants_app/version.hpp` and is shown on screen next to the FPS meter (bottom right of every screen).
This file is updated with every release, together with the [README](README.md); it is also published on the beta site at
[beta.playants.org/changelog.html](https://beta.playants.org/changelog.html).

How to read it: versions before v0.0.24 approximated the original 1998 game; from v0.0.24 on every system was re-derived from
the disassembly of `Ants.exe` and replaced (the "Original ..." series). "Rewritten tests" are old tests that encoded behaviour
the original does not have; they were changed to the verified behaviour, never deleted or disabled (each commit message lists them).
Ground truth for every entry is in [`docs/GAME_REVERSE_ENGINEERING.md`](docs/GAME_REVERSE_ENGINEERING.md); the network port is described in
[`docs/NETWORK_PORT.md`](docs/NETWORK_PORT.md).

## Unreleased - The game is 16:9 by default: the wide match screen (M3) with the fixes of its review and the score boxes spread over the strip, a 16:9 canvas behind --aspect (M2) and one screen layout (M1)

Milestone M1 of the widescreen / zoom / touch work (section 52 of the plan): the places that hard-coded the original's 640 x 480 screen read one layout now. **Nothing changes for a player**: the classic picture and the pointer are pixel for pixel and step for step what they were (the 307 fingerprints of the safety net of v0.0.92 are unchanged, and so is every other suite).

- **`ScreenLayout` (`include/ants_app/screen_layout.hpp`, pure, no SDL)** says where everything of the picture is: the size (640 x 480 = `classic()`, the original's numbers; `with_size(W, H)` for a bigger canvas), the map view (16, 21, 442 + dx, 440 + dy), the minimap, the chat log's view, the right panel's backing fill and the score slots (the local team's in the top bar, the others' in the bottom strip; a slot past the third repeats it, so more than four players are one function's change). `dx = W - 640` is what is anchored to the right edge (the right panel, the top bar's buttons, the score boxes), `dy = H - 480` what is anchored to the bottom edge (the chat box, the [All] and [Team] buttons, the bottom strip), and the map view takes the rest. The classic numbers are the original's, written out independently in the tests.
- **Who reads it.** The renderer (the view's clip, the culling of terrain, objects, ants, effects, markers, fog, bubbles and digits, the camera's origin and size: `Renderer::set_layout`), the HUD (`HUD::set_layout`: the rectangles of the buttons, the three pedestal slots, the zones of the map, the minimap and the chat log, the backing fill, the minimap and its view frame, the status line, the chat input and its caret, the score boxes, the rubber band, the cursor, the clicks, Ctrl+N, the chat log's window), the edge scroll and the minimap drag and the start view (`edge_scroll.hpp`: overloads with a layout; the old signatures stay and are the classic layout's), the pointer's limits (`pointer_clamp.hpp`), the frame-rate plate and the version (`CornerPlate`: the corner of the canvas, rule 7) and the latency texts next to them, the network overlay's box, and the application (the pointer gate, the sound listener, the first pointer position, the start view: `Application::set_layout`).
- **One place places the HUD's rectangles.** `HUD::apply_layout` is run by `init()` (so by every new match) and by `set_layout()`: a layout that was set earlier is not lost when the next match resets the HUD (the old `init` assigned the classic numbers).
- **Removed, because it never ran**: `Renderer::set_fullscreen`, the `integer_scale` argument and member of `Renderer::init` and `ApplicationConfig::integer_scaling`. The integer scale was never enabled, so the game has always scaled by SDL's logical size alone: the largest scale that fits, centred, a fractional scale unless the window is a multiple of 640 x 480 (1920 x 1080 shows the picture at 2.25x with bars), nearest-neighbour filtering. **The README's claims of "integer scaling" and a "pixel-crisp 4:3 display" were wrong and are corrected.** What `set_fullscreen` did besides (it applied the logical size again on every size event of the window) is `Renderer::refit_canvas`, which the size events call.
- **Left for M3 and M4** (listed in `docs/audit/M0_notes.md`): the parts of the original's animations that carry absolute screen coordinates (the shell, the pedestals' art, the egg tray, the Stop button, the chat cover, the buttons' hover art), the dialogs and pages (the quit and alliance dialogs, the options, setup, results, quick help and loading screens), the pedestal's own drawing, the window's grid.
- **Tests: a new suite 3.13 (`tests/test_app/test_screen_layout.cpp`, 348 checks)**: the model against the original's numbers (the view, the minimap, the chat log, the panel, the four score slots, the eight edge strips written out, the zones at every pixel against `HUD::in_map_rect` and `in_minimap_rect`), the relations that hold in every size (the panel reaches the edges, the view meets the panel and the bottom strip), the HUD's rectangles with the classic layout one by one and with `with_size(960, 540)` (**exactly** the right-anchored ones moved by 320 and the bottom-anchored ones by 60, the quit dialog's buttons stay), a layout that survives `init`, `reset` and a new match, presses on the pedestals where the layout puts them, the HUD's drawing (backing fill, minimap and its view frame, status line, chat input and caret, the four score boxes), the cursor, the rubber band, the minimap drag and right click, Ctrl+N, the chat log's window, the eight strips of the scroll and the steps they give, the minimap's point and the start view, the pointer's limits and the placing of a picture inside a canvas, the plate's corner and the latency texts and the overlay's box, the camera, the renderer (the clip, the culling at the layout's size, SDL's logical size) and the application (listener, pointer gate and limits, start view, the plate in the canvas's corner, a size event). **The renderer is checked against itself**: whatever the classic layout draws for a window of the world (terrain, objects, 300 ants with ears and digits, effects, score bubbles, the click marker, the hill's brackets, fog of war) is, pixel for pixel, a part of what the 960 x 540 layout draws for the larger window (five offsets), and a view whose corner is (40, 30) draws the same picture as the one at (16, 21), moved.
- **Proven by mutation** (one change at a time in a scratch copy, the suite rebuilt and run, the file restored; the table is in `docs/audit/M0_notes.md`): 76 mutations of the model, the scroll, the pointer, the plate, the HUD, the renderer and the application (a view that does not grow, a rectangle that is not anchored, a classic constant back at one site, a layout that is lost at `init`, a size event that does not apply the canvas ...); 75 are killed (the first run left three, Ctrl+N, the chat log's limit and the rubber band's corner, which got their tests) and one is equivalent (the edge scroll's pre-test of the quiet area can never change an answer).
- **Rewritten tests**: `test_render_parity` and `test_view_fingerprint` each call `Renderer::init(win, archive, false)`; the dead third argument is gone, so it is `Renderer::init(win, archive)` (one line each; no assertion, no number changed).
- **Documents**: README (the scaling text corrected in four places, the suite's row), `docs/audit/M0_notes.md` (what M1 replaced, what is left, the mutations), `run_tests.sh` (suite 3.13).

**M2: a fixed 16:9 canvas behind `--aspect` (the default is still the classic picture).** The owner's decision for M2: the 16:9 picture is a FIXED logical canvas of 960 x 540, so everybody who asks for it sees exactly the same world area (no caps in online rooms); SDL scales the canvas into the window by the largest scale that fits, centred, with bars; fitting the shape of the monitor is a later option.

- **`--aspect 16:9` / `--aspect 4:3` and the settings key `aspect`** (native builds; the command line wins; anything else is refused with "only 16:9 and 4:3 for now" and the game does not start, a bad value in the settings file is reported and ignored). 4:3 is the original's fixed 640 x 480 canvas and was the default in M2 (M3 below flips it on a desktop); the web build takes both too (its page passes `--aspect`; 16:9 is its default since the web page became 16:9).
- **The canvas** (`include/ants_app/canvas_layout.hpp`, pure): `CanvasLayout::fit(window)` is SDL's own arithmetic for a logical size, so the program can say where the picture is in the window: 1920 x 1080 shows 960 x 540 at 2x exactly, 3840 x 2160 at 4x, 2560 x 1440 at 2.667x, 1280 x 720 at 1.333x, 2880 x 1800 at 3x with bars of 90 rows above and below; the scale is a whole number exactly when the limiting side of the window is a multiple of the canvas's (the classic canvas in a 1920 x 1080 window is 2.25x with uneven pixels, as it has always been). The window of a 16:9 game opens at the **largest scale in steps of 0.5 of 960 x 540 that fits the usable part of the display** (at least 1x; the first version of M2 took the largest whole multiple, which left a laptop at 1x: see the review fixes below; `--window-size` and `--grid` still win and the grid's cells are 16:9); fullscreen is the same canvas as large as the monitor allows.
- **In M2 the match screen and the original's pages are still the 640 x 480 picture**, drawn centred in the 960 x 540 canvas (the wide HUD with more of the map is M3, below: the pages stay so, the match screen does not): `Renderer::set_picture` gives everything the renderer draws the picture's corner and makes the picture the clip, the pointer's canvas position becomes the picture's own (the black bars count as its nearest edge pixel: the map scrolls from a bar, as the v0.0.92 pillarbox rule says), and the frame-rate plate and the version stay in the bottom right corner of the canvas (AGENTS.md rule 7).
- **Alt+Enter toggles fullscreen** (native builds): the key belongs to the window, no screen sees it (Enter alone is still START and the chat's send), a held key toggles once.
- **A known limit, found with the screenshots (not new)**: at a scale that is not a whole number SDL's software renderer (headless runs and the screenshot tool) rounds every sprite's rectangle, and neighbouring tiles can leave a 1 px black seam (a grid at 1.333x, a seam every few tiles at 2.667x; none at 2x, 3x or 4x). The classic picture does the same at any fractional scale (800 x 600). Whether the accelerated renderers keep their edges watertight is to be checked on a real window; the notes in `docs/audit/M0_notes.md` name the remedies. The default stays the classic picture.
- **`Renderer::save_screenshot` writes the whole picture as the window shows it** (the canvas scaled into the window, without the bars). It used to write a file of the window's size with the picture at its top left corner, which is right only when the window has the canvas's shape (a 16:9 canvas in a 1280 x 960 window gave a 1280 x 960 file with a quarter of it empty).
- **Tests: a new suite 3.14 (`tests/test_app/test_canvas_layout.cpp`, 313 checks)**: the table of window sizes, the rules over two thousand window sizes and the model against SDL's own layout of 32 real windows, the centred picture, the windows that open and the grid, `--aspect` and the key (refusals, precedence), the application's canvas, picture and window, **the 16:9 application's match screen and setup screen are, pixel for pixel, the 4:3 application's frame centred in the canvas** (M3 rewrote the match part), the bars black, the plate in the canvas's corner, the renderer's picture (origin, clip, cached text, the digits, an RGBA image), the pointer over every bar and far beyond the window, Alt+Enter, and the screenshot of seven canvas / window pairs. **Proven by mutation**: 55 mutations of the model, the window, the renderer and the application, all killed (the first run left five, each got a test); the table is in `docs/audit/M0_notes.md`. The classic path is unchanged: the 307 fingerprints and every other suite pass.
- **Documents**: README (`--aspect`, the settings key, Alt+Enter, the window and fullscreen text, the suite's row), `docs/audit/M0_notes.md` (what M2 does and what M3 needs, the mutations), `run_tests.sh` (suite 3.14).

**M3: the wide match screen, and the game is 16:9 by default (desktop builds).** The owner's priority (2026-10-02: "Please make making the game 16x9 a priority. The default should be 16x9, and eventually we will give people the options to match the aspect ratio of their monitor."; earlier: "it shows more of the map so you get a wider view"). **What a player sees.** The desktop game opens in a 16:9 picture (a fixed canvas of 960 x 540, scaled into the window by the largest scale that fits, centred, with bars where the window's shape differs): the **map view is 762 x 500 pixels instead of 442 x 440, about twice the area of the world at once**, and everybody who plays the same aspect sees exactly the same area. `--aspect 4:3` (or `aspect=4:3` in the settings file) gives the original's picture, exactly as before; the web build is still the 4:3 picture until its page can show another shape.

- **The frame is the original's own art, grown by repeating one line of its pieces** (the owner's idea: "I sliced out one pixel and then just repeated that one pixel"). `include/ants_app/shell_layout.hpp` (pure) classifies the 14 pieces of the original's frame animation by what they are anchored to (right edge, bottom edge) and says which six grow, each by ONE line inside a plain part of the art (measured, never inside a decoration, a score box or the clock box): the top bar at column 140, the bottom strip at THREE columns, 15, 188 and 346 (the plain band left of the first score box, and the plain runs 186 - 190 and 345 - 348 left of the second and third team's labels: one cut in each gap between the score boxes' recesses, never inside the recesses nor the columns 458 - 482, which are the panel's own fill), the left strip at row 300 (below the horizontal rule), and, in the right panel, the strip beside the map, the chat box and the right edge strip at ONE canvas row, 357, where all three are plain between their ant decorations (the owner, 2026-10-02: "further down on the chat there's a spot that can repeat cleanly"; the row he rejected stretched an ant decoration into a bar). The right panel is pinned to the right edge, the chat log is 60 px taller, the map view takes the rest. `HUD::render_shell` draws the pieces in the original's order; a stretched one in three parts with the new `IRenderer::draw_sprite_region` (default: nothing, so the test renderers compile); the original's picture is the 14 plain pieces, which the 307 fingerprints of the safety net keep proving.
- **The panel's art moves with its anchor**: the pedestals (`PedestalSlot::draw` takes an offset), the glow, the egg tray, the Stop button, the lunchbox indicator and the top bar's hover and pressed art by (320, 0); the chat cover and the Send to buttons by (320, 60).
- **Score boxes by slot, spread over the strip** (the owner: "Can you expand between the scores so they're not all offset to the right?"; M3 had the three boxes together at the right end of the bottom strip next to the panel): the local team's is in the top bar, the others' in the bottom strip, and the strip's extra width is spread over THREE plain cuts of its art (piece columns 15, 188 and 346, one left of each box), shared in thirds with the remainder going to the left cut: at 960 x 540 the cuts add 108, 106 and 106 pixels and the boxes sit at **x 213, 468 and 722** (255 and 254 apart, as the original's own boxes are 149 and 148), their labels staying with them and the art under each box being its own recess (a test compares the composed frame with the strip's art box by box). A box and its label move by what the cuts left of them add (`ScreenLayout::cut_share`, one rule for the frame and the slots), so the same holds at every width. The strip keeps the original's three slots at every width (M3's further slots, a pitch left of the first, have no room left once the boxes are spread, and were removed); a strip for more slots (more than four players is still its own project) would get one more cut for each, with the extra width shared equally.
- **The original's windows in the bigger picture.** The loading screen, the quick help at the start, the setup screen and the room, and the results stay the original's 640 x 480 pages, **centred in the canvas over a margin of the clay colour of the pages** (the loading screen's own fill; black was the other candidate, both were looked at). **The two windows that open during a match are over the map view with the frame around them, as the original's picture has them** (M3 filled the canvas with clay and drew a centred page, which hid the whole HUD): the options window (its 442 x 440 card) and the quick help (its 640 x 480 page) are centred in the 762 x 500 view, at (160, 30) and (77, 31), drawn clipped to the view (the options art's dither shadow reaches beyond the card); a new `IRenderer::set_origin` draws a window with its own numbers where the picture wants it, and the pointer is taken back to them in every handler **and in the 50 ms poll of `HUD::update`**. The dialogs of a match (quit, the three alliance dialogs, "get ready") are centred over the map view too. The original's pages themselves are unchanged.
- **Small maps**: a map smaller than the view (a 16 x 16 map is 512 px wide) is centred in it with black around it and the camera is fixed on that axis; the pointer over the black is the plain arrow, a click there does nothing at all (no deselect, no order, no marker, a latched pedestal stays latched), nothing that hangs over the map's edge is drawn onto the black (the world's clip is the part of the view that the map covers: an ant, an effect or a tall object at the edge is cut there; the review's 15 x 15 level had a gummy worm 65 px out on the black), the minimap's frame is the whole image on that axis. The original's camera keeps its own rule (the classic fingerprints pin it).
- **Everything that reads the view size reads the 762 x 500 view**: the camera's limits, the culling, the edge strips (they run along the edges of the whole 960 x 540 picture), the minimap's drag and its view frame, the start view of a match, the rubber band, the cursor and the clicks, the sound listener, the network overlay. The pointer clamp, the grid and the frame-rate plate were done in M1 and M2.
- **The flip**: `kPlatformDefaultAspect` (`canvas_layout.hpp`) is 16:9 on a desktop and 4:3 under Emscripten, and `Application::parse_arguments` puts it into the config; `--aspect` and the settings key win; a config made by hand keeps the original's 4:3, which is how the whole 1:1 test base stays on the classic picture without editing a test. **Flipping the web is that one constant** (M5 builds the page).
- **The start scripts' four windows are 16:9 cells** now (the scripts say nothing about the shape: the game's default decides; each window is the largest 16:9 rectangle of its cell).
- **The independent review of this work, and its fixes (folded into this entry).** An adversarial review of the three commits (drivers that click every control through real frames, maps of 12 and 15 tiles, a four-team network match with a 16:9 and a 4:3 machine, 60 mutations of its own) found one high, two medium and five low findings and one note. Each is fixed and has a test that fails without it:
  - **High: in the 16:9 match Return and the ON / OFF switches of the options window, and the quick help's Return, could not be clicked** (and opening the window by clicking the HUD's Options button made no difference: a second click on Return did nothing either). The 50 ms poll in `HUD::update` re-fed the open window the raw pointer without the window's offset, which the event handlers subtract: the poll took the pointer off the control and cancelled the press. `HUD::open_window_offset()` is the one place now, for the handlers, the poll and the drawing. The tests press, poll with `HUD::update` and release every control of both windows in both pictures, and open each window by clicking the HUD's Options and Help buttons with updates between every event (the review's own mutation of this line had survived every suite).
  - **Medium: the ping and delay of a four-team 16:9 network match stood on the third score box**: the limit of the readout's row in a match was the original's picture's 460 in every picture. It is the layout's now: `ScreenLayout::score_row_right()`, the right edge of the last bottom box's cover and 2 pixels (460 in the original's picture, 780 at 960 x 540); tested with two, three and four teams in four pictures against the boxes and covers that the HUD draws, and in the application's own frame of a 16:9 and a 4:3 network match.
  - **Medium: the first window of a 16:9 game was small** (the largest WHOLE multiple of 960 x 540: 960 x 540 points on a 1800 x 1130 display, where 1.875x fits). The owner decided: the largest scale in steps of 0.5 (1x, 1.5x, 2x, 2.5x ...) that fits the display's usable area, at least 1x (`default_canvas_window`; 1.5x of a Retina display's points is 3x in pixels, crisp); fullscreen is unchanged. A table of six displays (1800 x 1130 gives 1440 x 810, 1440 x 875 the same, 1920 x 1040 the same, 2560 x 1400 gives 2400 x 1350, 3840 x 2100 gives 3360 x 1890, 1366 x 728 gives 960 x 540) and the width and height limits one pixel either side of every step.
  - **Low: nothing hangs over the edge of a small map onto the black** (`Renderer::map_view_rect`, above); **the options window and the quick help of a match are over the map view with the frame around them** (above); **the pointer is held at a page's nearest edge pixel when the screen changes** (a pointer left of x = 160 became (-60, 240) when the results opened, and the cursor vanished); **a game that starts with `--fullscreen` has the canvas's window to go back to with Alt+Enter** (it kept the config's 1280 x 960, a 4:3 shape); **a click on the black of a small map does nothing at all** (it deselected), and neither does a right press that began there; and the boundary rows and columns of a map smaller than the view on both axes are pinned (the review's mutation 'one row of ground above the map' had survived).
  - **Info: `--window-size`** takes `WxH` as well as `W,H`, and anything else (a sign, a space, a third number, a size under 320x240) is refused with a message and the game does not start (it ignored `1280x720` silently).
- **A known limit, looked at again**: the 1 px seams between sprites at a fractional scale are a property of SDL's software renderer (the headless screenshots): with the accelerated renderer (Metal, a 5.333x scale, sampled over the whole map view) there is none, so the "render into a target texture and scale once" fix was not made (it would blur the TrueType text on a 4K screen). Notes in `docs/audit/M0_notes.md`.
- **Tests: a new suite 3.15 (`tests/test_app/test_wide_hud.cpp`, 609 checks with the review fixes)**: the anchoring of the 14 pieces at 960 x 540 and five other sizes (place, size, spans tiling each piece), the cuts (each repeated line in a maximal run of identical lines of its piece's plain part; the panel's fill shown to be identical too and refused; the right panel's three pieces cut at one canvas row and plain there; the owner's rejected row failing the same test), the composition (pixel for pixel the owner-approved mock-up's rule, written out again independently, at seven sizes; each stretched piece at its original size is the original), the real renderer (the frame pixel for pixel; the origin of a window), the HUD (the calls in order, every moved animation, the score slots of six rosters, the dialogs and pages and the pointer in every handler), the view (the camera on big and small maps, every pixel of the edge strips, the start view of six maps, the minimap's frame, the cursor and clicks on a small map) and the application (the picture of each screen and the pointer's move, the clay, the default, the grid's window shape, the bars of 16:10 and 21:9 windows). **New fingerprints in suite 3.10: 226 more ("*.wide.*": 82 HUD draw-call scenes, 87 pointer and camera families over all 518,400 pixels of the wide picture, 57 pixel hashes of the real renderer incl. whole application frames); the 307 classic numbers did not move. The review fixes measured 105 of the wide ones again, deliberately (all of them draw the bottom strip, now in seven parts with the boxes at x 213 / 468 / 722; seven also the options window or the quick help in its new place); the pointer sweeps, the cameras and the world pixels did not change.** Start-script test: no window gets `--aspect` or `--window-size`.
- **Proven by mutation** (one change at a time in a scratch copy, the six affected suites rebuilt and run, the file restored; the table is in `docs/audit/M0_notes.md`): 115 mutations of the sources, **110 killed** and five that survive for a reason (the picture of a screen is updated in several places on purpose, four of the five are caught when the places are removed together; one is unreachable code); **67 more for the review fixes and the spread, all killed** (64 by tests, three at the build by the `static_assert` that keeps the original's limit of 460, each with a wide-only variant that the tests kill; a 68th, a second separator in `--window-size`, survived because the number test refuses it anyway: the dead check was removed).
- **Rewritten tests** (their assertions encoded M2's interim state, in which the match screen was the classic picture centred in the 16:9 canvas, or the old rule for a slot past the last): `test_screen_layout` ("a slot past the fourth is the fourth" at 960 x 540: slots 4 and 5 were real slots in M3, and the review fixes took them out again with the spread: three bottom slots at every width, at x 213 / 468 / 722 at 960 x 540, so its three slot lines and the corner's row-or-stacked lines, which took the original's 460 and added 160 by hand, say that now; the original's picture keeps its rule); `test_wide_hud` (its own lines of M3 that encoded the clay page behind the in-match options and quick help, the five bottom slots, the one cut of the strip, 18 calls in parts, and a click on the black that deselects: they say the new behaviour); `test_canvas_layout` (the window table of whole multiples: steps of 0.5; the fullscreen start); `test_view_fingerprint` (105 wide golden numbers, above); `test_start_menu_app` A10.1 (v0.0.97's: it parses a command line, which is the 16:9 picture on a desktop since M3, so it says `--aspect 4:3` to keep checking the menu's own 640 x 480 picture; the new A10.1b takes the same screenshot at 16:9 and finds the menu's page centred at (160, 30) over the clay, pixel for pixel the 4:3 one: the start menu needed nothing else); `test_canvas_layout` (3.14): the default of `--aspect` (16:9 on a desktop), "the match screen is still the classic picture" and its picture checks (the match is the wide frame and the whole canvas; the unstretched art is the 4:3 application's pixels), "the bars around the picture are black" (the frame fills the canvas), and the pointer group (the bars of a match are the window's: a 16:10 and a 21:9 window; the original's pages keep the pointer clamp over their margin). Every other test (the application integration suite, the network application suite, the 506 E2E rows, the classic fingerprints) is unchanged.
- **Documents**: README (16:9 by default and `--aspect 4:3`, what the wider view shows, the settings key, the grid), `docs/audit/M0_notes.md` (what M3 did, the cuts, the consumers of the view size for M4, the seams, the mutations, and the section "Review fixes of the widescreen work"), `run_tests.sh` (suite 3.15). The three suites that this branch adds (3.13, 3.14, 3.15) define `main(int, char**)`, as SDL2main needs on Windows.

## v0.0.98 - 2026-10-02 - Reconnect, part A: the server can hold a lost player's seat (off by default)

The owner asked: "if a user disconnects can we give them some time to reconnect? maybe ask the other players if they want to continue without the player after some time?", then "if your browser crashed or your power went out, you could communicate with those players and they could hold the game paused until you get back", and, for the moment the match goes on again, "instead of instantly restarting ... a 10 second countdown". This release is **part A: the server's side** (the design, the messages, the flows and the limits are in [`docs/NETWORK_PORT.md`](docs/NETWORK_PORT.md), "Reconnect (protocol 10)"): **a room that is told to hold seats keeps the seat of a player whose connection is lost and pauses the match for everybody; the others may vote to go on without the seat; the player comes back with its key, is given the match again from the server's turn log, runs it without drawing it, and plays on once the referee agrees with its state; after a pause the match is held for a 10 s countdown before it goes on.** The independent review of this release found two high and three medium problems; they are fixed here (first below), then part 2 (the sessions and the server), then part 1 (the building blocks).

**What players see now: nothing changes until a server operator switches it on** (`--reconnect` for a server, `"reconnect": true` for one room), and **the games do not come back yet**: part B (the game keeps its key in the page's storage or in a file, rejoins by itself also after a crash or a power cut, shows the pause, the countdown and the vote, "Rejoin" in the start menu and on the page) is next and is what turns it on. A game on the local network, a room that does not hold seats, the web page and every screen and rule of the game are exactly what they were in v0.0.97 (no simulation rule, screen or golden hash changed; S3.50 and N2.69 play a room that does not hold seats exactly as before, and in the real checks below a cut seat is dropped at once, as before). **One thing does change for everybody: the network protocol is version 10** (v0.0.97: 9). The Hello and the Welcome carry a key and five messages are new, so **a v0.0.97 game cannot join a v0.0.98 server's room or LAN game, and the other way round**: it is told "version mismatch" (the start menu says "This game is v0.0.97, but the server runs another version of the game. Update the game, or wait until the server is updated.", and the LAN list names the room as another version); update the game, the server and the page together.

- **The server options and the room keys.** `ants_server --reconnect` (rooms hold seats unless their own `reconnect` says no; `--no-reconnect` is the default), `--hold-vote-seconds N` (the others may vote on going on without a seat once it has been away N seconds in all: 5 - 3600, default 30), `--max-pause-seconds N` (the cap on a match's total paused time: 60 - 86,400, default 1,800), `--max-catch-up-seconds N` (the catch-up time of one absence: 10 - 3,600, default 300), `--resume-countdown-seconds N` (0 - 60, default 10, 0 = none) and `--log-mb N` (the turn log of one room: 1 - 256 MiB, default 16; all the rooms' logs together are bounded by 256 MiB). A room made through the control interface takes the keys `reconnect`, `hold_vote_seconds`, `max_pause_seconds`, `max_catch_up_seconds` and `resume_countdown_seconds` (the server's values are its defaults; demo rooms follow the options); the status of a room names what happens (`paused`, `resume_seconds`, `absent` with seat, name, state, seconds away and progress, `vote`, `paused_seconds`, `rejoins`, `rejoins_refused`, `catch_up_expired`, `drops_by_vote`, `drops_by_cap`, `streamed_bytes`, `log`) and holds no key, nor does the result file, `/stats` or any log line.
- **Rebased onto v0.0.97** (v0.0.95's community maps moved the protocol to 9, v0.0.96's web start and hidden tab, v0.0.97's start menu); the conflicts were resolved keeping both sides. `protocol.hpp`: the history runs 8 less lag, 9 community maps, 10 reconnect, with the rule of the number above it. `session.hpp` / `session.cpp`: **`ClientSession::note_gap` and the asked-silence rule of v0.0.96 are one implementation, this release's**, which already contained the same rule and more (it counts the real time of `Rejoining`, `Reconnecting` and `CatchingUp` as well, N2.74); the duplicate `asked_` fields of the merge were removed and the tests of v0.0.96 (suite 3.6, N5.32 - N5.44) pass unchanged. `lobby.hpp`: the start menu's `was_open()` and `welcome_timed_out()` next to this release's `key()` and `rejoined()`. `application.cpp`: the menu's way back to the start menu when the host is lost, with this release's line on stderr. `application_menu.cpp`: the start menu's failure text names the three new rejections (dropped, rejoin failed, taken over; the menu's own Hello never shows a key, so a server does not send them to it) and `A5.2` / `A12.1` test them. `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/NETWORK_PORT.md`: both sides' sections and suite rows. **Renumbered tests** (two tests shared an id; none changed): `test_server` S3.34 / S3.35 of this release (`random_bytes`, keys) are **S3.58 / S3.59** (v0.0.94's laggard tests keep S3.34 / S3.35), `test_jitter` J1.10 of this release (`reset`) is **J1.11**; the lobby test N4.2c (a Hello of an old protocol or another number is refused) now also covers v0.0.97's protocol 8 and 9 Hellos with this protocol's layout.
- **Rewritten tests** (old tests that pinned what this release changes, now pinning the new rule; none deleted, disabled or weakened; each part's list is below): `test_lockstep` **N2.1 - N2.3** (protocol 10, the message types and the reject reasons, the fuzz with the new decoders), `test_lobby` **N4.2c** (a Hello of an old protocol is refused by the number before its layout is read), `test_server` **S3.3** (raw old-layout Hellos at the door), `test_app_integration` **12.108** (the version, v0.0.98: two lines), and, from the review of this release, **N2.40 - N2.44, N2.47, N2.50, N2.54, N2.57, N2.71, N2.73, S3.42, S3.43, S3.48**. Extended, not rewritten: `A5.2` and `A12.1` of the start menu's tests (three more rejections), N4.2c (the Hellos of protocols 8 and 9 with this protocol's layout).

**Review fixes of release A.** An independent review built drivers, mutated the code and ran the real server; its findings are fixed here, each with tests that fail without the fix (the mutations are listed below), and its drivers were run again against the fixed code and the real server image.

- **A seat holder could freeze a room for ever (HIGH).** A Hello every 12 s held a room whose cap was 60 s paused for 176 s with `drops_by_cap` 0 (also against an anonymous demo room): a Hello of a seat that was already catching up gave it a new stall clock and percent 0 and cleared its votes, a seat that is catching up is no subject of a vote, the cap dropped only absent seats, and the server took any number of Hellos. Now: **each absence has a catch-up budget** (5 minutes by default, a room's `max_catch_up_seconds` 10 - 3600, over all the attempts of the absence); **a Hello of an attempt that is in progress changes nothing of it** (stall clock, percent, start); when the budget is spent `update()` fails the catch-up at that moment (the seat is Absent: the vote and the cap apply) and a Hello is refused (`RejoinFailed`); and **the pause cap is absolute: at the cap every seat that is not Present is dropped**, one that is catching up and shows progress too. Real server image, the review's `exp_grief` (a Hello every 12 s, a cap of 60 s): the pause ended at the cap, **60 s** (`paused_seconds` 60, `drops_by_cap` 1, the vote open from 54 s, two Hellos refused by a budget), and the anonymous demo room of `exp_demo_grief` likewise (cap dropped the seat at 60 s); before, 176 s and no drop.
- **A key holder could make the server stream its log again and again (HIGH).** 50 full streams of a log of 14 MB in 10 s (about 69 MB/s) from one key holder; even a normal log of 0.6 MB with 100 Hellos a second made 60 MB/s. Now **a seat's rejoin budget: three Hellos a minute and three times the log's size streamed in ten minutes (at least 1 MiB, so that a log of a few KB is no reason to refuse the fifth reload of a page)**; beyond either, `RejoinFailed`, the seat stays held (the vote and the cap apply), a refusal costs nothing and is counted; the stream is charged as it is sent; the counters are in the status (`rejoins_refused`, `catch_up_expired`, `streamed_bytes`; no key). Real server image, the review's `exp_bigstream2` (a log of 14.1 MB, an unthrottled rejoin, then Hellos for 10 s): **3 streams (42 MB, the 3 x of the log) and then 3,764 Hellos refused in 10 s at no cost** (50 streams of 14 MB before).
- **Short pauses inflated every other player's jitter buffer (MEDIUM).** The others' runners read a pause as a stall: a rejoin pause of 130 - 210 ms took their buffers from 1 to 2 to 4 turns (about 150 ms more input delay for 30 s). Now Presence tells the client that the match is held and its runner knows: it runs the turns it has, waits without a stall (no lateness sample, no `on_stall`, no "Waiting for the other players") and, when the pause ends, forgets what it measured before it (`LockstepRunner::set_held`). The review's `drv_jit` (three cuts of a seat of three, links of 5 and of 20 ms): the others' buffers stay at **1** after every cut and 12 s later.
- **A flapping key holder slowed the match down and could not be voted out (MEDIUM).** A cut every 300 ms kept the match paused 72 % of the time (34 % of normal speed) and the vote was cleared at every return. Now **a seat that is lost three times within a minute flaps and is put to the vote at once, in any state** (missing, catching up, or back), its votes stay across its returns until the others drop it (its link is closed and told "dropped") or a minute passes without a loss; every pause counts at least 5 s toward the pause total, so flapping reaches the cap; and with the Hello budget a seat is refused at its fourth Hello of a minute. The review's `drv_flapspeed` (a cut every 300 ms for 40 s, 133 cuts): the match was paused **1 %** of the time (580 ms) at **99 %** speed and the seat was voted out.
- **A dead log kept its memory and its share of the budget (MEDIUM).** A log that is full (or that the budget refuses) is dead: it **frees what it held at the moment it dies** and gives it back to the server's budget (nothing can be streaming from it: a log only grows while the match runs and a seat is only brought back while it is paused); **the budget counts what a log has allocated (its capacity), not what it stores**. Real server image, `exp_hostile_log3` (a hostile room fills its log to the room's 16 MiB limit next to a good room): the budget's use fell from 26,355,712 bytes to **16,384** (the good room's log) the moment the hostile log died, and the process's memory from 33.2 to 16.5 MB; the good room's log was never touched. Sixteen hostile rooms (S3.56) are dead within seconds and a room made afterwards logs, holds a lost seat and takes it back. Documented limit: the budget is first come, first served (a room whose log has to grow while others hold the whole budget loses its way back; its status says so).
- **The resume countdown (the owner's request: "instead of instantly restarting ... a 10 second countdown").** After a pause of **3 s or more** (a seat back and verified, or a vote or the cap dropped the last seat that was missing) the match stays **held for 10 s** (a room's `resume_countdown_seconds` 0 - 60, 0 = none; `--resume-countdown-seconds`; status `resume_seconds`) before the next turn is sealed: Presence carries the seconds left (`resume_s`, a new byte at the end of the message, 0 - 60 and 0 while a seat is missing; at its start and once a second), chat is relayed, commands are discarded without a violation, a seat that is lost during it cancels it (the pause rules apply and a countdown follows that pause, however short), a pause under 3 s resumes at once; **it is no pause**: it does not count toward the cap or anybody's catch-up time, and the wall-clock limit leaves it out. `ClientSession::resume_seconds_left()` is what release B's overlay will show; the Drops and the resume are the same tick on every machine.
- **Smaller fixes and documents.** Presence goes out at once for a second loss while the match is paused (it could be up to a second late) and once a second, no faster; the client gives up by **the cap that the last Presence named** and a minute (31 minutes before any Presence); `Room::rejoin` keeps at most `max_connections` connections and answers `Full` beyond (a bound of the server, no key in the control interface); `docs/NETWORK_PORT.md` no longer says that a room never waits whatever a seat does (a room that holds seats pauses for a lost connection), nor that a returning seat may try without limit; the `HoldMatch` rig starts a reloaded session with the machine's own clock and a frame count of its own (it put the session's clock back after a long lobby phase: N2.88).
- **New tests**: `test_lockstep` **N2.75 - N2.88** (14 tests) and `test_server` **S3.52 - S3.57** (6 tests), and the end-to-end script (the two new options and their edges, the countdown after the cap against a real server): N2.75 the catch-up budget, N2.76 the rejoin budget (to the millisecond, from four clocks), N2.77 flapping, N2.78 the countdown rules, N2.79 the held runner (pauses of 40 ms to 30 s, no stall, the buffer stays), N2.80 the grief, N2.81 the amplification, N2.82 the jitter buffers after short pauses, N2.83 a flapping connection, N2.84 the countdown through whole matches, N2.85 a dead log, N2.86 the client's give-up, N2.87 Presence timing, N2.88 the rig's clock; S3.52 the countdown through the manager (the limit counts play), S3.53 the new settings and bounds, S3.54 the grief through the door, S3.55 the amplification through the door, S3.56 sixteen hostile rooms, S3.57 the connection bound.
- **Rewritten tests** (each pinned what the review showed to be wrong; changed to the corrected rule, none deleted or disabled): `test_lockstep` **N2.40** (the Presence layout has `resume_s`; a vote may be about any seat), **N2.41** and **N2.73** (a dead log holds nothing; the budget counts capacity), **N2.54** (the same through the session), **N2.42** (every pause counts at least 5 s toward the cap; "a second try: the percent starts again" became "a Hello of an attempt that goes on changes nothing of it"), **N2.43** and **N2.71** (a seat is taken back three times a minute: the blocks that say Hello many times raise the rule), **N2.44** and **N2.50** ("a progressing seat stays CatchingUp past 60 s" became "the cap is absolute"), **N2.47** (the others' runners are held, not stalled; their buffers are exactly 1), **N2.57** (three losses in a minute are put to the vote at the third, not the seventh); `test_server` **S3.42** and **S3.43** (the cuts are spread over the seats and over time: a seat is taken back three times a minute), **S3.48** (a dead log gives its memory back); `held_spec` has the countdown off (every other test would wait 10 s after every return).
- **The review's drivers, run again** (built against the fixed code): the chaos driver (random cuts, reloads, freezes, votes and leaves on the simulated network, clocks near the wrap, 240 s of virtual time a seed): **320 seeds, 0 failed checks**; the 30-minute soak (three seeds, 172 - 177 cuts, 55 - 60 reloads and 11 freezes each): **no desync, every machine and the referee identical, no machine lost** (the soak's cuts are spread so that no seat is cut more than a few times a minute: the review's cut a seat every 3.5 s, which the new budget refuses by design). The real-server numbers of each finding are above.
- **Mutations of the fixes** (a scratch copy of the sources, one change at a time, each run against the tests that must notice it): **80 mutations** of the fixes (the catch-up budget, the absolute cap, the rejoin budget and its byte window, the held runner, flapping, the dead log, Presence's timing, the client's give-up, the countdown, the room's and the control interface's settings, the decoder's ranges, the rig's reload); 5 of them did not compile under `-Werror` (an unused variable) and were redone as compile-clean variants, so **75 compiled mutants: 72 are killed by a test and 3 are equivalent**: the reset of a seat's catch-up time in `lost()` (a Present seat always holds 0: the reset when the absence ends does it), a cap that tests `used > max` instead of `used >= max` (both leave 0 ms), and the rig starting a reloaded session with the real clock instead of the machine's (the session's clock arithmetic is wrap-safe and a clock behind its start changes nothing). Two lessons: the rig's reload was only tested by its effect (the mutation that kept the old frame count survived: N2.88 now asserts that the machine's clock lag is kept), and the harness had a trap (GNU make 3.81 compares whole seconds: a source restored in the second that its object was written in kept the mutated object and named wrong tests), so every restore now waits and the whole battery was run again.
- **Gates** (on the release tree, v0.0.98 on v0.0.97): the build with `-DANTS_WERROR=ON` has **zero warnings** (a clean Release build in a fresh directory: 118 translation units); the full `./run_tests.sh` (47 suites and the 506 end-to-end tests) **passes, 353 s** (test_lockstep 104 tests / 1,778,288 assertions, test_lobby 20 / 64,468, test_netgame 19 / 669, test_server 60 / 13,187, test_jitter 27 / 1,454, test_latency 36 / 107,437, test_tcp 8 / 72,267, test_lan 13 / 5,005, test_ctl 39 / 1,441,308, test_ai 74 / 320,131, test_app_integration 219 / 8,162, test_network_app 51 / 1,081, test_start_menu_app 34 / 787, the server end-to-end script 138 checks, the opaque-box tests 506 / 506); the network suites and the application's network suites under AddressSanitizer + UBSan (lockstep, lobby, netgame, jitter, latency, tcp, ws, lan, server, network_app, start_menu_app): **no report**, the same totals; GCC 12.2 (Debian 12), the whole project with `-DANTS_WERROR=ON -DANTS_BUILD_APP=ON`: **0 diagnostics** and the same suites pass (test_server 13,182 and test_ws 1,171,202 assertions there: root-only and timing-dependent counts); `docker build -t ants-beta .` and `docker build -f Dockerfile.server -t ants-server .` build with no compiler warnings and the changelog page renders. **Real checks with the two images on a private docker network** (the web page, the server with demo rooms and no `--reconnect`: how the owner's stack runs it; details in `docs/NETWORK_PORT.md`): **the default behaves as v0.0.97**: a web page seat (headless Chrome, a throwaway profile) and a native client played a demo room at **19.99 ticks a second**, two native clients at 19.97, a hidden tab for 45 s at 20.00 with equal state hashes and no Lag notice, and a native seat that was cut was **dropped at once** (the match ended 0.11 - 0.12 s later, nothing paused, nothing held); **a room with reconnect on** (control interface; two raw clients that vote and a native client behind the cut proxy): the cut paused the room at once and the status named the seat, nothing ran while it waited (143 -> 144 ticks in 3 s), the vote opened **5.1 s** after the cut, one vote of two was not enough, the second dropped the seat, the status counted the **resume countdown 10 ... 1** (Presence carried `resume_s` to the raw clients), the match went on at 19.98 ticks a second, and with a cap of 60 s the cap dropped the seat **60.1 s** after the cut, followed by the countdown; no key in the status or the container's log; **the leader's START** (a room for four, with and without reconnect) and **the start menu's host, join and START** against the image (`test_start_menu_app --real-server`) work; a Hello of protocol 9 is answered "version mismatch"; a leak scan of `git diff origin/main` and of the messages (rule 10) finds nothing.


**Part 2: the sessions and the server.**

- **`HostSession` holds seats** (`Config::hold_seats`, off by default). A seat's connection is **lost** when its link closes, a send to it fails, or **nothing at all arrives from it for 10 s** (`silence_ms`: a power cut or a frozen machine leaves a half-open link that only the silence shows). A room that holds seats, has a usable log and knows the seat's key then **keeps the seat and pauses the match for everybody**: nothing is sealed (the sealing schedule slides, so no burst of turns follows), the others are told who is missing and for how long by a **Presence** message made for each of them (their own vote in it; on every change and once a second while paused), chat is relayed, **commands are discarded without a violation** (a pause is long: a player who clicks on would be thrown out by eight violations), a bot's orders wait, and the lag policy is suspended (nobody can fall behind in a pause). Every sealed turn is appended to the `TurnLog` **before** it is sent. Anything else (a room that does not hold seats, a seat without a key, a log that is full) drops a lost seat at once, as before there was a way back. **A player that talks but runs behind is lag, never a pause**: v0.0.94's rules are unchanged (told to the others from 3 s, catches up at up to 4x, dropped for good at 60 s behind or after 30 s without an acknowledgement). A bot's seat is never absent, never votes and is not counted as connected.
- **The way back** (`HostSession::accept_rejoin`): the key decides the seat (all four are compared in constant time; a key that fits no seat is answered like a Hello without one, "the match has started", so a wrong key reveals nothing; a seat that was dropped is told `Dropped`; a log that is not usable, or a machine with nothing and no Start to tell it how to load the match, is `RejoinFailed`; more turns than were ever sealed is `BadRequest`). A seat whose old link is still open (a Wi-Fi switch, a second window) is **taken over: the old link is told `Superseded` and closed**, and so is an earlier attempt of the same seat. The newcomer gets `Welcome` (rejoin flag, its key) and `CatchUp{turns it has, total}`; a machine with nothing (a reloaded page) gets the room's `Start`, answers `Loaded`, gets `Begin` and the whole stream. The log goes out in batches of at most 4096 turns and 48 KB **in a window of 256 KB counted in bytes** (a WebSocket fails a connection at 1 MB of backlog; a hostile log of the biggest turns, 5.4 MB in 503 turns, was streamed with at most 305 KB in flight), paced by what the machine has executed; a returning connection is held to the rules of a client (flood budget, eight violations; what it sends before the host has compared its state is no offence). It ends with `CaughtUp{turns, hash}`: **the referee compares the hash with its own state** (after running the turns that its own runner still holds back: a seat lost in the first 50 ms could never be given back otherwise); equal: the seat is Present again, everybody is told, and when it was the last one the match runs; different: a `Desync` to that machine alone, the room does not fail and the seat stays away.
- **The vote and the cap** (`Attendance` of part 1, driven by the session): after 30 s away in all the others get the vote in their Presence and send `Vote`; more than half of the connected players drops the seat (its `Drop` is sealed in the first turn after the pause, the same tick everywhere; with two players the survivor wins); there is no automatic drop for a seat's own time, but the match's total pause is capped (30 minutes by default) and at the cap every absent seat was dropped (the review fixes make the cap absolute: every seat that is not present). A seat that was dropped keeps its key: whoever shows it is told `Dropped`. A `Vote` to a host that does not hold seats is a violation, like any message that a host does not receive.
- **`ClientSession` comes back** (`Config::reconnect`, off by default; it needs the key): a closed link, or a host that is silent for 10 s, is no longer the end but **`Reconnecting`** (`wants_connection` asks the owner for a new link at once and every 2 s, `attach` hands it over), then **`Rejoining`** (the Hello with the key and the turns it has goes out when the link is OPEN, not before: a TCP link is still connecting when it is made; no Welcome in 10 s abandons the attempt), then **`CatchingUp`** (the stream is run at once without drawing, acknowledged at every higher percent, then `CaughtUp`, and the machine waits for the server's word before it plays). Any `Reject` ends the session at once with its reason; it gives up 31 minutes after the first loss. `submit` is refused while a seat is missing; `vote` and `chat` need `Normal`. **A silent server is silent in real time, and only when it was asked** (`ClientSession::note_gap`, the rule of the work for hidden browser tabs, which this builds on: a page that the browser does not wake gives the session at most a second per wake-up and hands the rest of the gap over afterwards; only a host that has been asked by a ping and has not answered is held to all of it, one that was not asked yet gets the gap only up to 3 s short of the 10 s, so that a live host that holds its turns is never condemned). The same rule serves Normal and CatchingUp, and a Hello that waits for its Welcome, the attempts and the 31-minute give-up count in real time too.
- **The server** (`Room`, `RoomManager`, the control interface, `ants_server`): `RoomSpec::reconnect` (off by default in this release), `vote_after_ms`, `max_pause_ms`, `max_log_bytes`; a room that holds seats gives each player a key from the operating system's generator; **the door** takes a Hello with a key to the running room (anything else is `MatchRunning` as before, a finished room `NoSuchRoom`); a held seat counts as somebody being there (a room whose players all lost their connection waits, then ends "everybody left" at the cap); the wall-clock limit counts the time that the match ran, not the time that it waited; at the end of a match its log is freed and what it held is kept for the status. **A budget for the logs of all the rooms together** (256 MiB, `LogBudget`): an append that it refuses makes that log unusable, and the room falls back to dropping a lost seat at once. **A use-after-free of the design is fixed**: the room owns every connection and the session points at them, so `prune_connections` asks the session before it frees a closed one (found with the prototype under AddressSanitizer; `test_server` S3.42 runs in the sanitizer suite and fails without the fix, also in an ordinary build).
- **The status and the control interface**: `GET /rooms/<code>` (and the list, and the result file) gains `reconnect`, `hold_vote_seconds`, `max_pause_seconds`, `paused`, `absent` (`[{seat, name, state: absent | catching_up, away_seconds, progress}]`), `vote` (`{seat, continue, voters}` or null), `paused_seconds`, `rejoins`, `drops_by_vote`, `drops_by_cap` and `log` (`{turns, bytes, usable}`); `GET /stats` gains `log_bytes` and `log_budget_bytes`; `POST /rooms` takes `reconnect`, `hold_vote_seconds` (5 - 3600, default 30) and `max_pause_seconds` (60 - 86,400, default 1800). **A key is in none of it**, nor in a result file or a log line (S3.46 searches everything for the hex of every key). Options of `ants_server`: `--reconnect` / `--no-reconnect`, `--hold-vote-seconds N`, `--max-pause-seconds N`, `--log-mb N` (1 - 256), each with its bounds (status 2 outside them).
- **Limits that are documented, not hidden**: the key of a seat travels in clear on the native TCP door, as the room's token does (on the WebSocket door it is inside TLS); a returning key holder has budgets (the review fixes above: 5 minutes of catch-up per absence, three Hellos a minute, three times the log's size in ten minutes), and every connection has the flood budget and the violations.
- **A change in the game**: one line on stderr when the host's link is lost (the end-to-end script reads it); nothing else of the application, the simulation, the lobby's screens or the golden hashes changed.
- **New tests** (all of them fail without the code they test): `test_lockstep` **N2.47 - N2.74** (28 tests) and `test_server` **S3.36 - S3.51 and S3.59** (17 tests), and `tests/scripts/test_ants_server.sh` (the options and their bounds; a real server with `--reconnect`, a client behind `tests/scripts/flaky_proxy.py` whose link is cut, a client that is stopped with `kill -STOP`): N2.47 a lost link pauses the match within one pass, nothing is sealed for 20 s, the others are told, the held seat gates nothing, the machine comes back in memory and the match resumes at the right schedule, three machines end identical; N2.48 a machine from nothing is given more than 300 turns (and a catch-up that lasts past the vote's 30 s is never put to the vote or dropped while it progresses); N2.49 the vote (not before 30 s, one of two is not enough, a changed choice counts, the `Drop` at the same tick everywhere, the key told "dropped"); N2.50 the cap; N2.51 two seats gone; N2.52 a wrong key, no key, a made-up count of turns, a second window that takes a live seat over (`Superseded`), a session with no Start message; N2.53 a different state after the catch-up (a `Desync` to that machine alone, the room does not fail); N2.54 a full log falls back to dropping at once (and refuses a second window with a live seat's key), the log is appended before the send, a seat without a key cannot be held; N2.55 400 commands in a pause are no offence; N2.56 `Leave` is final; N2.57 absences add up; N2.58 two reloads at once; N2.59 a stalled catch-up is let go; N2.60 Presence for each recipient; N2.61 chat in a pause; N2.62 a bot's seat; N2.63 the hostile log; N2.64 a `Reject` ends the session in every mode; N2.65 the way back (the Hello when the link opens, the attempts, the give-up, leaving, finishing); N2.66 the catch-up through a scripted server and every protocol failure; N2.67 lag is not a loss; N2.68 silence is a loss after 10 s; N2.69 a host that holds no seats is exactly what it was; N2.70 the referee's runner; N2.71 a returning connection is held to the rules of a client and the stream is paced in bytes; N2.72 the same timeline from five clocks, across the wrap; N2.73 the log's budget; N2.74 a silent server in real time. `test_server`: S3.59 keys, S3.36 pause and return and a whole TINY match to its end identical on three machines, S3.37 a reload of more than 700 turns, S3.38 the vote ends a two-player match, S3.39 the limit counts play, S3.40 everybody lost, S3.41 the cap through the door, S3.42 the prune window, S3.43 connections that are cut 40 times (three players take turns), S3.44 the door and a key, S3.45 a reload in the waiting room, S3.46 the status and no key anywhere, S3.47 the settings and their bounds, S3.48 the budget, S3.49 a slow machine is lag, S3.50 a room that does not hold seats is what it was, S3.51 silence. After the change: `test_lockstep` 84 tests, 1,742,630 assertions; `test_lobby` 20 tests, 64,460; `test_netgame` 19 tests, 669; `test_server` 51 tests, 11,980; `test_jitter` 18 tests, 288; the server end-to-end script 125 checks (43 new); `./run_tests.sh --clean` (a clean build with `-DANTS_WERROR=ON`, zero warnings, and every suite, the 506 end-to-end tests included) passes in 325 s
- **Mutations** (a scratch copy of the sources, one change at a time, each run against the tests that must notice it): **115 mutations** of the code (HostSession's hold, pause and Presence, the way back and the stream, ClientSession's way back and catch-up, the real-time silence rule, the room, the door, the control interface, the log's budget), each run against the tests that must notice it: **114 are killed by a test and one is equivalent** (a Hello without a key that is handed to the session instead of being answered at the door gets the same answer, "the match has started"). Where a first run left a mutation alive, a test was made stricter (the unusable log in `accept_rejoin`, a session without a Start message, the control interface's own bound messages, the catching-up state in the status JSON, a finished room's log given back, a live turn or a Presence that comes before `CaughtUp`, the Hello that counts received turns, a Vote to a host that holds no seats, a session that is finished while it is on its way back)
- **Rewritten tests: none** in part 2 itself (it only added tests and rig code; the scenarios of v0.0.94 that pin "dropped at once" and the lag drops pass unchanged, and S3.50 runs them again on rooms that say `reconnect: false` out loud). The review fixes above rewrote the tests that pinned what the review showed to be wrong.
- **Measured** (native, Release build, Apple silicon, a machine that was shared with other builds): the catch-up of a machine that starts from nothing (the real host, lobby and client sessions over a link of 1 ms, four players giving about 10 orders a second: busy play): **5 minutes of play, 6,001 turns, a log of 103,078 bytes: 1.3 - 1.4 s; 15 minutes, 18,001 turns, 309,566 bytes: 3.7 - 3.9 s; 30 minutes, 36,001 turns, 618,906 bytes: 7.3 - 7.5 s** (two runs; the host's share, the stream and the comparison together are under 1 ms: the cost is the simulation itself, about 4,800 ticks a second); a link cut and a machine that comes back in memory: the host pauses 20 ms after the cut, the others know 60 ms after it, the match resumes 140 ms after the new link was made (links of 30 - 40 ms one way: about 0.3 s from the cut to the first new turn); a page that starts from nothing was given 801 turns (a log of 11,402 bytes) and was back 290 ms after it opened; a hostile log of 5,434,302 bytes in 503 turns was streamed with at most 305,026 bytes in flight over a link that fails at 1 MB; the real server (`tests/scripts/test_ants_server.sh`, the local build): the room paused 0.1 s after the link was cut, nothing advanced while it waited, the cap dropped the seat 60.0 s after the cut, and a client stopped with `kill -STOP` paused the room 10.1 s later; the real server image: a room made with `reconnect: true` through the control interface of the `ants-server` image (a cap of 60 s), two headless native clients, one of them behind `tests/scripts/flaky_proxy.py`: the cut link paused the room **0.1 s later** and the status named the seat (`absent`, 0 s away); six seconds later the match stood at the same tick (70 and 70); at 31 s away the status showed the vote (`{seat 1, continue 0, voters 1}`); **the cap dropped the seat 60.3 s after the cut** (`paused_seconds` 60, `drops_by_cap` 1, `drops_by_vote` 0), the match ended with the survivor the winner, and neither the status nor the container's log held a key
- Documents: `docs/NETWORK_PORT.md` (the whole "Reconnect (protocol 10)" section: the behaviour, the messages, the sessions, the server, the flows, the limits, what release B will do, what was measured), the README (the server's options, the room's keys, the status JSON, the suite rows), `docs/BOTS.md` (a bot's seat is never absent and never votes), the comments of `session.hpp`, `protocol.hpp`, `room.hpp`, `room_manager.hpp`, `control.hpp` and `turnlog.hpp`, the usage text of `ants_server`, and `docker-compose.server.yml`.

**Part 1: the building blocks** (nothing in a running game or server used them until part 2):

- **Network protocol 10** (`protocol.hpp`; 9 is the community-map rules, which changed no message, so a Hello of 6 - 9 is told "version mismatch" without its layout being read). **Hello** carries a 16-byte seat key and the number of turns the machine has (`have_turns`, 0 without a key), **Welcome** carries the key and flags (bit 0: a rejoin was accepted), **Reject** gains `Dropped` (the key is right and the seat was dropped: the original's string 94), `RejoinFailed` and `Superseded`, and five new messages: **Presence** (26: who is missing and for how long, the vote, the receiver's own choice, the seconds that the match's pause cap has left), **Vote** (27), **CatchUp** (28), **TurnBatch** (29: up to 4096 turns and 48 KB of the server's log as one message) and **CaughtUp** (30: the turns executed and the state hash). Every decoder checks every length, count and range and refuses a trailing byte; Presence also refuses what an honest server could not say (a seat twice, entries not longest-away first, a vote about a seat that is not the first absent one, votes above the voters, a progress for an absent seat). `key_matches` compares keys in constant time and never calls "no key" equal to anything; `encode_turn_batch_packed` makes a TurnBatch from turns that are packed already.
- **`TurnLog`** (`turnlog.hpp`): the server's packed log of every sealed turn (36,000 turns = 30 minutes with turns of 50 ms; busy play is 651,372 bytes, an order-free match 216,000), cut into batches with `read` and measured with `bytes_between`. A hostile client could make it 1.25 GB in 30 minutes (64 commands of 32 ants per player and turn), so it has a limit (16 MiB per room, never more than `0xFFFFFFF0` bytes): the append that would pass it stores nothing and the log is unusable from then on, as it is after a turn out of order or a turn too big for a message.
- **`Attendance`** (`attendance.hpp`): who is at the table (Present / Absent / CatchingUp / Dropped), pure logic like the sequencer. A seat's away time is its total over the match, each loss counting at least 5 s toward the vote; the vote about the seat away longest (never one that is catching up) opens at 30 s and is won by **more than half of the connected players**; a voter that is lost leaves the count and its choice is gone; a catch-up with no progress for 20 s is let go; **there is no automatic drop of a seat for its own time**: the match's total paused time is capped (30 minutes by default) and at the cap every absent seat is dropped (as first built, a seat that was catching up kept its chance while it progressed: the review fixes made the cap absolute); `cap_s` tells the screens how many seconds are left. Times are 32-bit differences (the tests run the same scenario from eight clocks, among them 8 s before the wrap).
- **`LockstepRunner::fast_forward`** (with `on_catch_up_turn`, `at_boundary`): runs queued turns at once, with no hook and in exactly the order of `update()`, the state after every turn the same as the paced runner's. **The catch-up does not feed the adaptive jitter buffer** (its turns all arrive "late"): after it the runner is as at the start of a match (`JitterBuffer::reset()`, the next live turns collected before the first runs), so a rough link before the pause does not make the buffer grow after it.
- **Keys in the lobby** (`lobby.hpp`): `HostLobby::Config::make_key` (unset = no keys, as a LAN host) gives each guest a key in its Welcome (`key_of(seat)`; a zero or shared key, or a failing maker, gives none); a Hello with the key of a seated guest while the room is open **takes the seat over** (same seat, key, name and place in the order of the Welcomes, so the leader stays the leader; the old connection is told `Superseded` and closed; the thumb is measured again); in a match that loads or runs a key is still `MatchRunning` here (the session answers it in the next part). `ClientLobby::Config::key` makes the Hello show a key; `key()` and `rejoined()` read the Welcome. `ants::server::random_bytes` (the generator of the control secret) is public for the server to make the keys with (`ants_net` never reads the operating system's generator: it is built for the web too).
- **Texts**: `RejectReason::Dropped` shows the original's string 94 ("Sorry, you have been dropped from the game."), `RejoinFailed` "The game could not be rejoined.", `Superseded` "This game was taken over by another window.".
- **New tests** (`test_lockstep`: N2.40 protocol 10 byte by byte with every truncation and range rule, N2.41 the log with 36,000 real-looking turns and the limit to the byte, N2.42 - N2.44 the attendance rules, the cap, the stall, the wrap and Presence against the decoder, N2.45 fast_forward against the paced runner over 600 turns, N2.46 the jitter buffer and the catch-up; `test_jitter` J1.11; `test_lobby` N4.14 keys, N4.15 the takeover, N4.16 the rejoin flow through `ClientLobby`; `test_netgame` N3.17 the new rejection texts; `test_server` S3.58 `random_bytes`). Every one of them fails without the code it tests (mutations listed in the commit message).
- Rewritten tests (none removed, disabled or weakened): `test_lockstep` **N2.1** (the version is 10, `MsgType::Last` is `CaughtUp`, the Hello and the Welcome with a key join the list of messages that are refused one byte short or long), **N2.2** (the reject reasons run 1 - 9: 10 is the first refused, 7 was; the unknown types start at 31, `Last` is 30), **N2.3** (a plausible type byte runs to 30; the fuzz seeds and decoders include the new messages), `test_lobby` **N4.2c** (it built the Hello of protocol 6 as "this very layout" with `encode`: it is raw bytes now, for 6, 7, 8 and 9, answered "version mismatch" by a LAN host and by a server), `test_server` **S3.3** (a loop of raw old-layout Hellos at the door), and `tests/scripts/test_ants_server.sh` (the raw flood client's Hello gains the key and the turns). No LAN test changed (the LAN datagram carries `kProtocolVersion` and passes as it was).
- Documents: `docs/NETWORK_PORT.md` ("Reconnect (protocol 10)": the decided behaviour, the messages with their layouts and rules, the keys, the log, the attendance rules, the catch-up, the limits; the milestone), `docs/GAME_REVERSE_ENGINEERING.md` (**5.47 corrected**: the departure reasons are 0 = quit or loss, 1 = the map file is missing, 2 = the map has the wrong version; strings 0x70 - 0x73 are the missing-map and wrong-version texts, and `0x1d0` is the size of the dialog object that `FUN_01029411` allocates, not a string id; 5.46 gains the pulse and the 60 s drop-out (`timeGetTime`, the setter, the pulse task, the removal and its callback); 5.48 says what sets the "dropped" state that shows string 94; every address was re-read with Capstone), the comments of `protocol.hpp`, `lobby.hpp`, `lockstep.hpp`, `jitter.hpp`, `turnlog.hpp` and `attendance.hpp`.

## v0.0.97 - 2026-10-02 - The desktop start menu: Single player with bots, Join with a code, Host an online match

The owner asked: "it would be nice if when you open the game (the built game, not the web game) it asks if you want to play single player or enter a code to join a lobby", "for single player mode, if you pick single player, it gives you the ability to add bots" and "even the binary game should be rejoinable as well". A native game that is started with **no mode on its command line** now opens with a **start menu** after the loading screen and before the quick help: **Single player** (a computer player of a level at each of the other three seats), **Join with a code** (a room of the game server), **Host an online match** (a room that you make on the server and share by its code), **Quit**. The original's own screens (loading, quick help, setup, match, results) are unchanged (the M0 view fingerprints of the classic picture pass untouched); no simulation rule, wire format or golden hash changed. The menu never shows in the web build.

- **The look** (`include/ants_app/start_menu.hpp`, `src/ants_app/start_menu.cpp`, `start_menu_view.cpp`): the background of the original's Single / Multi screen (`sm_screen`, 640 x 480: the orange tiles and the green frame), banners, buttons and boxes built from the colours of the original's button and box pictures (every button picture of `ants.chd` carries its label, so a button with its own words is made from the same palette: outline, face, light and dark edges, the red drop shadow; the dark boxes with their green frame are the setup screen's), the `agst301` ants of the setup screen in the seats' colours, and the game's TrueType face. Hover and keyboard selection light a button; the version and the frame rate stand in the corner as on every screen. It is a **pure model** (state, rectangles, texts, keys, mouse, the request it makes) with a thin drawing function over its elements, like the HUD layout code: `Up` / `Down` / `Tab` / `Shift+Tab`, `Left` / `Right`, `Enter`, `Space`, `Esc` and the mouse (the original's button class: the click sound at the press, the action at the release, leaving the button cancels).
- **Single player**: a row for each seat that is not yours (Red, Blue and Black for green), each **Empty / Easy bot / Medium bot / Hard bot**; Continue leads to the original's quick help and setup screen. All Empty is the original's single-player game exactly (four teams, no bot code: the state hash at tick 100 equals that of the game that never saw the menu); with a bot the match starts with exactly the bots `--bot SEAT:LEVEL` gives (same code path, `Application::menu_start_single` fills `config_.bots`; a test plays both ways and compares the roster, the names, the bot seats, the decisions and the state hash). Rule 8: the panel says "Bots play without fog of war. Empty seats have no ants." (the second sentence is what `--bot` has always done: a seat that is not taken has no hill), and with nobody seated it says what that means too, **"No bots: the original single-player game (the other colours stand still)."** (the other three colours keep their hills and six ants each: the original's roster `0xF`; the review found the two states told differently and one of them not at all); the setup screen's fog buttons are the original's and unchanged: with a bot seated START with fog on is refused with today's reason, with fog off it starts. The choice is remembered (`bots=off,easy,off,hard`).
- **Join with a code**: **Your name** (remembered; "Player" at first; 32 printable characters; a name that starts with "Bot (" in any blanks and case is refused on the panel with the reason: the server renames such a player to "Player N", and a test sends a battery of 19 names to a real server and demands that the menu refuses exactly those that come back renamed) and **Room code** (32 letters, digits, `-`, `_`; `Ctrl+V` / `Cmd+V` pastes through `SDL_GetClipboardText`; the ends are trimmed; **the case is never changed**: the server's rooms are case sensitive and only a lower-case `demo-...` code makes a demo room on demand). **Join** connects to the server like `--join HOST:PORT --room CODE --name NAME` (`NetGame::join`) and ends in the guest's screen, or in the leader's host screen with START when you are first. While it connects the panel says "Connecting to <server>..." and Esc / Cancel ends it at once (nothing of the attempt stays: the lookup is abandoned, the connection closed).
- **Host an online match**: the map (the six of the original, in the order of `web/four.html`), 2 - 4 players, your name, **Host**. The menu makes the code the way the page does (`make_room_code`: `demo-<map>-<n>p-<six characters of the page's alphabet>`, the alphabet is pinned against the page by a test that reads `web/four.html`), joins it as the first player (the leader) and shows **"Your room:" and the code in large letters**, the players in the room, **Copy** (`SDL_SetClipboardText`; `Ctrl+C` / `Cmd+C` too; the call is a hook so a test can check it) and **Continue to the room** (the leader's screen with START; a room that fills while the code is shown starts by itself). The window's title is **"Ants - room <code>"** from the moment you are in a room until you are back at the menu (a `--title` is kept in front). If the server refuses ("The server could not make a room now: it is busy, or it does not host online matches. Try again in a few minutes.") or the code was taken (you would have joined somebody else's room: you are put out again with a line), the Host panel says so.
- **Every failure comes back to the panel with a clear line** (never a crash, a hang or a silent return): the server cannot be reached (named), the name is not known, no room with that code (named; "capital letters matter"), the room is full, the match has already started, **another version ("This game is v0.0.N, but the server runs another version of the game")**, removed from the room, the request was not accepted, the connection was lost before the room, the server is busy (hosting), and a server that does not answer (**one line, "The server <name> did not answer. Check the server's address and your connection.", for a name that is never resolved or a connection that is never made after the attempt's 20 s, and for a server that accepts the connection and never sends its Welcome after the room's 10 s: the review found the second ended as "lost before you were in the room" while the README said "does not answer"; `NetGame::FailReason::NoAnswer` and `ClientLobby::welcome_timed_out()` tell them apart, and both limits are pinned by tests that run the production values**). Every `RejectReason` of the server has its own line (a raw test server answers each of them). To tell "cannot reach" from "lost before the room" `NetGame` has `fail_reason()` and `reject_reason()` (and `ClientLobby::was_open()`): a failed join used to keep only a text. The one step of joining that blocks, the lookup of the server's name, runs on a worker thread (`include/ants_app/host_lookup.hpp`; an address is answered at once, IPv4 is preferred when a name has both): the window stays alive and Esc cancels during it. A last-seat join, where Welcome, Room and Start arrive together, is taken for what it is (the first draft of the menu took the match that was loading for a lost connection; a test caught it).
- **After a match** (what the original does: Leave on the results screen and on the setup screen ends the program, docs 5.63): a single-player match keeps that; **a network game that the menu led to goes back to the menu** when its results are left, when the player leaves from the room's screen or the quit dialog, and when the connection is lost or the server closes the room (the notice is on the first panel; a room that dies under the setup screen no longer leaves a dead screen). The menu works again afterwards: another room, a single-player game with the local name back. A game started with a mode has no menu and ends as it always did.
- **The review's fixes**, each pinned by a test that fails without it (the mutations are in the commit message):
  - **The rest of a click that changed the screen is not a click on the screen it opened** (the lesson of v0.0.93's quick help, applied to the menu): the controls lie on top of each other, so the second click of a double click on the loading screen reached Quit (the program ended), on "Host an online match" the Host button (a room was made), on "Single player" the Red seat's row (Red became an Easy bot and was saved), on Cancel the Join panel's Back (or the Host panel's Host: a new attempt), and the quit dialog's Yes the "Host an online match" entry. After a left press or release changed the panel or the screen, a left press that SDL counts as part of the sequence (`clicks` above 1) or that comes within the double-click time only moves the hover (`Application::swallow_menu_gesture`, the quick help's `closing_click_pending()` rule for the menu: on the first panel and every other, the quick help and the setup screen after Continue, the loading screen); a click after that is a click; a release always belongs to a press that began on the panel that is up (`go()` lets go of what was held).
