  - **`Enter` pressed twice acts once**: the first Enter that opened a panel was followed by a second that pressed that panel's preselected button (Continue started the game, Host made a room, Cancel gave up the attempt without a word). A panel now preselects its **first input** (the first seat row, the map, the first empty field of Join) and **nothing on "Connecting"**, and Enter / Space do nothing for 300 ms after a panel appears (`StartMenu::kSettleMs`, the menu's own clock; Esc does not quit from the first panel then); a held key never acted and still does not.
  - **A code of an earlier room is selected, not appended to**: after Back or a finished game the Join panel kept the old code focused but not selected, so typing gave "room-oneroom-two"; arrival and Tab agree now (the text is selected, typing replaces it).
  - **A character that cannot be typed is said**: "José 李" gave "Jos" and no word; the field now shows "Only letters A-Z, digits and simple punctuation." (typed or pasted; a line end of a paste is still dropped silently); an input method's composition (`SDL_TEXTEDITING`) is ignored on purpose, its committed text arrives as `SDL_TEXTINPUT`.
  - **The copy failure line is not cut** ("Copy failed. Write down the code above."; the code stands in full in its own box), the setup screen's banners of `start_game.sh` / `.bat` name the keys that screen has (Up / Down, Enter or S, Q or X; the fog is a pair of buttons; Esc does nothing) and no more, and the code-alphabet test reads `web/four.html` by the source folder and **fails** when the page cannot be read (it skipped).
  - **A server that does not offer the chosen map**: a demo room of a server whose `--demo-maps` lacks the map is made on its default map; the menu compares the room's map with the one it asked for and says "This server does not offer the Islands map (its room is on TINY.LVL). Choose another map." and leaves the room (it had been silently on another map).
  - **Smaller things**: a lookup whose thread cannot be started says "Could not start the lookup" (it threw out of the menu; `HostLookup::start_failed()`, a launcher hook for the test); the guest's `NetGame` no longer sets a LAN discovery and a game version that only a host's announcement uses, and `return_to_start_menu` lost three resets that nothing read (the bots and the roster of a game that never had them); the name is no longer saved at every key (above) and `ConfigStore::save` is atomic.
  - Gaps that the mutation testing of the review found are closed by tests (they were unreachable by any other check): Leave of a menu-led single-player game ends the program (the original's way; pinned), the quit dialog, the match's quick help and options and the seat of the match do not stay after a network game, the attempt that a hosted room left at "InRoom" is gone, the setup screen's names of bot seats before START, a session that is over (`Failed` and `Over`: the test puts the session there with `NetGame::force_phase_for_test`, because only a lost match ends `Over`) under the setup screen, on the room's panel and in a join, "Continue to the room" needs the room, the error line is not carried to the first panel, a bot stored for the player's own seat is no bot, the guest asks for any seat (255), `poll()` of the lookup takes its lock (a stress test for ThreadSanitizer).
- **Which command lines show the menu**: a plain `ants`, `--name`, `--settings`, `--seed`, `--fullscreen`, `--frames`, `--show-grid`, `--team-name` / `-N`, `--title`, the window options, `--audio-focus`, `--port`, `--loopback`, `--lan-port`, `--no-lan`, `--start-when`, `--server` and unknown options. **These skip it** (each alone is tested): `--map`, `--map-select`, `--host`, `--join`, `--join-url`, `--room`, `--token`, `--seat`, `--bot`, `--headless`, `--screenshot`, `--player` / `-pnum:` / `-pnum=`, `--select-ant`, `--select-base`, `--open-options`, `--scorecard`. **`--start-menu` forces it** (also headless, with `--screenshot`; a `--bot` then sets the rows; refused with `--map`, `--open-options`, `--scorecard`, `--host`, `--join`, `--join-url`). **`--server HOST[:PORT]`** names the game server (default `beta.playants.org:4001`; a bad one stops the start with the reason). A config made by hand (the tests') has no menu: today's game exactly. `start_game.sh` / `.bat`: the four windows each have `--host` or `--join` and never show it; `--single` is a plain game and shows it (`--map-select` brings back the setup screen at once); the scripts' comments and banners say so and the script test checks both.
- **Settings** (remake keys, lower case; the original's nine keep their names): `name`, `bots`, `host_map`, `host_players` are written when they change (one write each, only that value; **the name when the field is left, a panel changes, Join or Host is pressed or the program ends, not at every key**), and the file is written all at once (beside it, then a replace: `ConfigStore::save`), `server` is read and never written by the menu; `--server` beats it, `--name` beats `name` for the run; invalid values are the defaults (a bad `server` is the default server with a line on stderr). Two Application instances on one file in a temp folder prove the restart.
- **Found in the original** (docs 5.63, read from the decompilation and Capstone): the original has **no built-in single-player seat** (the local team is `-pnum:<n>`, else the first team whose `-N<d><name>` matches the local slot's name; team indexes 0 - 3 = green, red, blue, black are the only seat numbers, and the remake's `--bot SEAT` and the menu's rows use them), its Single / Multi screen (`sm_screen`, `single1 - 3`, `multi1 - 3`) is shown unless `-spike` and its Multi button opens a web address and ends the program (the remake's menu only borrows the background), and the program ends after every match (Leave = `PostQuitMessage`).
- **Windows: seven test programs failed to link (SDL_main)** (found by the owner's MSVC build of v0.0.96): SDL's headers do `#define main SDL_main`, and the Windows build links `SDL2main.lib`, whose `main` calls `SDL_main(int, char**)`. `test_input_model`, `test_hud_layout`, `test_network_app`, `test_pointer_model`, `test_render_parity`, `test_status_messages` and `test_options` defined `int main()` and gave "LNK2019: unresolved external symbol SDL_main" (they compile and run on macOS and Linux, which is why no gate saw it); the menu's own `test_start_menu` had the same form. All eight now define `int main(int argc, char* argv[])` with `(void)argc; (void)argv;` (no test case changed; `test_app_integration`, `test_view_fingerprint` and `test_start_menu_app` had the right form). **A guard that fails on every platform, at every configure** (`CMakeLists.txt`, in front of the test targets): no program under `tests/test_app/` (all of them link `ants_app`, hence SDL), and no other test or tool that includes SDL or an `ants_app` header, may define `int main()` or `int main(void)`; the error names each file and line and says what to write. Shown on the sources of v0.0.96 (plus the menu): the configure stopped with all eight named, `test_hud_layout.cpp:3104`, `test_input_model.cpp:313`, `test_network_app.cpp:2965`, `test_options.cpp:473`, `test_pointer_model.cpp:2290`, `test_render_parity.cpp:1752`, `test_start_menu.cpp:302` and `test_status_messages.cpp:912`; with the fix it configures (the programs of `tests/test_net`, `test_ai`, `test_sim`, `test_assets` and the others that do not include SDL keep `int main()`).
- Tests: **new suites 3.11 `test_start_menu`** (the model: 30 tests, 5,159 assertions) and **3.12 `test_start_menu_app`** (34 tests, 763 assertions, against a real `RoomManager` over TCP: single player with nobody and with two bots equal to the game without the menu and to `--bot`, joins, hosts, the leader's START, every failure and every Reject reason, cancels, the way back after a match, the window's title, the clipboard (injected and SDL's own), the sounds, the window's own event loop, the names the server renames), **18 new fingerprints** `screen.menu.*` in suite 3.10 (the 307 classic ones are untouched), and 14 new checks in the script suite 3.8 (112 in all: each of the rig's four windows carries a mode flag; `--single` is the bare program, `--single --map-select` is the setup screen; the setup screen's banner of both scripts names the keys that screen takes), 4 in suite 3.7 (139: the settings file is replaced all at once and leaves nothing beside it). `test_start_menu_app --shots DIR` writes the screenshots of every panel; `--real-server H:P` and `--probe H:P` are the gate's modes against a running server. Each test fails without the code it tests (the mutations are listed in the commit message).
- Rewritten tests: none of an earlier release but the two version assertions of 12.108 (v0.0.97) and the signature of `main` in eight test programs (the bullet above); no case removed or weakened (the menu's own tests changed where its behaviour did: the first input of a panel is not a button, Enter needs a settled panel, the name is written when the field is left, the copy failure and the texts; six of the 18 `screen.menu.*` fingerprints were pinned again for the new selections, lines and hints; the other 307 are untouched).
- Documents: README (the Start Menu section, the options table, the settings keys, the quick launch table, the network and bot paragraphs, the suite rows), `docs/BOTS.md` (bots from the single-player panel), `docs/NETWORK_PORT.md` (joining and hosting from the menu, the new accessors), `docs/BUILD_AND_RUN.md` (what a plain start shows), `docs/GAME_REVERSE_ENGINEERING.md` 5.63, `STATUS.md`, the comments and banners of `start_game.sh` / `.bat`.
## Unreleased - The web game is 16:9

The browser build's page and the game's picture in it: the web game is now a 16:9 picture (the game's fixed canvas of 960 x 540) in a game box that is 16:9 and as large as fits the window, with the original's 4:3 picture one click away. The simulation, the network protocol and every state hash are untouched. (The wide match screen itself is the widescreen milestone M3; this release is the page and the browser build's default: until M3 the web game showed the classic picture in a 4:3 box.)

- **The picture is 16:9 by default.** The web build now takes `--aspect` and the settings key `aspect` like the desktop game (they were switched off in the browser) and its default is 16:9 (`kWebDefaultAspect` in `canvas_layout.hpp`). The page passes the shape it shows as `--aspect 16:9` or `--aspect 4:3`. **Classic 4:3** (the original's 640 x 480 picture) is chosen by `?aspect=4:3` on the address or by the selector "16:9 / Classic 4:3" under the game (the browser remembers the choice, the page reloads with the parameter, and a page that is in a room asks before it leaves the match); only `16:9` and `4:3` are accepted, anything else on the address is ignored, the address beats the remembered choice. A **portrait phone** gets the classic picture (a 16:9 picture would be a thin strip) and says so when it is turned to landscape.
- **The game box is 16:9 and fits the window.** Its width is `min(100%, 1280 px, (window height - the page's header and bar) x 16 / 9)`: the whole picture and the bar under it are on the screen without scrolling (the page measures its own header and bar, wraps the header's buttons, and shows a slim header on a phone on its side). The ring around the box is a shadow, not a border, so the box has no frame inside it. The classic picture's box is 4:3, at most 960 px.
- **No black bar inside the box.** The game's window system (SDL's Emscripten driver) sets the canvas to `floor(CSS size x devicePixelRatio)` of its box and takes its first size from it (measured: 1000 x 562.5 CSS pixels gave a 1000 x 562 canvas, 853.33 x 480 at 1.5x gave 1279 x 720), and the game then draws its logical canvas with the largest scale that fits, with bars where the shapes differ: a canvas that is one pixel off 16:9 has a black line of its own. The page therefore sizes the box itself to a whole number of 16 x 9 device pixels (4 x 3 for the classic picture), the largest that fits, centred in a slot of the width above, with a CSS size that survives the layout's rounding. The canvas is exactly 16:9 at every window size and device ratio checked (1, 1.25, 1.5, 2, 3).
- **The page tells the game when the box changes.** SDL listens to the window's `resize` event only (and reads the device ratio then). The page watches its slot, header and bar (`ResizeObserver`), `fullscreenchange`, the window's `resize` and the device ratio (`matchMedia`) and sends a `resize` whenever the box's device pixels change, so resizing, fullscreen (a toggle does not always send a `resize`: reproduced), zooming and moving to another screen keep the canvas, the picture and the pointer right.
- **Fullscreen** (header button) shows the picture as large as the screen allows, bars on a screen of another shape (the stage's black, not the game's). A browser without the Fullscreen API for an element (Safari on an iPhone), or one that refuses it, gets the page's own fullscreen (fixed over the window, safe areas respected, a button to leave it).
- **Native sharpness with a 60 frames a second cap.** The canvas has the screen's own resolution (CSS size x device ratio). The game's loop is `requestAnimationFrame`, so a 120 / 144 / 240 Hz display drew it two to four times as often at that resolution for nothing: the page now measures the display (24 frames) and holds a display of about 100 Hz or more to 60 frames a second by a schedule that keeps its phase (60, 75 and 90 Hz displays are not touched; measured in headless Chrome with no frame limit: a display of 116 frames a second gave the game 59.8).
- **`web/four.html` (Play online)**: the four games' frames are 16:9 (4:3 with `?aspect=4:3`, or the choice the game page remembered, or the page's own selector); each game gets the shape (`&aspect=` on its address; a link for somebody else carries it only if this address names it); the games keep their places in the corners by colour; the windows that "Open a window" makes are sized for the wide picture.
- **Verified in real browsers** (headless Chrome with a throwaway profile and the built-in browser, against the real web image): 1280 x 720, 1920 x 1080, 1440 x 900 (16:10), 2560 x 1080 (21:9), 1366 x 768, 800 x 600, a narrow window, and a phone portrait and landscape at device ratios 1, 2 and 3 (the canvas is exactly 16:9, or 4:3 for the classic picture, and fills the box; clicks and taps land on the START button and the match screen's buttons; resizing, a change of the device ratio and fullscreen enter and leave with the picture right); a match between the page and a native client ran 20.00 and 20.01 ticks a second at device ratios 1 and 2, and the hidden-tab check still passes (19.98).
- **Fixed in passing: the guide and the footer lost their spaces.** The build's HTML minifier (emcc's `--collapse-inline-tag-whitespace`) deletes every plain space between an inline tag and the text next to it, so the production page read "Goal:get the most points", "Tapacts like a left click", "Versionv0.0.95" (found in the minified page of the image). Those spaces are now U+2005 (a breakable four-per-em space), which the minifier keeps.
- **Tests**: `tests/scripts/test_web_aspect.sh` (opt-in, not part of `./run_tests.sh`, like the hidden-tab check: needs a running web page and a Chromium-based browser): the page's own logic (`ANTS_PAGE`: the address and the order of the choices, the box's rules over 64,000 boxes (2,016 areas, 16 device ratios, both shapes, including the layout's rounding), the frame cap at 30 - 360 Hz and over a hidden gap), the page at desktop and phone sizes at device ratios 1 to 3, the address and the selector, resizing, fullscreen (the browser's and the page's own) the pointer and a tap on a phone; `--four` also checks `four.html`'s frames (about 350 checks in all, run against the real web image at every size of the matrix). The web build's 16:9 default (`kWebDefaultAspect`) is pinned by a unit test (suite 3.12).
- **Files.** `web/shell.html`, `web/four.html`, `include/ants_app/canvas_layout.hpp`, `src/ants_app/application.cpp` (`choose_aspect`), `tests/test_app/test_canvas_layout.cpp` (these three are the flip of the web default, on top of M2 / M3), `tests/scripts/test_web_aspect.sh`, `tests/scripts/web_aspect_check.py`, `README.md`, `docs/NETWORK_PORT.md`, this file. `docker/nginx.conf` is unchanged (every page and script is still revalidated).

## v0.0.96 - 2026-10-02 - The web game starts at once and keeps playing in a hidden tab

Two changes to the browser build; the desktop game and the simulation are untouched (no state hash, no protocol message, no golden number changed: the protocol stays 9). **For the player**: the page no longer hides the game behind a "Click anywhere to play!" card, and a network match goes on when its tab is hidden or its window minimised. An independent review of the first version found nine things (music that started in a hidden page, a hidden seat that took ten minutes to notice a silent server, a frame rate at which a hidden seat fell behind, a test that only held with turns of 100 ms, five changes of the code that no test saw, and four smaller ones): they are fixed in the same release and listed at the end. The change was made on v0.0.93 (turns of 100 ms, two ticks each) and is built here on v0.0.95, which sits on v0.0.94's 50 ms turns and lag policy: **every number below that depends on the length of a turn was measured again on this tree** (turns of 50 ms, one tick each, v0.0.94's server and runner), and what v0.0.94 changed for a hidden seat is said where it matters.

**No start overlay.** The page covered the game with a card and a start button until the first click, to get the browser's permission for sound. The game now starts as soon as its data has downloaded, with the original's own loading screen and quick help as always; the loading card shows only the download, goes away by itself and takes no clicks (a failure still puts its message and its button there). The sound follows the first press on the page:

- **What the old page did.** SDL's own Emscripten audio already resumes the Web Audio context at the first `keydown`, `mousedown` and `touchstart` (`autoResumeAudioContext`, once each, on the document and the canvas). The page's own tries did nothing: it looked for a global `SDL2` (SDL keeps its context in `Module.SDL2`, so the checks never ran) and made a second context at the click that never played anything. Both are gone.
- **What the page does now.** It keeps trying until the context really runs: on `pointerdown`, `mousedown`, `click`, `touchend` (iOS Safari and Chrome on Android grant the permission at the END of a touch) and `keydown`, and takes its listeners off as soon as the context runs. A press before the context exists (a click while the game's data downloads) is not lost, a press that grants nothing (Esc, a browser shortcut) does not use the attempt up (the `once` of SDL's own handlers would), and a context that stops later (Safari: a phone call, the lock screen) is resumed by the next press. The music and the sound effects begin the moment the browser lets the context run. What each browser needs is in `docs/NETWORK_PORT.md` ("Sound and the autoplay rules").
- **Keyboard.** The canvas takes the keyboard when the game is ready, and again at the first press inside the game; the guard that keeps the browser's own reactions (Ctrl+O, Ctrl+S, F1, the arrows) away from the game now switches on when the game is ready (it used to wait for the overlay to go).
- **Also.** `web/four.html`'s hint about hidden windows now says what happens (see below: v0.0.94's text said that the others play on and a hidden seat is dropped after 30 s; it keeps playing now); the frames of that page and the windows it opens start the same way.

**A network match keeps playing in a hidden or minimised tab.** A browser stops `requestAnimationFrame` in a page that is not shown and slows its timers: to once a second, and after a while hidden to once a minute (headless Chrome 154: the 100 ms interval of a page that another tab covered ran once a second for the first 58 s and then once a minute, with one call 13 s later and none in the rest of a 130 s measurement; Chrome documents the minute for a page hidden for five minutes). The frame loop was the only thing that polled the connection and ran the lock-step runner, so a hidden seat ran no turns and acknowledged none. What the server does with such a seat is v0.0.94's lag policy: the room goes on at 20 ticks a second without it, the other players read "<name> is lagging (N s behind)" from the moment it is 3 s behind, and the server drops it after 30 s without an acknowledgement (the player then reads "You were away too long and were dropped from the match."): in v0.0.94 a hidden tab was a lost seat after 30 s (measured there: a tab hidden for 40.5 s was dropped 30.0 s after its last acknowledgement). Before that, v0.0.93's server held the whole room for the seat at 3 s and dropped it 20 s later (measured with that build, the built-in browser, Chromium 152, two seats, one hidden: the room's ticks stopped 4 s after the seat was hidden and the match ended about 20 s later). With this release the hidden seat keeps up, and none of it happens.

- **The network drives the match while the page is hidden.** Every message of the server (a turn every 50 ms in a match, the answer to the page's ping every second in a room too), an error and the close of the socket wake the game from the browser's WebSocket callback (`WasmWsConnection::set_on_wake` -> `NetGame::set_on_wake` -> `Application::background_pump`; events, not timers, so the browser does not slow them). A step does what a frame does for the network (the network clock, the room or the session, the lock-step runner's ticks under its own rules, the acknowledgements, hashes and pings, the room and start events) and nothing that is drawn or heard. A timer of the page calls the exported `ants_background_pump()` as the fallback for a quiet server: a Worker's interval of 250 ms, which the browser does not slow in a hidden page (headless Chrome 154: 18 to 40 messages in every 10 s of 150 s hidden, while the page's own `setInterval` ran once a minute from the 58th second), and the page's own `setInterval` where a Worker cannot be made.
- **No sound and no music in a hidden page.** The sound effects of a step's ticks and the start voice are dropped, not saved up; the buttons' cues and the can't-go cue (`play_effect`) are silent in a step too; and the music that the start of a match, its end or the loss of the server would start or close (`enter_match`, `check_match_over`, `return_to_map_select`) is not touched: the last such wish is kept and carried out once, by the next frame or when the page is shown (a hidden page that still draws: a frame is what a heard page runs). The piece that plays goes on meanwhile. Checked in a real hidden tab by logging `Module.FS.open`: the tab that was hidden from its start opened `INTRO.mp3` when it started (the setup screen's music: not a step) and no music file after that, while the match began or after the server was stopped under it (a guard of the start voice alone had left `ANTS2A.mp3` opened at the start of a match: `tests/scripts/test_web_hidden.sh` catches that).
- **One driver at a time.** The web build follows `visibilitychange` itself (and asks the browser at start and at every wake-up). A shown page is driven by its frames only; a hidden page by the wake-ups, unless its frame loop keeps up with the turns by itself, a frame at least once per turn (an embedded browser pane, such as the built-in browser used for testing, reports every page hidden while the pane is off screen and still draws it at 120 frames a second): then the frames drive it, with picture and sound, as before, and the wake-ups stand down. A frame loop that is slower than the turns still acknowledges only once per frame, so the wake-ups step between its frames (the first version of this change stood down for a quarter of a second after every frame and counted a frame for at most 0.1 s: on v0.0.93's runner, with four frames a second, 215 - 260 ms apart, the review saw a hidden page run 16 ticks a second and fall behind, in headless Chrome with a Worker as the page's frame clock; v0.0.94's runner runs a standing backlog down at up to 4x also in a long frame, so on this base even that window keeps such a page level, but it would acknowledge in bursts, four times a second, instead of with every turn: N5.41 pins the 20 ticks a second and that the wake-ups step between the frames). A step never starts inside another (a frame inside a frame or a step is refused), and frames and wake-ups share one timer, so the time between two of them is counted once. A local game is untouched: it stands still while the page is hidden, as the native game does when minimised.
- **Time, and the silence of the server.** A wake-up gives the network's clock, and with it the lock-step runner, at most one second of the time that passed (a page that was asleep must not be paid back more at once; a clock that went backwards counts for nothing; v0.0.94's runner then runs what waited down at up to 4x inside that step). The rest of a long gap is handed to the session AFTER the connection has been read (`pump_network(dt, gap)` -> `NetGame::note_gap` -> `ClientSession::note_gap`): what waited in the link is heard first and is no silence; a host that said nothing has been silent for the gap too, so the session's 10 s of silence are 10 s of real time however seldom the browser wakes a hidden page. A host is called silent only after it has been ASKED (the review found that a host that holds its turns, quiet but alive, was condemned at the first wake-up after ten seconds, because a sleeping page pings nobody): a wake-up that finds it unasked since it last spoke counts the gap only up to 3 s short of the limit, the ping it sent is the question, an answer is a message that wakes the page and is heard, and a host that does not answer has the gap held against it by the next wake-up. With the page's timer a Worker's, four wake-ups a second, a silent server is noticed after its ten seconds, as a shown page notices it; measured with a paused server (`docker pause`: the connection stays open and nothing comes) on the images of this release: a shown seat at +10.0 to +10.1 s, and seats that had been hidden for 22 s, for 75 s (the page's own timer already at once a minute) and for 150 s, at +10.0 to +10.3 s each (the connections polled every 0.1 s; polled once a second they read +10.0 s, +11.0 s and +10.0 s). Counting a second per wake-up, as the first version of this change did, needed ten wake-ups, which with Chrome's page timer at once a minute are ten minutes: the review's paused server was noticed at +10 s by a shown seat and at +606 s by a hidden one (measured again with that version on v0.0.93: +10.0 s and +610.6 s, the seat hidden for 75 s before the pause; the rule does not depend on the length of a turn). A live host, whether it streams its turns, holds them or has data waiting while the page sleeps, is never taken for silent. When the page is shown again the last wake-up brings the clocks up to date and the frame timer restarts from that moment, so the hours that the page was hidden are never one huge frame.
- **The console's line.** One line per hidden period of a second or more in which wake-ups stepped, and at most one every ten seconds (a tab that is flicked through said a line every time, 258 for 258 toggles): "The page was hidden for 45.1 s; the match advanced by 901 ticks and 1126 wake-ups stepped it in the background", or, for a period in a room, "The page was hidden for 25.0 s; 51 wake-ups kept the connection going in the background (no match tick ran)" (it used to say "advanced by 0 ticks").
- **Safe against its own result.** A step may end the session (the host left, the connection was lost): the callbacks run from copies because ending the session deletes the connection whose callback is running. Checked in the browser: the server was stopped under a hidden page, the page noticed the close, went back to the setup screen with "The connection to the other players was lost." and raised no error.
- **In a real hidden tab** (headless Chrome 154 with a throwaway profile, the real web and server images of this release, a tab hidden from its start or covered by a second one): the room ran 2,404 ticks in 120.2 s, 20.00 a second, with both players in it; the hidden seat's own hash reports gave 20.00 ticks a second and all 24 reports that both games posted agreed; 2,619 and 2,611 messages of the server reached the hidden and the shown page and **none of them was a `Lag` notice** (`tests/scripts/test_web_hidden.sh 120`, which checks that now; the same counter in a page frozen for 8 s counted 7 notices at the other seat, and for 45 s 29: it does see them). A server stopped under a hidden page (`docker stop`, `docker kill`): both pages saw the close within 0.2 s of each other, went back to the setup screen and raised no error. `web/four.html` with two frames, hidden for 45 s: the games agreed at ticks 600, 900 and 1200 and again at 1300 after the show, and the console said "The page was hidden for 45.1 s; the match advanced by 901 ticks and 1126 wake-ups stepped it in the background". 253 toggles of a tab in 40 s: one console line (the first hidden period) and no mismatch. A hidden seat whose frame loop is a Worker's interval of 1,000, 250, 240, 230, 220 or 100 ms, or none (the room's ticks from the control interface over 40 s, the hidden seat's own from its hash reports): the room ran 19.99 to 20.01 ticks a second at every period and the hidden seat 20.00 to 20.02, every hash equal. A shown seat next to a hidden one drew 60.0 frames a second and its corner read "ping 3 ms" and "delay 98 ms" after five move orders (the hidden seat gave none: "delay -"); the seat that had been hidden for 57 s read "ping 3 ms" one second after it was shown, with the match clock level and no "Catching up..." or lag line.
- **Limits.** A computer that sleeps and a phone that locks its screen or leaves the browser (iOS suspends the page within seconds and the system closes its connections) stop the page whatever it does, and a tab that the browser FREEZES loses its WebSocket at the thaw (close code 1006, measured with a page frozen for 8 s: the room's match was over 0.3 s after the thaw; a plain `WebSocket` does the same): the server sees the connection close and drops the seat at once, and a tab that stays frozen for longer than the server's patience is dropped before the thaw (v0.0.94's rule: 30 s after its last acknowledgement; measured with a page frozen for 45 s: the match ended 30.3 s after the freeze, before the thaw). Any seat that stops for such a reason is called lagging by the others after 3 s and dropped after 30 s, as before. The reconnect work that comes next will let such a seat come back. Where the page's Worker timer is slowed or missing, the page's own timer (once a minute in Chrome after a minute hidden) is the only wake-up, and a silent server is noticed at the second of them after its ten seconds.
- **Known gap.** No automated test reaches the code that exists only in the web build (the WebSocket callbacks' wake-ups, `NetGame::set_on_wake`'s wiring, `ants_background_pump`, the page's timer, sound and visibility logic). `docs/NETWORK_PORT.md` ("Known gap, and the checks that cover it") says what covers it by hand, and `tests/scripts/test_web_hidden.sh` runs that check against a stack that is running, with a throwaway headless browser (opt-in, not part of `./run_tests.sh`): the room at 20 ticks a second with both players in it, every hash equal, no music file opened while hidden, and, new on this base, **no `Lag` notice reaching either page** (the server tells the room when a seat is 3 s behind: a hidden seat that keeps up is never told it lags).
- **Tests.** `test_network_app` (suite 3.6) grows from 38 to 51 tests (750 to 1,081 assertions). A native test cannot receive a browser's events, so `HiddenDuo` plays the browser, and a `VirtualClock` is the clock of the application's frames and wake-ups (`Application::set_clock`: no test sleeps for a window any more, every bound is exact). Every test of the change is independent of the turn length (`net::kTurnMs`, `LockstepRunner::kTickMs`): it passed on v0.0.93 with turns of 100 ms and two ticks, and passes here with turns of 50 ms and one tick. What the tests pin: the match runs on wake-ups alone and every hash is compared; one driver at a time, the stand-down window to a hair either side of one turn; a wake-up gives the network at most exactly one second (ten minutes, five seconds, one, 0.4) and never less than nothing; no sound and no music in a step (a match that begins, ends or is lost hidden, a Begin that the very last wake-up reads); a seat whose hidden page gets a frame a second, four, 220 ms apart, fifty a second or none runs 20 ticks a second; a host that was asked and does not answer is called silent by the wake-up after its ten seconds of real silence (2.9 s after the question no, 3.1 s yes; wake-ups every 1.5 s, a minute, ten minutes), a host with data waiting and one that holds its turns but answers pings are never taken for silent, and a shown page's frames hand over no gap; a wake-up after the application has stopped does nothing; the console's line. Each rule was undone in turn on this tree (turns of 50 ms): 64 mutations of the code, one at a time, each against the whole suite (rebuilt after every change and again after every restore): 61 were killed by at least one test; the other three change nothing that can be seen from outside (the silence stamp of a session that is not in its normal mode is never read; `NetGame::note_gap` outside a match finds nothing to tell; and the stand-down of 0.05 s, which is the real one turn at 50 ms, so that "one turn -> 0.05 s" is no change at all). The stand-down of the first version (a quarter of a second) is caught by the pins of the wake-ups' steps (N5.33, N5.41), not by a slower tick rate: v0.0.94's runner keeps such a page level.
- **Rewritten tests**: `test_network_app` N5.34 (it bounded what one wake-up of a second pays out at `1000 / kTurnMs + 1` turns, one second of play; v0.0.94's runner runs a standing backlog down at up to 4x also in a long step, so a second of clock pays out the turns that waited, up to the 80 ticks that a second holds at 4x (31 turns ran in the test, for 30 that waited): the bound is `4 * 1000 / kTurnMs` now; the exact second of network clock that the cap gives, which is what catches a larger cap, is asserted as before). The new tests are numbered N5.32 - N5.44: they were N5.27 - N5.39 on v0.0.93, and v0.0.94 took N5.27 - N5.31 for its latency tests. No other existing test case or assertion of v0.0.94 or v0.0.95 was changed, removed or weakened.
- **Found by the review of this change and fixed.** (1) A test held only with turns of 100 ms (`ran >= 3 && ran <= 4`; with 50 ms turns it was 20): every bound derives from the turn length now, and so do the documents. (2) A wake-up started music in a hidden page (`enter_match` and `return_to_map_select`): see "No sound and no music". (3) A hidden page with frames at 215 - 260 ms slowed the room: the window and the clamp, see "One driver at a time". (4) A hidden seat noticed a silent server after about ten minutes (and the first fix condemned a live host that held its turns: the independent review of the fix found that): see "Time, and the silence of the server". (5) Five changes of the code that no test saw (`kMaxWakeSeconds` 1.0 -> 8.0, `kFrameAliveSeconds` 0.25 -> 0.05 and -> 0.29, the lower clamp of a wake-up's time, the `is_running_` check of a wake-up): pinned exactly now. (6) Chrome's throttle began after about a minute in the measurement, not five (the page, the documents). (7) A frozen tab loses its socket at the thaw (the documents). (8) The known gap above. (9) The line "advanced by 0 ticks", the line for every toggle, the stale comment of `wasm_ws.cpp`.
- **Files.** `include/ants_app/application.hpp`, `src/ants_app/application.cpp`, `include/ants_net/netgame.hpp`, `src/ants_net/netgame.cpp`, `include/ants_net/session.hpp`, `src/ants_net/session.cpp`, `include/ants_net/wasm_ws.hpp`, `src/ants_net/wasm_ws.cpp`, `web/shell.html`, `web/four.html`, `tests/test_app/test_network_app.cpp`, `tests/scripts/test_web_hidden.sh`, `tests/scripts/web_hidden_check.py`, `include/ants_app/version.hpp`, `tests/test_app/test_app_integration.cpp` (12.108), `README.md`, `docs/NETWORK_PORT.md`, `STATUS.md`.

## v0.0.95 - 2026-10-02 - Community maps play as in the original: their default ant types and power-ups by tile

Maps from the community's editors (they are not part of the repository) play as they do in the original. **What a player sees.** (1) A map that names a **default ant type** has every ant of every team of that type, the ants it starts with and every hatch: `popcorn.lvl` hatches combat ants (the owner, who plays the original: "the original maps all spawn a regular ant when you hatch an ant, but some of the custom maps will spawn a specific ant type. For instance, on the popcorn.lvl, it spawns combat ants"). (2) **Power-ups and flower droppers are recognised by their tile and look the same on every map** ("the power-ups always display the same on every single map regardless"), whatever the dictionary of the file calls them: a dictionary that names them `.` no longer turns them into rocks. (3) **A Bomber, Fire ant or Swimmer that is busy** (planting, lighting, bridging, picking up a power-up, flinching, burning, grabbing food, entering the hill ...) gets the plain move cursor and a plain move on a right click, as in the original (it showed the target cursor over a valid special target and ordered a special). (4) **A pick-up rebuilds the panel of a selected ant every time**: the text of its type (string 12 for a group) and both command pedestals pop up, also when the ant takes the power-up of its own type again. **The rules of the lock-step game changed on the shipped maps too** (the attack clip's rule, first item below: 72 of 480 scripted plays of the shipped maps differ from v0.0.92, GAUNTLET 40 of 80, MEDIUM 25, TREASURE 7, none on TINY, SMALL and ISLANDS; the twelve plays of test 1.7, which give orders of movement only, and `map_sweep` over the six maps, 216 plays of 2,400 and 14,400 ticks with nine seeds, are those of v0.0.94), so **the network protocol is version 9**: a game of v0.0.94 cannot join a v0.0.95 server's room or LAN game, and the other way round ("version mismatch"): **update the game**, the server and the page together. Everything was read again in `Ants.exe` with Capstone before anything was changed (`docs/GAME_REVERSE_ENGINEERING.md` 5.62 has every address), and an independent review of the first version of this work found nothing critical or high; its findings are fixed here (below: the panel after a pick-up, the protocol number, and "Also from the second review").

- **The attack clip resumes the saved auto-engage of every ant that still has the engage flag, as the original does: a change of the lock-step rules that shows on the shipped maps.** The original calls the resume (`FUN_0101dd6f`) at the end of every attack clip (`0x101ef4a`) without looking at a type: the engage flag `+0xbc` alone decides. The flag is set by the combat ant's auto-engage and cleared only by the resume and by StartEngaged; **a new order and a pick-up leave it alone**, so a combat ant that was ordered away in the middle of an auto-engage and then took another power-up (a thief, a bomber, a fire ant, a swimmer) still has it, and at the end of its next attack it walks back to where the engage began. v0.0.92 asked `own type == Combat` first and left such an ant standing. It happens in real plays of GAUNTLET, MEDIUM and TREASURE: a scripted player that clicks power-ups, attacks and raids gives **72 plays of 480 that differ from v0.0.92** (six maps x two rosters x 40 engine seeds, 8,000 ticks: GAUNTLET 40 of 80, MEDIUM 25, TREASURE 7, none on TINY, SMALL and ISLANDS), and with the old condition put back every one of them has v0.0.92's hashes again, tick by tick. **A v0.0.92 peer and a newer one can therefore disagree on a shipped map in such a play**: the state-hash check reports a desync and ends the match, and the door checks nothing but the protocol version, which guarded the layout of the messages only: **v0.0.95 raises it to 9 for this reason** ("The network protocol is version 9" below), so that a game of v0.0.94 and one of v0.0.95 refuse each other at the Hello instead of desynchronising in the middle of a game. Both sides must update, as for every engine fix. Tests: 4.6 (every ant that has the flag resumes, whatever its type: fails with v0.0.92's condition and with `type_of(a) != Combat ||`), 4.7 (the same through the player's commands: a combat ant engages, is ordered away, takes a Thief power-up, attacks and goes back to where it stood), 1.8 (twelve plays of the shipped maps with power-ups, attacks, raids and special orders, pinned: five differ from v0.0.92, each at an ant of own type Bomber, Fire or Swimmer with the flag set).
- **The level's default ant type (LVL block 3).** The loader read block 3 and threw it away. In the original it is the level's **default ant type**: the second word is a tile index, kept in the level (`+0x70`) only when the tile has the power-up flag, so a level has one exactly when block 3 holds one of the ids 62 .. 66 (Combat, Thief, Bomber, Swimmer, Fire), whatever the dictionary calls that entry (`POPcOrN` names 62, `Bombz Away` 64), and the original's ant type getter (`0x100f9cb`, 25 call sites, all catalogued in the RE doc) answers it for every ant whose own type is 0. Every ant is created with the own type 0 (the constructor `0x101a77a`; the message that creates an ant carries its team, its index and its place, no type), so **the ants a level starts with and the ants that hatch are all of that type**, for all four teams: its clips, voices, panel text and ability pedestal, its abilities (the combat ant's punch and auto-engage reflex, mines, fire walls, swimming and bridges, the burn overlay) from the first second. What the original reads from the own type field itself stays 0: a default-type ant that takes a power-up drops nothing, one that dies leaves nothing, default thieves cannot raid (the raid test reads the field), a failed attack does not cancel a default combat ant's timer and its pick box is the small one. The remake: `Grid::default_ant_tile()` / `default_ant_type()`, `SimulationEngineImpl::type_of` is the getter and every one of its sites asks it (movement, can-enter and the A* weights, set-action, combat, special orders and their prediction, the ability API), the own-type readers keep `AntUnit::type`; `AntSnapshot::type` is what the ant IS (the HUD, the voices, the sprites, the bots read it) and the new `raw_type` the field (the pick box reads it); the state hash covers the default tile **only when a level has one**; `BotView::default_ant_type()` and the worker bot's pool "the ants of the default type" (a bot sees what a person sees). One site is not modelled because it does not exist in a lock-step game: `0x101c7e3`, the blocked-tile branch of the ants of the OTHER machines.
- **Power-ups and flower droppers are recognised by tile id, not by dictionary name.** The remake made a power-up of a layer-2 cell only when its dictionary name began with `pu_` (and typed it by six exact names), and a solid rock of a cell named `.`; the original asks `FUN_01007202` (the power-up flag of the id, 62 .. 66) and types by `FUN_01021087`, the dictionary's name never enters (the remap is the identity, `0x10067a5`; a name only decides which graphics are loaded, and the table at `0x1001ba8` forces the ids of the power-ups, the plants and the droppings to load whatever the dictionary says). The droppers (`FDTASK`, `0x100fc0d`) were keyed on a name with `flower` or `clover` and a flag of exactly 1; the original takes a plant by the property bit of its id (`FUN_01007227`), the FIRST Block-4 record at its cell, and a flag other than 0, one dropper per record. All three follow the ids now (`movement::is_powerup_tile`, `is_dropper_plant_tile`, `ant_type_of_powerup_tile`).
- **The power-ups are drawn the same on every map** (the owner's observation, and the shipped maps show it: `SMALL.LVL` names four of the five ids `.` and its droppers drop bombers and fire ants all the same): `Renderer::set_level` skips power-up ids in the static pass whatever their name and draws a plant by its id, the animation of that number.
- **The library of the community, as the sweep sees it** (the owner's folder of 586 files, 540 of which load; numbers only). Block 3 names a default type on **99 maps** (62 Combat 56, 63 Thief 17, 64 Bomber 16, 65 Swimmer 5, 66 Fire 5); 416 say `0x7FFE`, 13 name a tile that is no power-up, and **12 name an index outside their dictionary** (11 say 4094 with a dictionary of 1,324 names, one says 38912 with 670: the original reads a word of its heap there, so nobody can say what it does; the remake plays them as maps without a default, test 1.9); 98 of the 99 start ants, which are now typed (the 99th places none). The tile ids hold **15,527 power-up cells on 489 maps**; the engine had found 6,822 of them on 386 maps (390 maps differed, 103 lost every power-up, and 8,673 cells that the dictionary calls `.` had become rocks); it finds all of them now (3,278 bombers, 2,463 fire, 1,873 thief, 3,891 combat, 4,022 swimmer; 26 cells of 3 maps, whose names began with `pu_` but were none of the six, had no kind at all, and the cells of two more maps were of other kinds than their ids say). Droppers: 154 on 87 maps before, **164 on 94 maps** now. `map_sweep` over the 540 maps (2,400 ticks, both rosters, every play repeated in the same process and in a second one; before and after on the same machine): **no crash, no hang, no error, no divergence, in either run** (1,060 plays); 121 maps play hash-identical, 419 change, and every one of them for one of the reasons above (the sweep's before / after hashes against the engine's view of each map: no map changed without a cause, and none that has a cause stayed the same but the two whose rosters the sweep refuses). The same sweep of a build with AddressSanitizer and UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer: 1,060 plays, no crash, hang, error or divergence and **no sanitizer report**, and every one of the 1,067 plays has the hashes of the normal build.
- **Fixed on the way.** (1) The last frame of the attack clip resumed the combat ant's auto-engage only for an own type of Combat; the original calls the resume at `0x101ef4a` without looking at any type (the engage flag is the test), which matters for a default combat ant and also moves plays of the shipped maps (the first item above). (2) `docs/GAME_REVERSE_ENGINEERING.md` 4.5 called the second word of message 5 an "ant type"; it is the ant's index in its team's table (the constructor stores it at `+0x58`, the type at `+0x54` is written 0), and 4.4 said that the community template's block 3 "maps to nothing" (it is the level's default type).
- **The cursor and the right click know a busy ant, and a pick-up rebuilds the panel** (two HUD differences that the first review turned up and the second review finished; neither is specific to the default type). (1) The ant type getter `FUN_0100f9cb(ant, 1)` is not only the 0 ms cooldown that A11 of `docs/reverse_engineering/movement/06_orders_goto_and_group_dispatch.md` and 5.62 said: for an own type of Bomber, Fire or Swimmer it answers Worker for every action other than idle, walking or stunned (Capstone `0x100f9eb` - `0x100fa27`), and its flag-1 callers are the cursor's special-target test and the right click (`FUN_010282e0(1)` at `0x1026f9d`, `0x1027da6`, `0x1027db3`) and the accept predicate. A bomber that is planting a bomb, a fire ant lighting a wall or a swimmer building a bridge therefore shows the plain move cursor over a valid special target, and its right click is a plain move, until the clip ends; the panel text and the ability pedestal (flag 0) keep the ant's type, and an ant whose own type is 0 (every worker of a default-type level) is never filtered. `AntSnapshot::action` carries the original's action, `HUD::homogeneous_type(world, type, for_orders)` models the branch, and the whole table is pinned: every own type against every action 0 .. 0x20 and 0xFF, with flag 1 and flag 0 (`test_pointer_model`, "busy table" and "snapshot action"). (2) `FUN_0100cd40` is the last thing every pick-up does (its only call, `0x1020dd2`, in the same run of `FUN_01020cdb` as `SetAction(4)` at `0x1020d2a`; `FUN_0100cd7d` asks that the observer flag be off and the ant's team be the local player's, and the ant's selected flag `[ant + 0x50]`): it rebuilds the panel (`SetPanelMode(3, ...)`, or 4 when the stored panel is not 3), which posts the text of the type (string 12 for panel 4) **and raises both command pedestals** (`FUN_01028360(1, 1, kind, 1, 0, 1)` at `0x1027fde` - `0x1028001`, `(1, 1, 9, 1, 0, 1)` at `0x1027f99` - `0x1027fa7`: the slot states `[54f8]` and `[54fc]` go back from 2, latched, to 1), and **it plays no sound** (a status text and a change of the pedestals: never a voice). The HUD noticed a pick-up by the ant's own type changing, so a worker of a default-type level that took the power-up of that very type, and a typed ant that took the power-up of its own type again, were silent (a gap that the first fix documented), and a latched pedestal stayed latched after any pick-up. It notices the rising edge of the action 4 of a selected own ant now (`HUD::check_selected_pickups`), so every pick-up rebuilds the panel once: a group of two that take power-ups in the same update post string 12 once; another team's ant that is inspected, an own ant that is not selected, an ant that is selected while its clip already runs, a dead and a drowning ant rebuild nothing. Both are in `docs/GAME_REVERSE_ENGINEERING.md` 5.62.
- **The network protocol is version 9, and what moves the number is written down** (the second review's medium finding). The attack clip's rule changes the rules of the lock-step game on the shipped maps, and only `kProtocolVersion` separates two builds at the door (a Hello of another number is refused by a LAN host and by a server's room, and a LAN announcement of another number is listed as another version of the game). Nothing on the wire changed: the number moves because the rules of the engine did (history comment: "9: the community-map rules (default ant types, power-ups by tile, the attack clip)"). **The rule**, written in `include/ants_net/protocol.hpp` next to the constant and in `docs/NETWORK_PORT.md`: the protocol version changes with every change that the peers of a match must share, the messages AND the simulation's rules, any fix that changes a state hash of some play, even one that shows only on some maps or in rare sequences of orders. A v0.0.94 game cannot join a v0.0.95 server's room or LAN game and the other way round (the game says "This version cannot play with the host's version."): **update the game**. Tests: `test_lockstep` N2.1 (the number), `test_lobby` N4.2c (a Hello of protocol 6 and one of protocol 8 are refused by a LAN host and by a server's room and nobody is seated), `test_server` S3.3 (protocols 4 and 8, for a room that exists and for one that does not: the door decides the version first), `test_lan` L1.9b (a room of protocol 8 is listed as another version); the number 8 and 10 and the three places that compare it are mutation-checked.
- **Also from the second review.** An independent review of the first fix commit found no critical or high finding (the busy rule, the pick-up change and the action snapshot match the binary; a differential of 217,944 cases agreed). Besides the protocol number and the panel rebuild above: A11 of `docs/reverse_engineering/movement/06_orders_goto_and_group_dispatch.md` listed four writers of the ant's `+0x4c` / `+0x48`, there are six (also `0x101eca9`, `+0x48` = 0xd, and `0x101eded`, 0xe: the swimmer's bridge build and demolish), corrected; `docs/ORIGINAL_BINARY_MAP.md` (and its generator, `tools/analyze_binary.py`) calls `0x100cd40` an "Audio / SFX Dispatch" because the function pushes 3 and 4, the arguments of the panel rebuild, and a sound table has those ids: the document says now that its sound column and classification are a keyword guess; the suite's scratch folders (`test_server`) were `ants_server_test_<tag>` and `ants_secret_test_<tag>` under the temp folder, the same for every run on a machine, so two runs at the same time removed each other's files (S3.1, S3.18 and S3.21 failed when two copies of the binary ran together: measured), and are private to the process now (`ants_server_test_<pid>_<random hex>`, made new and removed at the end, S3.0; two copies at once pass); the README's row for the map sweep says 62 checks, which `map_sweep --selftest` prints.
- **Tests: a new suite 2.9.1 (`tests/test_sim/test_level_defaults.cpp`, 45 test cases, 9,910 assertions)** on synthetic levels made from the bytes of `TINY.LVL` (nothing of the community is in the repository): 1.1 - 1.7 the default tile (the five ids, none for anything else or for an index outside the dictionary, the dictionary's name irrelevant: `.`, `pu_comb`, anything, the loader reading block 3 from bytes, a re-init forgets it, the state hash covers it and a shipped map's does not move: **1.7 pins the state hashes of the six shipped maps in both rosters at tick 0, 800 and 1,600 of a scripted match of move orders, taken from the commit before this change; 1.8 pins twelve plays of 8,000 ticks with power-up pick-ups, attacks, raids and special orders, five of which differ from v0.0.92 by the rule of the attack clip's last frame**; 1.9 a block-3 index outside the dictionary (4094, 38912, the first one past it) is no default and plays like `0x7FFE`); 2.1 - 2.4 the starting ants (also with two teams), the hatch (clip and label), the idle labels and the Cancel order; 3.1 - 3.11 **equivalence scenarios**: an ant of own type T on a map without a default and a worker on a map whose default is T must stay identical tick by tick in every snapshot field, over walking on every terrain, the punch and the reflex, a swimmer in water and on bridges, fire walls, bombs and duds with the burn overlay, landings and bridge collapses, hits while engaged; 4.1 - 4.9 the own-type rules (a pick-up and a death drop nothing, default thieves cannot raid, the prediction of both, the combat timer's cancel, **the attack clip's resume for every ant that has the engage flag, white box and through the player's commands**, the AntOrder interface's raid, the ability entry points of a default-type worker); 5.1 - 5.6 power-ups by id (grids equal for the names of the shipped maps, `.` and damaged names; hashes equal through a pick-up; solid, walkable and picked up on arrival; the five kinds; the effect ids are no power-ups; the shipped maps' counts unchanged); 6.1 - 6.6 droppers by id (the flag other than 0, all 14 plant ids and their drop offsets, non-plant ids, the first record and a record shared by two plants, the plants list with the effect ids). `run_tests.sh` runs it as 2.9.1. Also: `test_render_parity` grows from 419 to 429 checks (`test_dotted_power_ups_and_flowers`: named, `.` and damaged dictionaries draw the same pixels, which are the animations of the ids 62 .. 66 and of three plants), `test_status_messages` from 271 to 292 (`test_default_type_texts`: panel text, go text and voice per default type, the pick box of a default combat ant against an own-typed one), `test_ai` from 71 tests / 319,699 assertions to 74 / 320,131 (AI1.21 the view shows the default type, copies keep it, the worker bot earns on a default-Combat and a default-Swimmer map; AI1.22 the map analysis' power-ups and costs for a `.`-named level equal the named one; AI1.21b the idle label that another team's ordered-but-standing default combat ant or swimmer is seen with), `test_ai_worker` AI3.23 (on a default-type level a stuck carrier is rescued and the pool works the others), `test_pointer_model` from 428 to 3,339 checks (the cursor and the right click for a busy bomber, fire ant, swimmer and thief, the burn overlay and the stun, a group, the own-type-0 worker; on the five default-type levels the ready voice by click, rubber band and Ctrl+A, the ability pedestal, the special targets, the right click and the panel rebuilt after a pick-up; and the second review's groups: **busy table** (every own type against every action 0 .. 0x20 and 0xFF, flag 1 and flag 0, the default-type workers, a hand-made snapshot's default action, the mixed groups: 515 checks), **snapshot action** (the state-only fallback row by row, and a bomber blown away, hit, attacking, unable to go, entering its hill and grabbing food, the HUD's answer checked at every tick: 2,153 checks) and **pick-up panel** (68 checks: both pedestals raised and the text posted at every pick-up: the same type again, the default-type cases, a group, panel 4 with one ant, two ants in one update; nothing for another team's inspected ant, an unselected ant, an ant selected during its clip, a dead, drowning or foreign snapshot, a fresh HUD)), `test_lockstep` N2.1 (the protocol number), `test_lobby` 64,222 to 64,231 assertions (N4.2c: a Hello of protocol 8), `test_lan` 12 to 13 tests and 4,995 to 5,005 assertions (L1.9b) and `test_server` 35 to 36 tests and 10,277 to 10,294 assertions (S3.0 the scratch folders, S3.3 the door), and `test_commands` N1.13 probes the default tile. The full `./run_tests.sh` passes on the release tree (every suite, 506 of 506 E2E, built with `-DANTS_WERROR=ON` without a warning).
- **Proven by mutation** (one change at a time, the affected suites rebuilt and run, the source restored byte for byte; **every mutation is killed**): 43 in the simulation and the loader (M01 block 3 never stored, M02 a getter that ignores the default, M03 / M04 a tile without the flag or outside the dictionary taken as a default, M05 - M12, M15, M17 - M20, M22, M24 - M28 one site of the getter at a time asking the own type, M13 / M16 / M21 / M23 / M29 a raw-field site asking the getter, M14 the old attack-clip condition, M30 power-ups by name again, M31 - M35 the old dropper rules and a wider plant flag, M36 a wider power-up flag, M37 two kinds swapped, M38 / M39 the labels, M40 a plain hatch, M41 / M42 the state hash without the default and with it always, M43 the loader's two words of block 3 swapped) and 12 in the application and the bots (A01 a plant's animation by name, A02 / A03 power-up cells by name in the renderer's static pass and in the grid, A04 - A06 the pick box and the voices by the wrong type, A07 / A08 the snapshot's two type fields mixed up, A09 - A11 the bots' view, pool and `AntView::type`, A12 `MapInfo` by name). The one that first survived (M42: the hash always mixing the tile would have moved the six shipped maps and no test noticed) is why test 1.7 exists. **The review of this entry found 15 mutants that no suite noticed (14) or that only the view fingerprint noticed (1); the new tests kill all of them, with 14 more mutants of the new code and its neighbours** (29 in all, one change at a time in a scratch copy, the objects rebuilt after every change and again after every restore): the attack clip's old condition and the effective-type variant (`type_of(a) != Combat ||`: 1.8, 4.6, 4.7), never resuming, a block-3 index equal to the dictionary's size (1.9) or wrapped into the tile table (1.2, 1.9), the plants list without the effect ids and `is_plant_object_tile` without 0x20 (6.6), the two raid sites of the AntOrder interface by the effective type (4.8: its InfiltrateAnthill order, which only tests give: the game's group orders are AntOrders of the Move type, and the bots give Commands), the ability entry points' readiness by the own type (4.9), the per-tick water flag of a default swimmer by the own type (2.3), the three ready voices by the own type, the selection's common type by the own type and the pick-up noticed by the effective type (`test_pointer_model`, default type), the idle labels another team's default ants are seen with and the idle carriers of the harvest task asking Worker (AI1.21b, AI3.23), and ten mutants of the busy-ant rule (none, also for flag 0, also for thieves, also for own type 0, walking, stunned or idle counted as busy, the cursor or the right click asking flag 0, the snapshot's action always 0: `test_pointer_model`, busy ants). One of them survived at first and made a test longer (the busy rule for an own type of 0). **The second review's fixes were proven the same way, 88 mutants, every one killed** (one change at a time in a scratch copy; only the objects of the changed file rebuilt, every test binary relinked): the protocol number (8 and 10) and the three places that compare it (a lobby, the server's door and the LAN list that refuse or mark only a newer version); the pick-up panel (the pedestals not raised, only the move or only the ability pedestal raised, raised at every update; the edge inverted, missing, taken for an ant without a record, looked for on the wrong action; another team's ant, a dead and a drowning ant counted; panel 4 with one ant, a group, the own type field, no text, string 12 never; the record kept across a new match, never updated); the busy rule (each of its conditions: the three types one by one, thieves, combat ants and workers added, the actions 0, 1 and 3 turned busy, the rule negated or applied to flag 0, the right click and the special target asking flag 0, the first ant deciding for a group, and every action from 2 to 0x15 and 0xFF taken out of the busy set one by one); the snapshot's action (always 0, the raw clip action, the state number, no state fallback, a busy default) and `orig_action_of` row by row (and the clip-wins rule); the scratch folders of `test_server` (the old fixed name, no removal, no process id). The review's own survivors (the combat ant filtered, six busy actions not busy, the pick-up guards, the state-only fallback, a hand-made snapshot's default action) are among them.
- **Rewritten tests**: `test_lockstep` N2.1 (it pinned `kProtocolVersion == 8`: it says 9 now). Extended, nothing weakened: `test_lobby` N4.2c (the Hellos of old protocols that a LAN host and a server's room refuse now include protocol 8), `test_server` S3.3 (protocols 4 and 8, for a room that exists and for one that does not) and the scratch folders of the whole suite (the helpers give a folder of the process, no assertion changed). No other existing test case or assertion was changed, removed or weakened; new cases and one probe were added to the files named above (the titles and comments of 1.7 and 6.5 and the header of the suite say what the first review corrected).
- **Documents**: `docs/GAME_REVERSE_ENGINEERING.md` 5.62 (the verified facts with addresses: block 3, the getter and its 25 sites, the creation of every ant, the own-type readers, the ids and the dictionary's `.`, what changed, the library), with notes in 4.4, 4.5, 5.29 and 5.38 (corrected after the review: the getter's flag 1 and its four callers, the engage flag that outlives the type and what it does to the shipped maps, the block-3 indexes outside the dictionary of 12 maps, the dropper's unsigned compare that may post twice for a shared record) and A11 of `docs/reverse_engineering/movement/06_orders_goto_and_group_dispatch.md`; `docs/BOTS.md` (the typed-ants and hatching rows, `BotView::default_ant_type()`, the power-up list by id); the README (the loader, the unit types, the power-up and dropper bullets, the suite rows 2.9.1, 2.20 and 3.1 - 3.5). The second review's: the rule that moves the protocol version (`include/ants_net/protocol.hpp`, `docs/NETWORK_PORT.md` "Protocol version 9"), 5.62's bullet on the pick-up chain (every address of `FUN_01020cdb`, `FUN_0100cd40`, `FUN_01027f07` and `FUN_01028360`) and on the pinned busy table, with a cross-reference from 5.60 (the burn overlay's action), the old "not ported" note of 5.38 about the text after a pick-up corrected, A11 (six writers), the note in `docs/ORIGINAL_BINARY_MAP.md` and `tools/analyze_binary.py`, the README (the status line, the protocol paragraph, the version lines and the suite rows measured).
- **Not done, and why**: the other layer-2 classes that the remake still names by the dictionary (food `fd*`, `*hill*`, `*start*`, `bridge`, `broken*`, and every other cell named `.`, an obstacle overlay: 93,892 cells of the library are named `.`, 8,673 of them power-ups, which this entry fixed) and the start markers by name (4.5) are the same kind of difference and are written down in 5.62 as not changed here; a block-3 index outside the dictionary (12 maps of the library) reads the original's heap (undefined there): "no default" here.

## v0.0.94 - 2026-10-02 - Less lag: ping and delay next to the FPS counter, 50 ms turns, an adaptive buffer, and a lagging player no longer freezes the others

The owner: "the multiplayer seems a little laggy on beta.playants.org, can we display latency with the fps?", and, once it was there and the lag was measured: lag reduction approved, and "I'm not a fan of making the other players' experience worse because somebody lags. Is there a way to punish the lagger and not punish everybody else?" The rules and the simulation are unchanged (every golden hash and every state hash suite is as it was; no test pins the hash of a network match: they compare the machines of a match with each other). **The network protocol is version 8** (7 is the room leader's START of v0.0.93: its `StartRequest` keeps type 24, and the new `Lag` message is type 25, the last one): a game of version 8 and one of version 7 (or any other) refuse each other at the `Hello` ("version mismatch"), so the server and the games must be updated together.

- **What the player feels.** A click takes about the round trip + 25 ms (the wait for the next seal) + 50 ms (the jitter buffer, one turn) + the frames of the window that sends and runs it (none for a headless test client, which runs flat out without a frame limiter; 17 - 21 ms measured in a window at 60 frames a second). **Headless clients: 91 ms on the same machine (the Docker stack, round trip 20 ms; it was 188), 130 - 137 ms at a round trip of 60 ms on a steady link (v0.0.91 gave 224 - 242 against the live beta server and 229 - 239 in the emulation of it, which matches)**; **a window at 60 frames a second (the web build in headless Chrome): 99.5 ms at a round trip of 3 ms and 162.5 ms at one of 71 ms**. On a link with 0 - 60 ms of jitter in each direction the delay is **164 ms (it was 292 in v0.0.91)** for a headless client, on one that also loses a packet every 30 s (a resend of 250 ms) 144 ms (it was 225) with as many stalls a minute (2.4 against 2.1, each about 30 ms longer). When one of the players stops (a window in the background, a process that hangs), the others' games ran at 3.1 turns a second of 10 in v0.0.91 (the host waited for the slow machine: 31 turns in a 10 s stop); now **they run at 20.0 of 20 (201 turns in 10 s), the laggard catches up at 4x (level again 3.1 s after coming back from a stop of 10 s; a tab of Chrome that was hidden for 5.5 s is level 0.2 s after its first frame, one hidden for 20.5 s 5.2 s after it)**, and the two windows of the match are 2 ms apart afterwards (463 ms in v0.0.91: the owner's screenshot, 6:40 against 6:41).
- **The readout** (new in this release): `ping NN ms` and `delay NN ms` next to the frame rate, in the room and in the match of a network game (never in a game of one machine), the web build too: the round trip to the host measured by this machine (the mean of the last five answers; a dash when the last answer is older than three seconds) and the time from sending one of its own commands to the tick that applies it (the median of the last five commands; a dash when the last one was applied more than ten seconds ago). See the review fixes below for what the first version of it got wrong (a ping that included the frame that read it, a mean that kept a stop on the screen for 25 s).
- **Turns of 50 ms, one tick each** (`kTurnMs` 100 -> 50, `kTicksPerTurn` 2 -> 1). Everything that is counted in turns keeps its time: the state hash every 20 turns (20 ticks, 1 s, as before), the turn log 600 turns (30 s), the host's flow control 60 turns (3 s), the referee's report window 128 turns (6.4 s), the seals a late pass catches up 10 (0.5 s), the runner's tick allowance in time; acknowledgements go out once per frame for the last turn that ran (at most 20 a second). The server's `turns` in a status is one per tick now. The bots' arena keeps its own model of a room's turn (2 ticks: `kTurnTicks` in `arena.cpp`), so the pinned baselines and every bot result are byte for byte what they were (suite 2.22 and `bot_arena --selftest` pass; nothing of `src/ants_ai` or `tests/test_ai/baselines.inc` changed); a room's bot now has half of that wait for its commands, never more.
- **The adaptive jitter buffer** (`ants_net/jitter.hpp`, a pure rule, tested alone; `LockstepRunner` uses it). One turn (50 ms) on a steady link, up to four (200 ms) where the jitter needs it. It measures the lateness of the turns (how much later than the earliest of the last 200 turns, 10 s, each was read: independent of the link's delay and of the clocks) and keeps the 95th percentile and 10 ms, in whole turns; **grows at once** when that asks for more, and by one turn at once after a stall that one more turn would have bridged (the late turn within 100 ms); a lost packet's 250 ms is a stall and grows nothing (a buffer for it would cost every command 100 - 150 ms); **shrinks slowly**, a turn after 10 s without a stall and then a turn every 10 s; a wait of 1.5 s or more (a frozen link, a host that stopped sealing) forgets what was read before it, and so does a frame that stood for a second (the application hands the network at most that: the clock jumped); a frame longer than 100 ms is no measure of the link (a hitch, a hidden window).
- **The runner, again** (it is what makes the buffer last): it starts when `target + 1` turns are queued; a stall is waited out **without owing the wait** (a late turn runs the moment it comes, never in a burst) and the buffer is collected again; a queue longer than the buffer is **run down at up to 4x**, in proportion to the time that the newest queued turn is further away than the buffer wants (a quarter more speed per 50 ms, 4x from 550 ms), so a bunch of turns after a frozen link, a frame that took 400 ms, a hidden window or a lagging player's backlog leaves nothing standing; a frame that stood for more than 100 ms runs the ticks its time holds at normal speed.
- **A server's room never waits for a lagger** (`HostSession`, a host without a seat; a game on the local network keeps its rule: its host has a seat and waits for a friend's slow machine up to 3 s, "Waiting for Bob..."). The server keeps sealing every 50 ms. A player more than **3 s** behind (its acknowledgements against the turns sealed) is announced to the others with the new `Lag` message once a second ("**Bob is lagging (12 s behind)**" at the top of their screens) until it is within **1 s**; its commands apply when they arrive; its runner runs the backlog at up to **4x** and its screen says "**Catching up...**" while it is more than 3 s behind; a player **60 s behind**, or whose acknowledgements have not moved for **30 s** while turns wait for it, is dropped like a player who left (a `Drop` at one tick for everybody, "%s dropped out of the game!", the existing path); the numbers are named constants (`kLagNoticeMs`, `kLagClearMs`, `kLagNoticeEveryMs`, `kLagDropBehindMs`, `kLagDropIdleMs`). Memory is bounded by the transports' buffers (512 KB TCP, 1 MB WebSocket: 60 s of turns are 18 - 115 KB, a flooding room is cut off in seconds) and the work per pass is the same. The old rule (the room waits after 3 s and drops the seat after 20 s: `laggard_drop_ms` in the server's room) is gone. **The room's leader** (v0.0.93: the first player) is a player like the others once the match runs, and the waiting room before it seals no turns, so nobody lags there: if the leader's window stops after the early START the room does not wait for it, the other player is told, its late clicks on START are counted as ignored requests (16 are free) and cost it nothing, and it catches up (`test_server` S3.33).
- **The server's turn clock.** It seals by a schedule of its own (`next_seal_ms += kTurnMs`), so it neither drifts nor bunches: measured with a client that polled without sleeping, in Docker (Linux) the turns of a room came every 50.00 ms with sd 1.11 ms (47.7 - 53.0 over 740 turns), and with 12 rooms of 4 clients sd 1.56 ms and 56.9 ms at the worst, none bunched: the 2 ms pass makes each turn 0 - 2.2 ms late and nothing drifts (the server of v0.0.91, measured the same way: 100.00 ms with sd 1.12 ms, and sd 1.51 ms and 109.8 ms at the worst with 12 rooms: the same spread in milliseconds, the code of the clock is unchanged). Nothing to fix. A variant that slept until the next turn was due was tried and dropped: on macOS it made the spacing worse (sd 4.2 ms, worst 79 ms, against 1.2 and 54).
- **The frame loop.** Poll, run the due ticks and draw happen in that order in every frame (native and `requestAnimationFrame`), so a turn that has arrived is run and drawn by the frame that follows; running a tick between frames would change the state earlier than the screen can show it. What is left of the 17 - 21 ms that a window at 60 frames a second adds (the input read at the start of a frame, the frame that polls) is described in `docs/NETWORK_PORT.md` with the changes that would cut it; not done.
- **Server load.** 12 demo rooms of 4 headless clients at 50 ms turns (a scratch tool of the measurements): the server container uses **8.5 % of one core on average** (6 - 13 %; 1.4 % idle) and 3 - 4 MB, and nobody was called a lagger; **the server of v0.0.91 (turns of 100 ms) used 5.7 %** (4.4 - 7.8 %) under the same load, so the shorter turn costs 1.7 times the work above the idle pass (2.8 points of a core for 48 players, about 0.6 % of a core per room). A finding of that test, fixed in this release (next but one bullet): the TCP listener's backlog was 8, so a burst of 48 connections in a few milliseconds lost some on macOS.
- **Together with the room leader's START and flood control (v0.0.93).** Both changes touched the same code, so the overlaps were measured and tested instead of assumed. **The message budget** (1000 a second, a burst of 1000) was set for turns of 100 ms, and a client acknowledges twice as often now: `test_lockstep` N2.39 measures what an honest client sends, with the real sessions over a simulated link and the host noting when it takes each message: the busiest second held **27 messages in steady play, 48 for a person who gives 20 orders a second, 89 for a window that catches up at 4x after a freeze of 10 s**, and an uplink that was stuck for 25 s (a server drops a player after 30 s without an acknowledgement) delivered **637 at once**, within the burst. The budget stays as it is; the comment of `flood.hpp` (the sequencer takes 1280 commands a second now, not 640) has the new numbers. **A leader that lags** is described above and tested (S3.33). `StartRequest` stays type 24, the released one, and `Lag` is 25. The one thing that broke was a script: the raw flood client of `tests/scripts/test_ants_server.sh` said Hello with a literal protocol 7, a server of protocol 8 refused it, and the two flood checks of v0.0.93 failed (no seat to drop, no pongs); it takes the protocol from `--version` now.
- **The listeners hold 64 waiting connections** (found by the load test above). `listen()` was given 8 for the TCP door (native clients, LAN hosts, the server's game port) and 32 for the WebSocket door (the browsers behind the proxy); the control interface's had 64. Measured with a bare listener and 48 non-blocking connects, 0.3 s before the first `accept()` (macOS, `somaxconn` 128): a backlog of 8 gave 8 connections, 16 gave 16, 32 gave 32 and 64 gave all 48, and with 8 the other 40 had not come after 12.5 s. A lobby that sends many players at once while a pass of the server's loop is slow is such a burst. Both doors use 64 now (`kListenBacklog` in `tcp.cpp` and `ws.cpp`, with the measurement in a comment; the operating system caps the value at its own limit). `test_tcp` N3.8 (48 connections, all accepted, each a working connection) and `test_ws` W1.23 (64) fail with the old numbers (mutation-checked on macOS) and pass.
- **Also fixed in this release** (found with the readout): "Waiting for the other players..." stayed on the screen after a real stall (`stalled_ms()` is the time since a tick was due and had no turn); a stall used the buffer up for good (the 400 ms of unspent time ran every later turn the moment it came, ticks in pairs, no buffer); a queue left standing was never run down (a bunch, a 400 ms frame, a hidden window: half a second of delay for good, the two windows of a match 0.5 s apart); the network's clock was cut at 100 ms with the local simulation's, and a hidden window ran 8 of the 15 ticks that its time holds and slowed the host's flow control for everybody. Each is reproduced by a test that fails without the fix.
- **Measured** (real native clients, headless, so **unpaced: they run flat out with no frame limiter**, two in a demo room, a relay on the client's machine that logs every message at the wire; "before" is v0.0.91: turns of 100 ms and the buffer of two turns): the Docker stack (round trip 20 ms): delay 188 -> **91 ms**; round trip 60 ms: 235 -> **134 ms**; jitter of 0 - 60 ms: 292 -> **164 ms**; a lost packet every 30 s on top: 225 -> **144 ms**; the other window while one stops for 10 s (`SIGSTOP`): **31 turns (3.1 a second of 10) -> 201 (20.0 a second of 20)**; the stopped window is level with the match 3.1 s after its first turn back; **a real browser tab (the WebAssembly build) blocked for 12 s by a script: the other window kept 20.1 turns a second (19.9 before, 20.1 after) at an unchanged delay**; the two windows of a match end up 2 ms apart (463 ms apart in the version of the owner's screenshot, v0.0.91). The tables with the stalls and the tick gaps are in `docs/NETWORK_PORT.md`.
- **Review fixes** (an independent review of this release, before it was pushed, found the following; each is reproduced by a test that fails without its fix, and every mutant of the fixes that was tried is killed: the list is at the end of this entry):
  - **The last turns of a match never ran after a stall (HIGH, a regression against v0.0.93).** The runner collects `target + 1` turns again after a stall; a host stops sealing when the match ends, so after a stall that had grown the buffer, or after the last turns came late, fewer than `target + 1` turns stayed queued for ever: the player never saw the match end ("Waiting for the other players..." for ever, then, after the server's grace period, "The connection to the other players was lost." instead of the results).
