The model of the differential reads only the stored directions 0..4 of its tables (it never used the virtual ids of the mirrored directions 5..7). `locate_assets_dir()` of both tests looks for `ants.chd` alone.
- **For an owner of the original game**: put your own copy of `Ants.exe` in `Original-Ants/` (and a decompilation at `docs/legacy/Ants.exe.c`); `tools/analyze_binary.py`, `tools/extract_movement_tables.py` and `tools/movement_reference_model.py` read it and stop with "put your own copy of the original Ants.exe in Original-Ants/" when it is missing (README, "Reverse Engineering").
- **Documents**: `LICENSE` (the exception names what remains: the archive, the maps, the music; no program), README ("License", the project tree with `tests/common/`, "Reverse Engineering" with the local copies and the tools, the suite rows 1.1 and 2.15), `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` (the original game's section lists exactly what remains; cnc-ddraw and the shaders moved to a short "Removed from the repository" note), `AGENTS.md` (rules 4 and 5: the program and the decompilation are local copies that are never committed), `.gitattributes` (comments; no linguist line for the decompilation), `docs/GAME_REVERSE_ENGINEERING.md` (the source artifacts, 2.0 with the pinned regions, 5.32, 5.56), `docs/AUDIT_ONE_TO_ONE.md`, `docs/audit/ledger_screens.md`, `docs/reverse_engineering/movement/README.md`, `tests/TEST_INFRA.md`.
- **Existing tests changed**: the two version assertions of 12.108 (`VERSION_STRING` / `VERSION_PATCH` for v0.0.91) and, in the two table tests, only how they obtain the bytes of the program (above); no test case or assertion was removed or weakened.
- **Still in the history**: this change takes the files out of the current tree only. They stay in the git history of every earlier commit (so in every clone and fork that already has it) until the history is rewritten, which is the next step; after that the old commits no longer provide them, and the restore command above no longer finds them (keep your own copies).

## v0.0.90 - 2026-10-01 - Play online: host a match, join it with a code, any map, 2 to 4 players

`four.html` used to open one match of four players on TINY and show all four seats on one page. It is now the **Play online** page (the game page links to it), for playing with other people:

- **Host**: choose one of the six maps of the original and 2, 3 or 4 players, then "Create the match". The page makes the code of the room (`demo-<map>-<n>p-<random>`); **the room is made on the server when the first player connects, and it waits up to ten minutes for the others**. The page shows the code, a link for anybody (the first free seat) and, per seat, "Play here" (a frame on this page), "Open a window" (a window of its own) and "Copy link" (for a friend on another computer). "All seats on this page" and "All seats in separate windows" do the whole match at once. The map and the number of players are remembered, and the address bar keeps `?room=<code>`: a reload, a bookmark or a copied address comes back to the room's panel.
- **Join**: type the code and your name; the game opens in that window with the first free seat. A link that names a room (`four.html?room=...`) opens its panel at once.
- The match starts by itself when every seat is taken. **A running match takes nobody new** (the server answers `MatchRunning`, or `Full` to a Hello that arrives in the very moment the last seat is taken; nobody already in it is removed). **A code whose room is over (finished or failed) makes a new room** when somebody uses it again: a friend who comes late, a reload, a rematch with the same link (the end of the old room is still reported). Rooms of this page have no computer players yet.
- **In step**: every game on the page or in a window it opened reports its tick and state hash every 100 ticks (a window over the browser's `BroadcastChannel` "ants-sync", with its room code), and the page says whether they agree.
- **The server**: the code of a demo room chooses its map and its players, `demo-[<map>-][<n>p-]<anything>` (`demo-small-2p-x7k2`: SMALL.LVL for two); a map word that the server does not allow keeps the player count (`demo-medium-2p-x` on a server without MEDIUM: the default map, for two); `--demo-maps A.LVL,B.LVL,...` lists the maps a code may choose (blanks around a name are dropped; all must be in the maps folder, checked at startup; a listed map that no room code can name, because its name holds a character that a code cannot hold or is longer than 25 characters without its extension, is a warning at startup); what a code does not choose is 4 players on `--demo-map`, as before. **A demo room waits ten minutes for its players** (it waited one minute: the link goes to friends on other computers). The network protocol is unchanged (the choice rides in the room code). The stack starts the server with the six maps of the original and **12 demo rooms** at a time (`ANTS_DEMO_ROOMS`, `ANTS_DEMO_MAPS`).
- **Fixed by the review before release**: a friend who came after a minute found the room gone (rooms wait ten minutes now; a code whose room is over makes a new one); a map the server does not allow cancelled the player count; "Open a window" on an open seat dropped it from the match (it is brought to the front), "All seats in separate windows" opens only the seats not open yet, "Play here" on a seat already on the page reconnects it; the page said "Is the game server reachable?" during a running match when only some seats were on it; `?players=` took fractions; the "out of step" line named seats that a two-seat room does not have; the name box held 24 characters, the game takes 32; a room of the control interface that was over answered `MatchRunning` (now `NoSuchRoom`); the web build's sync report had an empty character constant (a warning, an error with warnings as errors).
- Tests: suite 2.19 grows from 22 to 25 tests, 10,044 assertions (S3.23 the map in the code: the longest of two names that fit (a temporary maps folder with `BIG.LVL` and `BIG-ISLAND.LVL`), any case, names with dashes, a map that is not in the list, codes that are no code; S3.24 the players in the code: 2 to 4 only, alone, after a map, or after a map that the server does not allow, without a list of maps, a room for two starts with two and turns a third away; S3.25 friends who come late: the default wait is ten minutes (a friend who comes after 61 seconds still gets in), a late Hello to a failed demo room makes a new room and the end of the old one is reported, a room of the control interface that is over (closed by its owner) answers `NoSuchRoom`); suite 3.9 grows from 43 to 55 checks (`--demo-maps` without `--demo-rooms`, with a map that is not in the folder and with an empty name are refused at startup; a good list starts the server, also with blanks around the names; a listed map that no room code can name is a warning; the log names the maps that a code can choose; and five demo rooms made by real clients: SMALL and TINY by their codes, a code without a map and one with a map that is not in the list on the default map, and `demo-small-2p-...` on SMALL for two players while the others have four). Each is proved by a deliberate bug that fails it: a default wait of one minute, an ended demo room that is not replaced, an ended room that answers `MatchRunning` (S3.25); the player count read only after a listed map (S3.24); the first of two names winning instead of the longest (S3.23). Checked in the built-in browser against the real images, after the review fixes: a room on SMALL for two hosted on the page (the address bar shows `?room=<code>`; the server has no such room until the first player connects; seat 0 on the page and seat 1 a native client: the room runs with both seats for more than a minute with no desync, the page says "Playing: Green at tick 300", a third player is told "The match has already started."; a reload comes back to the room's panel, "Play here" on a seat that is on the page reconnects it, and a host who waits alone is still in a waiting room after three minutes), a room on TINY for two with both seats on the page ("In step: all 2 games agree at tick 200"), a game in a window of its own that reports to the page over the `BroadcastChannel`, the join box (a name of 32 characters arrives whole), `?players=2.5` (4 players), and, with native clients, a finished room whose code is used again at once (a new waiting room; the old end is in the server's log). Before the review fixes: a room on ISLANDS and four browser tabs opened with the seat links of one room, in step (tabs, not the page's own "Open a window" buttons: the built-in browser loads such a window into the same tab, so those buttons have not been tried as real pop-ups).
- Rewritten tests: S3.10 (it pinned the one-minute wait of a demo room, which the review proved wrong for friends on other computers: it now sets `demo_wait_ms` to 60,000 for its own timing, its title says "one minute here, ten by default", and the default is S3.25's; nothing weakened) and the version assertions of 12.108. The test client of `test_server` reads the map size with `width()` / `height()` (the plain members were a `-Wconversion` error with GCC 12 and warnings as errors).
- Documents: README (the Play online bullet, the demo options of the server and the stack, the suite rows, the feature table), `docs/NETWORK_PORT.md` (the door, demo rooms, the test counts), the comments of both compose files, of the `Dockerfile` and of `room_manager.hpp`.

## v0.0.89 - 2026-10-01 - The server makes its own control secret, one stack for the site and the server

Deploying the server from a stack (Portainer, auto-deploy from GitHub) failed on the first required value, and the second. This version removes both: the server needs no setup to start.

- **The control secret is made by the server when there is none.** `ANTS_SERVER_SECRET` in the environment is used as before and always wins. Without it the server makes a random secret (32 bytes of the operating system's generator, 64 hex digits) the first time it starts, stores it in a file (`--secret-file PATH`, default `control-secret` in the results folder; an explicit `--secret-file` wins also when `--results-dir` is given, as it is in the image), prints it in its log **the moment it is made** (before the ports are opened and the options are checked, so that a start that fails afterwards has shown it all the same; every later start reads the same file instead and shows only its name: `docker exec ants-server cat /results/control-secret` shows it again; a container with its results folder in a volume keeps it). On POSIX the file is for its owner only (mode 600, whatever the umask: a umask can only make it stricter); **on Windows the server sets no permissions, the file takes those of its folder, which must be private**. The file appears all at once and complete: the server writes a temporary file next to it and links it to its name (POSIX `link()`; Windows `MoveFileEx` without replacing, a branch that is compiled with MinGW, warnings as errors, and has never been run on Windows), so servers that start at the same moment get the same secret, exactly one of them makes it, and a crash leaves no half-written secret (a stale temporary file is harmless: its name is random). It needs a file system with hard links; otherwise the server says so and `ANTS_SERVER_SECRET` or a `--secret-file` elsewhere does the job. **A file that exists is used as it is**, whoever made it: its owner and mode are the operator's responsibility (the server neither checks nor changes them), and a symbolic link is followed (a mounted secret is often one); a link to nothing is an error that says so, and nothing is made behind it. A file that is not a usable secret (a directory, a device or a FIFO, empty, shorter than 32 or longer than 256 characters, a space, a control character (DEL too) or a second line, bigger than 1,024 bytes: no more than 1,025 bytes of a file are ever read) stops the server with a message and is never overwritten; without the variable and without a place for the file a control port refuses to start, as before. A line end, a carriage return or trailing blanks of a hand-written file are not part of the secret (a leading blank refuses the file). A secret that the file reader accepts is always one that the control interface's HTTP server accepts.
- **The images carry what they need.** `Dockerfile.server` copies the six maps of the original game into `/maps`; the stack mounts a volume `ants-maps` there (filled from the image the first time), and `ANTS_MAPS_DIR` is only needed to use other maps (read-only host folder). `docker-compose.server.yml` follows.
- **`docker-compose.stack.yml`**: the web game and the server as ONE stack for Portainer's Git deployment (compose path `docker-compose.stack.yml`, GitOps updates on); only TCP 4001 is public (native clients), browsers reach the server through `/ws` of the site. The demo rooms of `web/four.html` are on there (`ANTS_DEMO_ROOMS`, default 4 and 1 to 255: 0 or 256 and more stop the server at its start and, with `restart: unless-stopped`, restart it again and again; `ANTS_DEMO_MAP`, default `TINY.LVL`; whoever reaches a game port, the site or TCP 4001, can fill them): the page was refused ("no such room") by a server that had them off.
- Tests: suite 2.19 grows from 14 to 22 tests, 9,964 assertions (S3.15 the secret from the environment, made once, for its owner only whatever the umask of the runner is, stable, nothing else left in the folder; S3.16 every kind of bad file and folder is refused with the right sentence and left as it was (leading blank, DEL, exactly 1,024 and 1,025 bytes, a secret padded to more than 1,024 bytes, a device, a FIFO); S3.17 hand-written files; S3.18 the generator at full strength (512 secrets: every one of the 64 positions shows at least 10 of the 16 digits, no position copies another, no zero half) and all 256 byte values at the start, in the middle and at the end against the HTTP server's own rule; S3.19 a 256 MiB file is called too big at once, without being read (time and peak memory); S3.20 symbolic links (to a good file, through a chain of links, to a bad file, to a directory, to nothing, a loop); S3.21 eight threads released together on a fresh folder, 200 rounds: every start works, one secret, exactly one maker, a complete file, nothing else left; S3.22 stale temporary files of every naive name never block a start, and a process that is killed at its first write (SIGXFSZ) leaves nothing that blocks the next one). Each is proved by a deliberate bug that fails it: the code of the first version of this change (S3.19 the unbounded read, S3.20 the false "keeps changing" for a link to nothing, S3.21 the race, S3.22 the crash), `rename()` instead of `link()`, modes 0644 and 0666 (also when the suite runs under umask 077), six weakened generators (a constant, every digit doubled in two ways, 8 or 16 random bytes, bytes copied over the rest, a zero half), DEL accepted, leading blanks trimmed, a bigger size limit, the size branch removed, links not followed, fixed or process-number temporary names, an unbounded read, no check for a link to nothing, a temporary file that is not removed. Suite 3.9 grows from 21 to 43 checks (the real server without a secret: made, 64 hex digits, mode 600 under umask 0, shown once, the same at the second start, a link to the file is followed, a link to nothing stops it with status 2 and says so, the environment wins, a first start that fails after making the secret has already shown it, an empty variable counts as not set, a bad file stops it with status 2 and is left alone, nowhere to keep one is status 2, `--secret-file` alone and together with `--results-dir`).
- Documents: README (server options, "The control secret" with what is true on Windows and for an existing file, the stack, `ANTS_DEMO_ROOMS` is 1 to 255 and 0 or 256 and more stop the server and so restart it again and again under `restart: unless-stopped`; whoever reaches a game port can fill the demo rooms), `docs/NETWORK_PORT.md` (the image carries the six maps; the control secret; the test counts), the headers of `Dockerfile.server` and both compose files, `.gitignore` (`results/`, `control-secret`, `.env`: the README's example run and the compose `.env` files cannot be committed by mistake).
- **The game page links to `four.html` (Play online)** in its header and footer (`web/shell.html`).

## v0.0.88 - 2026-10-01 - Bots B3: the worker bot

The third milestone (B3) of the computer players of [`docs/BOTS.md`](docs/BOTS.md): the first bot that plays, and the fixes of an adversarial review of it. The simulation changed in ONE place, a fidelity fix for human players too (the third bullet); every golden state hash and every existing test is as it was, but for three assertions whose premise was that every bot is the idle bot (see below) and the two version assertions of 12.108.

- **The task model** (`ants_ai/tasks.hpp`): `AntLedger` (which ant belongs to which task: an ant has one owner), `TaskContext`, `Task` (a state machine with `step`, `on_command`, `finish`) and **`HarvestTask`**, the economy. All of a task's state is soft (an ant that was hit, healed, born or stranded is idle and empty-handed and is in the pool again), so a bot may be restarted at any moment: a fresh worker put in the middle of a match finishes within 8 percent of one that never stopped.
- **The worker bot** (`worker_bot.hpp`; kind `worker`; **`standard` is an alias of it until the standard bot of B4**, which the header of `bot_arena`, its report and the docs say): every idle, empty-handed worker is sent to a food pile; a group move per pile starts the engine's own loop (bite, walk home, deliver, walk back), so the whole economy takes 0.001 to 0.055 commands per second. Piles are ranked by points per tick of the trip (Medium, Hard) or nearest first with at most 4 ants to a pile (Easy); a pile takes at most `min(8, trip / 90 + 2)` ants (the hill banks about one deposit per 90 to 110 ticks); at most 8 ants are newly ordered at one look; an ant that cannot walk to the hill's side (another island, a ring of obstacles) is never sent; no trip is started that cannot be finished before the clock runs out (trip + 10 + the level's longest reaction delay); a carrier is never ordered onto a pile (it would walk home and deliver with the order of its loop replaced: a bot that does so fails nine tests of the suite); an invitation to team up is answered with ONE Deny, also through the latency of a room; it never hatches (it is a fixed yardstick, and hatching helps only a little on TINY seat 0 and loses on the other maps: one hatch, mean of the four seats against the plain worker, TINY 1824 (1789), SMALL 1891 (2019), MEDIUM 1614 (1799), GAUNTLET 681 (846), TREASURE 2831 (3028)), fights, raids or uses a power-up. On ISLANDS no hill can walk to any food: it does nothing there until the island hops of B4a (0 = 0, exempt).
- **A fidelity fix of the engine** (found by the review of B3; `src/ants_sim/movement_system.cpp`, `deliver_path`): when a walk is refused with "Can't go there." the ant used to forget the food it was harvesting (`harvest_origin`, the original's `+0xf4`). The original never clears it: the count-0 branch of its path delivery (`FUN_0100cba4`, `0x100cbe7` to `0x100cc29`) stops the ant, plays the can't-go clip and posts text 58, nothing else, and the only writers of `+0xf4` in the whole executable are `SetHolding` and the arrival of a carrier at a pile (a Capstone scan of every operand with displacement `0xf4`). A carrier that was refused and later sent home by a hand therefore walked to the idle tile beside its hill after the delivery instead of back to its pile. New golden case `test_food_actions` 3.11 (it fails on the old engine); every golden and state-hash suite passes unchanged, and so does the pinned bot table. As with every earlier simulation fix, a lock-step game between versions that differ here would show the difference in the state hash check; there is no protocol change.
- **Stuck carriers, found by measuring, explained by the review, and fixed by the clock.** After a bite, or on the way to the gate, the engine's walk home can end in "Can't go there.": a team-mate that stands paused (ANTPAUSE, 300 ms) or idle on the doorway in front of the hill's entrance makes the hill unreachable for the moment of the search (the mound is solid except the entrance and the doorway above it, every path costs 8000 through an occupied doorway, the path manager reads the occupancy live slice by slice, and a node that was blocked when it was reached is not expanded again), the failed search is answered in the same tick, and nothing in the original retries: the ant stands with its food for the rest of the match until a hand orders it home (the whole chain, with addresses, is in `docs/GAME_REVERSE_ENGINEERING.md` 5.40; it replaces "the cause was not isolated"). TINY seat 3 loses a fifth of its score to it (seeds 2, 5, 8: the carrier stands at its pile), TREASURE seat 0 lost one worker of six for 94 percent of the match (seeds 1, 2, 3 alone, 1, 2, 5 with four workers: the carrier stands on the tile in front of the gate). The first rescue looked only at carriers more than 4 tiles from the mound and missed the second case; and ants that queue for the gate are idle with food as well, so no distance can tell (with 12 or more workers on a hill the queue reaches past 4 tiles and that rescue stirred it). **Time tells:** a carrier that has stood idle with its food for 900 ticks (the longest legitimate wait on the five shipped maps is 772 ticks in 100,000 waits; the median is 130) is sent to the entrance by hand, as a person would click it (a carrier more than 4 tiles from the mound with nobody queueing within 4 tiles after 40 ticks; never while 8 or more carriers stand idle at once: that is a deep queue, the gate is the bottleneck and a rescue only stirs it, up to 10 percent lost with 12 to 24 workers). TREASURE seat 0 alone 2795 to 2980, four workers 2265 to 2545 (Medium).
- **Failure learning, ant first, from the tick the order LEFT** (`Bot::on_command`, `Fate::Sent`), not from the tick it was decided (the reaction delay and the budget hide the order from the bot for a while: a timer from the decision blacklisted every pile before the first order had left, in the first prototype). An order that came back `Applied` and did nothing (the ant stands where it stood 24 ticks after the release, empty, no path pending, or it shows the "can't go" reaction within one look of those ticks while it is still within a tile of where it stood) is a failure; whose? The ant's when another ant of the task works the pile or when the ant failed on another pile lately: it is kept away from that pile for 900 ticks and the pile stays; the pile's when two ants that fail nowhere else failed on it or when nobody of the seat makes progress anywhere: blacklisted for 900 ticks for everybody. Before, one ant that was shut in (a nook whose door a stranger blocks) blacklisted one pile after the other: 22 piles learned and 900 points in a hand-made world where the two healthy workers alone make 1050; now at most one pile and 1050. An order that expired, or whose ants died, is forgotten.
- **The map as it is now**: a pile that was shut off at the start (a wall that opens, food that plugs a corridor) and an ant that the start analysis took for shut in are asked again every 200 ticks (a pile behind a plug of 8 units had 60 units left after 6,000 ticks; now it is worked).
- **Packed ants**: one group order of 24 workers on adjacent tiles keeps 15 requests of the engine's path manager pending for the rest of the match (no bot needed: the seat scores 0); the worker orders at most 8 ants at a look, and a watchdog stops ants that walk on one tile for 300 ticks and orders them again in smaller groups.
- **Two things found by measuring**: the analysis of the start has no component for the tiles where a big pile was eaten (they were solid at the start and have no free neighbour), so a first version took the ants that stood there for stranded and left three of six ants of TREASURE seat 3 idle for thousands of ticks (2400 points instead of 2875); and the stuck carrier above.
- **The pinned numbers**: `tests/test_ai/baselines.inc` holds, per shipped map and level, the worker alone against three idle bots after two minutes and over the whole match, and four workers over the whole match, per seat (seeds 1 and 2, the arena's latency of 3 ticks); the test holds them to 8 percent (ISLANDS exactly 0 = 0). **`bot_arena --write-baselines > tests/test_ai/baselines.inc`** writes the table (the procedure is one function in `ants_ai/baselines.hpp`, shared by the test and the tool; a GCC build writes the same table as macOS). The review moved 23 numbers of the 18 rows: one on MEDIUM Easy (seat 2 alone over the whole match 1710 to 1700) and 22 on TREASURE (the stuck carrier; Medium seat 0 alone 2795 to 2980, with four workers 2265 to 2545); TINY, SMALL, GAUNTLET and ISLANDS did not move. With four workers the scores add up to 100.00 to 101.67 percent of the reachable pot on every map (180 matches; 100.00 to 102.50 in 600) and at the end not a unit is left on a pile that a hill can reach: two hills that bite the last unit together both get the points.
- **Tests**: suite 2.22 `test_ai_worker` (21 tests, 4,457 assertions): the margin over the idle bot on the five maps with food on foot (every seat, every level), the economy under 0.15 commands per second, re-targeting at an emptied pile (also from where a big pile stood), failure learning with a 60-tick reaction and a look every 4 ticks and with a stale "can't go", the rescue by the clock (far, at the gate, after a blast, a saturated gate, two whole matches), no Hatch, one Deny (also through the latency of a room), the endgame veto and its margin, the levels not worse than each other, the budget in every window of a whole TREASURE match, four workers sharing the pot, the pinned table, the task model, determinism and replay without a bot, the effect of the level, whose fault a failed order is, the map as it is now, 24 packed workers, deep queues. **68 deliberate mutations of the bot** (the blacklist ignored, carriers ordered, a hatch, no endgame veto or another margin, no component check, a naive one, a timer from the decision tick (two ways), no Deny, a double Deny, no rescue, a rescue of the queue, a wrong ring, clock or gate, the old attribution of a failure, no re-ask, no throttle, no watchdog, no pile cap, Easy by value, Medium nearest first, no `approach_now`, orders that never left not forgotten, blacklists of 90 ticks and for ever, a failure clock of 2 and of 240 ticks, the ledger ignored, `standard` idle, no check that the ant has not moved, ...) **are each caught by at least one test**. `bot_arena --selftest` has 4 more checks (56). `./run_tests.sh --asan` leaves the pinned table (AI3.9, AI3.12) out of the sanitizer run of 2.22 (25 minutes of CPU time with it, under 6 without; neither sanitizer reports anything) and `run_tests.sh` clears `W_ONLY` and `W_SKIP`.
- **Existing tests changed, three assertions plus the two version assertions** (the three had the premise "worker and standard are the idle bot until B3 / B4"; nothing weakened): the registry test of `test_ai` (a `worker` or `standard` bot says "worker"), AI4.3 (the arena report says "worker" for them), AI6.3 of `test_network_app` (the standard bot of the local game sends its ants to the food); and `VERSION_STRING` / `VERSION_PATCH` of 12.108 for v0.0.88.
- **Documents**: `docs/BOTS.md` ("B3 done"; "Prototype measurements" became "Measurements", regenerated from `bot_arena`, with where they differ from the prototype and why), `docs/GAME_REVERSE_ENGINEERING.md` 5.40 (the path of a carrier whose way home is refused), README (bots, suite rows), [`docs/audit/B3_notes.md`](docs/audit/B3_notes.md) (what was measured, the mutation table, the review, the open points).
- Not in this version: the standard bot with its tactics (B4), island hops and the swimmer ferry (B4a), raids, fights, hatching, alliance acceptance, the community-map sweep as a test (B5), bot rooms on the dedicated server (B6).

## v0.0.87 - 2026-10-01 - Bots B2: perception and the arena

The second milestone (B2) of the computer players of [`docs/BOTS.md`](docs/BOTS.md): what a bot sees, the analysis of the map, and the headless arena in which every later bot is measured. Nothing in the simulation changed (every golden state hash and every existing test is as it was); the bots are still the idle bot until B3 and B4.

- **`BotView` is complete**: besides the clock, scores and ants of B1 it holds the seat's OWN egg stock and incubator (nobody else's), the food piles (index in the engine's table, anchor, units left, points per unit, lunchbox or not; an eaten pile leaves the list; two objects on one anchor keep their indices), a read-only reference to the map, the engine's own prediction of which ant would acknowledge an order (`predict_ack`) and whether an order of an own ant is still waiting for its path (`has_pending_path`). Everything the view keeps is a copy (the engine's world state is a cache that is rewritten after any tick or command); the map, the prediction and the pending-path question are borrowed from the engine and valid only while the bot thinks, and a copy of a view has none of them. Other teams' hit points, carried points, eggs and orders are in no accessor, and `has_pending_path` and `predict_ack` say nothing about another team's ants.
- **`MapInfo`** (new): hills (ramp, hole, queue tile, raid tile, waiting spot), a walking-cost field per hill on the engine's own step weights, for every pile and team the cost of the approach and the click tile, walker components (an ant stranded on another island is never sent to a pile of the hill's island), the power-up list, the reachable points of the map, and the trip model (there and back at 0.4 tick per unit of cost plus 70 ticks). The controller owns one and hands it to the bots (`BotContext::map`, `BotView::map()`). It is a hint about the map at the start, not a promise; `approach_now` asks again for a pile that has shrunk.
- **What the engine's own rules turned out to be** (found by holding the analysis against the engine's path finder; no behaviour of the game is changed): a walking ant may stand on a tile that the engine does not mark solid even when the tile's obstacle-overlay flag is set (TREASURE has one at (52, 41)): `TileCell::is_passable` is NOT the engine's rule, the engine's `can_enter` is (terrain, the 4 x 4 mound of every hill, solid objects, other teams' queue rows); the path finder gives up on a path that costs 8,000 or more (a flood fill still reaches the goal); about 3 to 6 percent of the paths it finds cost a little more than the best (worst +11.8 percent); a pile's footprint shrinks as it is eaten, so the click tile of the start may no longer be food; of two objects on one anchor the last owns the cells and sets the points, the first loses the units; the engine's call that ends a match does not advance `current_tick()`, so a replay has to count the calls, not the ticks.
- **The match runner** (`ants_ai/arena.hpp`, pure: no threads, no clock, no files) and **`tools/bot_arena.cpp`** (CMake target `bot_arena`, built on request like `map_sweep`): one or many headless matches with the real engine and the controller, `--map`, `--seeds`, `--seat N=KIND:LEVEL`, `--ticks full|N`, `--latency-ticks` (default 3, a room's 100 ms turn boundary), `--rotate` (every arrangement of the bots over the seats), `--repeat`, `--replay-check` (the applied commands replay into a fresh engine without any bot: same hash at every 20th tick and at the end), `--threads`, `--out report.json` (map names only, no paths; `--no-wall-time` for a bit-reproducible file). The news and audio queues are emptied every tick. The report and the header say that `worker` and `standard` still run the idle bot. A full idle four-team match takes 23 ms (TINY) to 160 ms (TREASURE, ISLANDS) in a release build.
- Tests: suite 2.20 grows from 22 to 46 tests (AI1.1 - AI1.20 and AI4.1 - AI4.4: the view, a conformance test for every rule `ants_ai` takes from the engine, including a flood fill against the engine's PATHMGR on 300 random pairs per shipped map with a fresh engine per pair (0 disagreements), reachable points per map pinned, the match runner and the replay without a bot) and suite 2.21 is `bot_arena --selftest` (52 checks). Switching any one rule of `walkable` or `step_cost` off is caught by at least one test.
- **Documents**: `docs/BOTS.md` ("B2 done": the view, `MapInfo` and the arena, the measured limits), README (the arena, the suite rows), [`docs/audit/B2_notes.md`](docs/audit/B2_notes.md) (what was measured and what differs from the design).
- Not in this version: the worker bot (harvest), the standard bot with its levels, the pinned baselines, bot rooms on the dedicated server.

## v0.0.86 - 2026-10-01 - The map loader reads what the original reads, and a cleanup of the repository

The community's map library (586 files) was run through the engine (`map_sweep`, v0.0.81) and the loader was compared with `Ants.exe` function by function (Capstone, `docs/GAME_REVERSE_ENGINEERING.md` 4.5). Network protocol 6 (unchanged).

- **The loader accepts and refuses what the original does.** The header's version (8) is the only header check: the mode dword is read and never looked at (it used to be refused unless it was 1: 17 files). A file that ends inside block 4 or the final word is read as far as it goes (the original's one `try` block swallows that error; 10 files), a food object without stages ends block 2 where it stands (1 file), every other cut is an error exactly where the original's checked read throws (`Truncated`, naming where). Loads: **522 to 547 of the 586 maps; load failures 64 to 39** (32 truncated before block 4, 6 not maps at all, 1 with a grid of 0 x 0). The 39 are refused for the reasons the original itself fails on them.
- **A start marker outside the grid makes the map unplayable for a roster that contains that team.** The original indexes its row table with the record's row and column and no check (a crash or a heap write there); the remake used to compute an ant position from it with an undefined `int32` shift (three maps, found by the sanitizers). The engine now places no ant for it and the map is refused for that roster only: a team that does not play is never looked at. **Sanitizer findings of the sweep: 3 to 0.**
- **The invented 40 x 40 rule is gone.** A 40 x 40 map used to get two flower droppers and obstacle cells that the original does not add (no rule of the original depends on the size of the map); four community maps played differently, now as the original plays them. The six shipped maps and every golden hash are unchanged, and 514 of the community maps play bit-identically before and after.
- **A question a server can ask**: `LevelValidation`, `LVLParser::check_file` / `check_memory` and `LevelData::validate(roster)` answer "is this map playable by these seats?" with the reasons. **The dedicated server uses it when a room is about to start**: a room whose seated players cannot play the map (a start marker outside the grid for one of them) fails with the reason, and the same map starts when those seats are not taken (S3.14). The game does the same for `--map` and for START on the setup screen.
- **Documents**: `docs/GAME_REVERSE_ENGINEERING.md` 4.5 (the loader as it really is: the version is the only header check, the one `try` block and which reads are checked, the unchecked reads, the dictionary names looked at once for ".", what an out-of-grid marker does), with three statements corrected after its own review (the dictionary names, the `Problem!` box that a read error before block 4 shows, the sentence of 4.4).
- **Cleanup pass of the repository (no behaviour change)**: the result of an audit of the tracked files, the dead code, the build files, the documents and the repository's hygiene (no secret, key or private path was found in the tree, and no tracked file is provably unused). The full suite gives identical results (211 integration tests with 8,012 assertions, every golden result and state hash unchanged, 506 E2E tests), and the build is clean with `-Werror -Wsign-conversion`.
  - **Dead code** (each symbol proven unused by a search of the whole tree and a warning-free build of every target): `MapSelectScreen::quit_button()`, `ScreenEdit::set_text`, `UIRect::contains`, `TextureCache::get_named_sprite_texture`, `operator<<(std::ostream&, Direction)`, `BinaryReader::pos()` (two copies) and the unused `read_u8` of the level parser's reader, `PlayerMatchStats::operator==` (it compared four of the fields and ORed two others: a trap for a future caller; the state hash has its own field-by-field code), the setup screen's `mouse_x_` / `mouse_y_` and `MixerChannel::sound_id` (written, never read).
  - **Build files**: the root `project()` no longer claims version 1.0.0 and the language C (the code base is C++ only; nothing reads the version); the copies of `Original-Ants/Maps` into the build tree and next to the binary are gone (nothing reads them: the game looks in `Original-Ants/Maps` relative to the working directory), and so is the duplicate SDL2 block of `tests/test_app/CMakeLists.txt` (`ants_app` exports SDL2 to its dependants); two `ORIGINAL_ASSETS_DIR` definitions that no source used are removed. New option `ANTS_WERROR` (default OFF) turns every warning into an error; `-Wsign-conversion` is in the warning list (the project rule since the beginning).
  - **A broken ctest**: `test_server` failed under `ctest` (it looked for `Original-Ants` relative to the build folder, `run_tests.sh` ran it from the repository root and hid that); it gets the folder as a compile definition like the other suites, and `ctest` runs 34 of 34. `run_tests.sh` also runs the four asset suites it had never run (steps 1.2 - 1.5: `test_challenger_m1_1`, `m1_2`, `m1_it2`, `m1_it2_2`; 13,204,999 / 12,889,631 / 316 / 1,347,747 assertions).
  - **Repository files**: `.gitattributes` (LF for scripts and sources, CRLF for the Windows batch files; the original's data and the files that are stored with CRLF are never touched), `.editorconfig`, `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` (dr_mp3, Libre Franklin, cnc-ddraw, the libretro shaders, SDL2 / SDL2_ttf), five more lines in `.gitignore`; three Python tools are executable again and three MIDI files are not; trailing blanks removed from eleven lines; five comments that named files which do not exist now name the right ones.
  - **Documents**: `docs/GAME_REVERSE_ENGINEERING.md` held a block of 194 lines twice (the truncated first copy is gone, no text is lost), two sections numbered 5.21, two 5.25 and two `#### 20.` (renumbered, nothing cites the old numbers); the diagonal step cost of 5.28 said the square root of 2 and is 1.4 (the double at `0x10049e0` of `Ants.exe`, used by the `fmul` at `0x1020c24`); older sections that later Capstone-verified ones contradict carry a pointer to them (nothing was deleted), and the invented sound names of the early notes now carry the names of the archive. `docs/BUILD_AND_RUN.md`, `tests/TEST_INFRA.md` and `tests/TEST_READY.md` no longer point to files that do not exist, to a broken anchor, to maps that are not in the game or to a runner path that makes an untracked binary; `docs/NETWORK_PORT.md` and the README were brought up to the code (protocol 6, 131-byte datagram, 40 lock-step tests, the end rules of v0.0.62, no WebRTC, the counts of the suites).
  - **The changelog page**: two entries escaped the asterisk of A* with a backslash, which the page's converter cannot bold across, so the 'Cleanup pass' item rendered with misplaced bold tags; they now write `A*` in code.
- Tests: suite 1 (`test_assets`) has the new group 6c "Loader parity" (12 cases: modes, versions, 24 cut points before block 4, 9 inside it, food without stages, grids, the dictionary limit, 7 placements of a bad start marker, the six shipped maps clean for every roster), suite 2 (`test_powerup_actions` 2.6 and 2.7: no droppers on a 40 x 40 map), the application's `--map` check, and S3.14 of the server suite; `map_sweep` reports the findings per map. The sweep of the 586 maps, in release and under the address and undefined-behaviour sanitizers: no crash, no hang, no nondeterminism.

## v0.0.85 - 2026-10-01 - Bot plumbing, and the allied score box

The first milestone (B1) of the computer players described in [`docs/BOTS.md`](docs/BOTS.md): the plumbing, with a bot that stands still. A bot is a **virtual client** (project rule 8): it reads a read-only copy of what its seat can see and sends ordinary `Command`s through the door a person uses; it never changes the simulation. A game without `--bot` runs no bot code and every golden state hash is unchanged. Network protocol 6 (unchanged: `SlotState::Bot` has been in it from the start).

- **The `ants_ai` library** (new, namespace `ants::ai`, depends on `ants_sim` only: no SDL, no sockets, no threads, no clock, so it builds for the web image too): the `Bot` interface and `Orders` (the only way out of a bot: move, attack, special order of ONE ant, stop, hatch, the alliance commands; its named methods cannot express `Quit` or `Drop`, and the controller filters them anyway), `BotView` (the clock, the numbers of the score boxes and the alliance state of every team, the ants; hit points and carried points of the own ants only; the bot gets no reference to the engine), the three difficulty levels as `Profile`s (Easy / Medium / Hard: a look every 5 s / 1 s / 0.2 s, reaction time 3 s / 1.2 s / 0.4 s, 0.4 / 1.5 / 3.0 commands per second with bursts of 2 / 6 / 10), `parse_bot_spec`, `bot_display_name` ("Bot (Medium)"), `check_setup`, a splitmix64 generator per seat (no `std::random_device`, no distributions, no clock), and the `IdleBot`.
- **`BotController`**: called after every simulation tick; schedules each bot (first look on tick 1 + seat), releases the commands of a look after one reaction time (plus or minus 25 percent, drawn once per look from the seat's own generator, so they leave in the order proposed), lets the newer of two due orders win for an ant, pays for it from a token bucket (one token per command whatever the number of ants), queues by priority (Urgent, Normal, Background) and drops what cannot be paid within the time to live, takes dead ants out of waiting commands (a command with none left never leaves), splits commands at 24 ants (12 at Easy; each part pays), stamps the issuer with the seat, refuses what a person could not click (`Quit`, `Drop`, `None`, empty or oversized ant lists, a special order of other than one ant, a tile off the map, an attack on an ally, an alliance command that a person could not give: an answer without its invitation, a withdrawal without its offer, a break without an ally, an offer to a team that is not in the match), keeps the same ant from being ordered twice within 10 ticks unless the order is urgent, falls silent when the match is over or its team dropped out, and counts everything (`SeatStats`).
- **`--bot SEAT[:SPEC]`** (repeatable; SPEC `easy`, `medium` (default), `hard`, `idle`, `worker`, `standard` or `KIND:LEVEL`): a local game plays the seats that are taken (you and the bots: a team nobody plays has no hill and no ants), the bots are named "Bot (Medium)" on the HUD, in the chat log and on the results screen (`-N` / `--team-name` wins), the controller runs from the last statement of `post_tick` and is torn down with the match. Refused with a message on stderr: a spec that does not parse, a bot on your own seat, two bots on a seat, `--join`; a game started from the setup screen checks again at START and shows the reason (Fog of War) on the setup screen's prompt line. **Until the worker bot (B3) and the standard bot (B4) exist, `worker` and `standard` are the idle bot.**
- **Bot seats in rooms**: `HostLobby::add_bot` / `remove_bot` (a slot of its own kind: counts as a player, round trip 0 so the thumb is good, never waited for at the start; Fog of War and a bot refuse each other in `set_fog` and `add_bot` (and in `start`, a last line of defence that the public interface cannot reach); a person whose name looks like a bot's is renamed, spaces and case ignored, and a bot's name always carries the marker; a room of bots alone cannot start), `HostSession::add_bot_seat` / `is_bot_seat` / `submit_bot` (the host acknowledges every turn for a bot seat, without that the sequencer stalls after 31 turns; also for the host without a seat of a dedicated server; a bot seat never reports a hash and is never dropped for silence), `NetGame::add_bot` / `remove_bot` / `submit_bot`, and `--host --bot SEAT[:SPEC]`: the host's machine runs the bot, guests never do. When a LAN host leaves, its bot leaves with it (the new host drops every seat of the roster that did not follow; documented in `docs/NETWORK_PORT.md`).
- **Documents**: [`docs/BOTS.md`](docs/BOTS.md) is now the final design (status "B1 done"), the README has the option, a Bots section and the suite row, AGENTS.md rule 8 no longer names a Combat Ant guard post patrol (the guard post AI was removed in v0.0.33; the only computer behaviour in the simulation is the original's own auto-engage reflex), and three stale comments that still spoke of the guard AI (`ant_unit.hpp` twice, `sim_engine.hpp`) were corrected (comments only).
- Tests: suite 2.20 (`tests/test_ai`: 22 tests, the vocabulary, the controller with the idle-controller state-hash identity at every tick on TINY, MEDIUM, TREASURE and ISLANDS, and bot seats in rooms: lobby, session, a 60 s match, host migration, a LAN room over real sockets) and eight tests in suite 3.6 (the command line and its refusals, roster and names, results rows, the fog refusal, a room whose host runs a bot, and a bot that acts, in a local game and in a room: its commands go through the application's two sinks, reach the guest in the turn stream and keep both machines bit-identical). No golden hash changed; the version test was bumped.
- **The score box of an allied team showed twice the sum (a bug of the game, found while the bot view was being reviewed).** The engine's `player_scores` are the display scores already (a team's score plus its ally's: `get_display_score`), and the HUD added the ally's entry once more: allied teams with 100 and 50 points showed 300 in both boxes instead of 150. The box now draws the team's own entry (the original's number: the team's score plus its ally's, `FUN_01021e36`). Test `hud_layout` has the engine's values (an allied pair with 300 and 450 points shows 750 in both boxes, 1500 is refused) and a check with the real engine.
- **Fixes from the review of the bot plumbing** (four reviewers, every finding checked by a second agent: 22 confirmed, 1 rejected; all fixed):
  - **The commands of one look could leave in a different order than proposed** (a reaction time was drawn for every command): "stop, then move" could become "move, then stop", and the anti-thrash cool-down made the stale order the last one the engine saw. One reaction time is drawn per look now, so the commands of a look leave in the order proposed, and a later look never leaves before an earlier one (AI2.13). Among orders that are due together the newer wins an ant from the older (the later click of a person; an older order left with no ant is never sent: `Fate::Superseded`, `SeatStats::superseded`); an order that is not due yet takes nothing from the ones before it, so a bot that re-issues an order at every look is not starved by its own newer looks (AI2.14).
  - **Alliance commands follow the HUD**: an answer needs the invitation it answers, a withdrawal the offer it takes back, a break an ally, an offer a team that is in the match and has not dropped out (AI2.15).
  - **The view shows what the score boxes show** (a team's score plus its ally's, never below 0) and nothing more: it used to give the individual scores. AI2.16 tests it on a hand-made world: other teams' hit points and carried points hidden, the own ants exact, dropped teams, invitations.
  - **A bot has no reference to the engine** any more (`BotContext` lost `sim`): the view is the only window, as rule 8 says.
  - **Names**: a bot's name always carries the marker ("Zed" becomes "Bot (Zed)"), and a person's name that looks like a bot's is renamed whatever the spaces or the case (" Bot (x)", "bot(x)", "B o t (x)"); **a room of bots alone cannot start** (it needs a person).
  - **The start scripts**: `--bot` is an option of one game (like `--host`), and is refused together with `--players` (the extra windows are guests, and a guest runs no bots); suite 3.8 has 93 checks.
  - **Tests that could not fail now can** (the match-over guard of the controller, the unknown slot state of the wire, the application's two bot sinks: a bot that acts is run through both and its orders reach the simulation, the sequencer and the guest's copy of the game).
  - **Documents**: the 24-ant cap is a chosen cap (the HUD allows 32, the original's limit is not established); the reference numbers are measurements of a prototype worker bot that is not in the repository yet; the server and web bullets are marked as the B6 design; rule 8 says what is true of names; B1 notes and counts corrected.
- Not in this version: the map analysis and the food piles in the view, the worker bot, the headless tournament runner, the standard bot with its levels, bot rooms on the dedicated server (the room specification), replays.

## v0.0.84 - 2026-10-01 - The browser joins the server: four games on one page, and the first security fixes of the server

The web build can play through the game server. Network protocol 6 (unchanged).

- **A WebSocket client for the browser** (`include/ants_net/wasm_ws.hpp`, `src/ants_net/wasm_ws.cpp`, Emscripten's `websocket.js`): a `Connection` over the page's own WebSocket (binary messages, one message = one protocol message, a text frame or an oversized one fails the connection, at most 4096 messages queued while the page does not poll). `NetGame::join_url` joins through it (the browser build had no network at all); a server's room has no host migration and no links between guests, so the browser needs nothing more. `--join-url ws://host/path` (or `wss://`) is the new option; a native game refuses it with a message (it joins with TCP: `--join host[:port]`).
- **The page takes the room from its address** (`web/shell.html`): `?join=/ws&room=<code>&seat=<0-3>&name=<name>`; `join` must be a path on THIS site (nginx passes `/ws` to the server's WebSocket port, v0.0.83), the room code, seat and name are checked before they become program arguments. `&embed=1` makes the page a frame of another page: no header, guide or start click, music and sound only while the frame has the focus (`--audio-focus`).
- **`web/four.html`: four games on one page, one match through the server.** Four same-origin frames (Green, Red, Blue, Black, random names) join one room through `/ws`; the match starts by itself when the fourth is in. Every 100 ticks each game tells the page its tick and state hash (`postMessage`, from `Application::post_tick`) and the page compares the four: "In step" or "OUT OF STEP at tick N" with the hashes. Checked in the built-in browser against the real containers (web image, server image with a native Linux referee, nginx's `/ws` proxy in between): all four connected, the match began, the four hashes agreed at every report (ticks 100 - 900 and on), the server's referee never complained, and a move order given in one window went through the server to everybody (the other windows kept the same hash). The frames run slowly inside the embedded browser pane (about 10 frames per second each, the game clock about a third of real time); the lock-step waits for the slowest window by design.
- **Demo rooms** (`ants_server --demo-rooms N --demo-map NAME`, off by default): a Hello for a room "demo-..." that does not exist makes it (4 players, the demo map, at most N at a time, one minute to fill, forgotten half a minute after it ended), so a public test page needs no secret to make rooms with. The price is documented: whoever reaches the door can fill these rooms. Real rooms are still made by the control interface. Test S3.10.
- **Security review of the new network code** (a read-only attack on the WebSocket transport, the control interface, protocol 6 and the map names, every finding verified by a second agent; fuzzing with millions of inputs under the address and undefined-behaviour sanitizers found no memory-safety bug, no authentication bypass and no request smuggling). Fixed:
  - **WebSocket: quadratic cost of a flood of pings (critical, CPU exhaustion).** `append_frame` reserved exactly the size it needed on every frame, so every few frames copied the whole output backlog; a peer that never reads and sends pings cost the server seconds (762,000 pings: 8.1 s, now 0.024 s). Now the buffer grows with slack. Test W1.18 (third part).
  - **Control interface: 32 silent connections locked it out (low).** A new connection now takes the slot of the oldest one that is waiting for its head with nothing unread on its socket (or of the oldest that only waits to close); connections that are being answered, are receiving a body or have a request waiting are never evicted. The test of the old policy (CTL2.17: "the rest are closed at once") was rewritten for the new one.
  - Already fixed in v0.0.82: the sequencer kept reports of turns that were never sealed (a client could make its maps grow). Open: an anthill start marker outside the grid (map library work).
  - Looked at and not changed: a short or guessable control secret is the operator's choice (the server refuses an empty one and the documents say "long random"); no rate limit on wrong secrets (documented: the loopback address and the secret are the protection).
- **Fixes from the review of this release's own work** (four reviewers, every finding checked by a second agent: 24 confirmed, none rejected; all fixed here):
  - **A room never started after 24.8 days of server uptime (high).** The room's retry time started at 0 and was compared as a signed difference, so once the server's clock (its uptime in milliseconds) passed 2^31 a full room neither started nor timed out. Every millisecond deadline of the network code is now wrap-safe (`include/ants_net/clock.hpp`, `time_reached`): the turn sealing, the request limit, the pings, the election hold, the lobby's ping timer, the room's start; the simulated network of the tests keeps a 64-bit time. Test S3.11 plays rooms across the signed flip and across the 32-bit wrap (it fails with the old code).
  - **A seat that stops executing turns held a room for ever (medium).** The match clock only advances while every seat executes turns, and a seat that kept answering pings was never dropped. A seat that has held the match up for 20 seconds is dropped (`HostSession::Config::laggard_drop_ms`, set by the server's rooms; peer-to-peer games still wait), and a running room has a wall-clock limit (`max_run_seconds` in the room spec, 2 hours by default, 30 minutes for demo rooms). Test S3.12.
  - **128 silent connections could lock every player out of the door (medium).** The door now has the same policy as the control interface: a new connection takes the place of the oldest silent one. A room with no keep time (`keep_seconds: 0`) was forgotten before its end was reported: now it is reported. Demo rooms write no result file and no log line unless a match ran (a peer chooses these codes), and `--demo-rooms` is validated (a positive number below `--max-rooms`). Test S3.13, and startup checks in suite 3.9 (21 checks).
  - **Control interface: the eviction never sacrifices a request that has arrived but has not been read yet** (an accept pass that took 33 connections could have evicted a real request); CTL2.17b tests the all-waiting, the mixed and the request-waiting cases.
  - **Browser client:** the Hello is sent when the socket opens (`ClientLobby::send_hello`), not at the next frame: a tab that is not drawn runs no frames, and the server closes a connection that says nothing for 10 seconds. The close code of a protocol error is a legal one (4002; a browser throws on 1002 and the socket stayed open), the socket is always closed when the connection is dropped, the address check is stricter (no fragment, bad port or bad host: `new WebSocket` cannot throw out of `main`), and the page reporting uses `EM_JS` (no `$0` warnings under `-Wpedantic`).
  - **Page:** "New match" can no longer leave the site (a path that began with `//` made it an open redirect); a second match in the same page no longer shows a false "OUT OF STEP"; the frames no longer scroll the page or take the focus from the frame you play; `join` accepts only `/ws` and below; the name trimming is no longer quadratic; the verdict bar stays on screen; the "OUT OF STEP" text names every seat.
  - **Tests and documents:** a vacuous `--join` assertion and the ping-flood test's assumption about the host's socket buffers are fixed; S3.10 checks the demo defaults and that a failed demo room is forgotten; the documents now say what a native `--join-url` does (it refuses with a message), that the four-games page needs a server started with demo rooms, and the statements that this release made stale are corrected.
- Tests: suite 2.19 has 13 tests (210 assertions), 2.13.1 39 tests, 2.17's W1.18 has the ping flood, 3.6 has 18 tests (306 assertions), 3.9 21 checks; the web image and the server image build.

## v0.0.83 - 2026-10-01 - The dedicated server program

`ants_server`: a headless program that hosts many games at once, each run by a referee that plays nobody (implementation plan section 43). It is a new program; the game itself changes only in what it needs to talk to it. Network protocol 6 (unchanged).

- **Rooms made from outside.** A lobby's backend creates a room over a control interface (HTTP + JSON, `POST /rooms {"map", "players", "fog", "code", "seed"}`, `GET /rooms`, `GET /rooms/<code>`, `DELETE /rooms/<code>`, `GET /stats`) that checks a **bearer secret** (read from the environment variable `ANTS_SERVER_SECRET`, never the command line; a control port without a secret refuses to start). Clients join with `--join HOST:PORT --room CODE`; the match **starts by itself** when the expected seats are taken and everybody has loaded the map (nobody presses START), runs on the server's own engine as the referee, and the result rows are kept for the status call and written to `<code>.json` in `--results-dir`.
- **One door for TCP and WebSocket.** Native clients connect to the TCP game port, browsers and Electron to the WebSocket port behind a reverse proxy that ends TLS (`--ws-port`); the first message must be a Hello within 10 s, its room code names the room, a Hello for another room / protocol / anything else is answered with a Reject. Limits: rooms (`--max-rooms`, 256), connections that have not said Hello (128), a room that nobody joins fails after two minutes, a client that cannot load the map cancels the start (a few tries, with a back-off).
- **A map library that is not part of the image.** The `.lvl` files are read from `--maps DIR` (a mounted folder); a room whose map is missing, invalid or does not load is refused at once (404 / 400), so a started room can always be played.
- **Docker.** `Dockerfile.server` builds the program alone (`-DANTS_BUILD_APP=OFF`: no SDL, no assets; the new CMake option skips the game program and its tests), runs as an unprivileged user with a healthcheck on the game port; `docker-compose.server.yml` is the example service (secret and maps folder from the environment, only the game port public, WebSocket and control ports on the host's loopback address, read-only root file system, no capabilities). `--ws-any-interface` / `--ctl-any-interface` exist for the container, where a published port does not reach a program on the container's loopback address (the host's port mapping decides who can connect).
- Tests: suite 2.19 (`test_server`: the map store, rooms, the door, the control calls, over real sockets; 9 tests, 146 assertions) and 3.9 (the real `ants_server` and two headless game clients: the secret, a room by code, an automatic start, a clean stop; 16 checks). Checked with the image: the control interface with and without the secret, a room made, a WebSocket upgrade through the published port, the healthcheck.
- Not in this version: the browser's WebSocket connection and the four-games-on-one-page test, the in-game host / join screens, bot rooms, a token check by the lobby.

## v0.0.82 - 2026-10-01 - A host without a seat: the referee of a dedicated server

The core of the dedicated server (implementation plan section 43): a host that plays nobody. Network protocol 6 (unchanged). No new option for players yet: the server program that uses it is the next step.

- **`HostSession` without a seat** (`host_player = kNoSeat`): the host seals the turns, runs the match on its OWN engine as the **referee** and compares every client's state hash with its own (`Sequencer::on_referee_hash`; a diverging client is named within a second with the subsystem, everybody is told, the match stops sealing): suite S2.1 - S2.2 (four clients over 25 - 120 ms links with jitter, 60 s, the referee's engine equal to every client's). The host has no commands and no chat of its own.
- **`HostLobby` without a seat** (`host_seat = 255`): the guests take the seats from 0, the room message has no Host slot (that is how a client knows it is in a server's room), `min_players` says how many seats a start needs, the LOADED / READY barrier is the same (the server loads the map itself and reports `host_loaded`): test N4.5b.
- **No host migration in a server's room** (`ClientSession::Config::migration = false`, chosen by `NetGame` when the room has no Host slot): a client whose server link dies does not elect itself the host of a one-player game, the match is lost on that machine and the server drops the seat at the same tick for everybody else (S2.4).
- **A hostile client cannot make the sequencer's hash maps grow**: a report for a turn that was not sealed yet is dropped (it used to be kept forever); the old sequencer test now seals the turns it reports (S2.3).
- Not in this version: the server program, the room manager, the control interface calls, the map store and the "map is playable" gate (the open loader issues of the sweep are listed in the implementation plan, section 43).

## v0.0.81 - 2026-10-01 - Protocol 6, community map names, and the first pieces of the dedicated server

The map library of the owner's lobby (586 `.lvl` files) was run through the engine for the first time; the server pieces that do not depend on the room logic were built and tested. Network protocol 6 (**not compatible with 5**).

- **Map names of the community work.** The protocol accepted only letters, digits, `_`, `-`, `.` and 32 characters: **348 of the 586 names** (spaces, `!`, `#`, `~`, `&`, `'`, `$`, `..`, and two names over 32 characters) could not be chosen, shown or started. A name may now hold any printable ASCII character except `/ \ : * ? " < > |` (so it can never name a path), up to 64 characters, and may not start with a dot; the LAN announcement grew to at most 160 bytes.
- **Room code and token in Hello** (`--room CODE`, `--token T`): a server hosts many rooms, a lobby with a room code answers a Hello for another room with `NoSuchRoom`; the slot state `Bot` exists for the bots to come (`docs/BOTS.md`). Tests: names (the real kinds of names, every refused character, the length limit), room codes, tokens, the rejections, the command line.
- **Engine: a second match on the same engine began with the first match's attack times** (`last_attacked_ms_` was not reset by `init`, and is part of the state hash): a rematch, or a server that reuses an engine, gave a different hash than a fresh start. Found by the sweep (437 maps, 835 plays), fixed.
- **Engine: the power-up pick of a flower dropper converted a probability of 1e13 and more with an undefined cast** (one community map: x86 and Apple Silicon would pick different power-ups: a desync). It is now the original's `__ftol` (0x1034580: truncate to 64 bits, keep the low 32 bits, the sum wraps like a 32-bit register); test N1.9b.
- **WebSocket server transport** (`src/ants_net/ws.cpp`): RFC 6455 for browsers and Electron, checked with Node's and Python's WebSocket clients and through nginx (suite 2.17, 22 tests, 1.17 million assertions). **Control interface primitives** (`src/ants_ctl`): a strict JSON library and a loopback HTTP server with a bearer secret (suite 2.13.1, 38 tests). Neither is used by the game yet.
- **`map_sweep`** (`tools/map_sweep.cpp`, optional target; `--selftest` runs in the suite as 2.18): over the 586 maps, 1,035 plays, no crash and no hang (also under the address and undefined-behaviour sanitizers); a long match costs about 83 microseconds per tick on average (at most 1.2 ms, 282 ants, 13 MB), so a server holds hundreds of games per core. Still open (implementation plan section 43): a start marker outside the grid is not rejected (undefined behaviour on three maps), the loader refuses 26 maps that the original reads, and a fixed fallback for 40 x 40 maps alters four community maps.

## v0.0.80 - 2026-10-01 - The setup screen of host and guest, the START button, music with several games, the cursor over dialogs

Five reports of the owner, each checked in `Ants.exe` first (implementation plan sections 44 - 48). Network protocol 5 (unchanged).

- **Your own name is the first row of the setup screen.** Row i of the original's screen is slot i of the machine's OWN peer table: the local machine is slot 0 on host and guest alike, a guest's slot 1 is the host, the others follow in the order they were met; the portrait colour follows the player's name, not the row (docs 5.50). The host and the local screen keep seat r in row r (a hole stays blank); a guest shows itself, then the host, then the others; the local row always has the good thumb; a guest that is still connecting shows itself alone.
- **The guest has another screen, as in the original.** `FUN_01014228` builds the composite `nh_start` ("Game Set-Up", "Map", "WAITING FOR GAME TO START!", a fixed "Fog of War?" box showing No, with `d_fowyes` over it when the host chose fog): no Up / Down arrows, no START, no Fog of War buttons; the map name is the host's file name, the description comes from the guest's own copy of the file ("???" without it); the keys are Q / X only; the buttons that are not there get no pointer events. The host's screen and the local screen are unchanged. (A rule found on the way and not changed: the original has no two-player minimum and a refused START is silent.)
- **The START button.** The original's own data puts START at (529, 437) on the quick help and at (526, 439) on the setup screen (pressed pictures (528, 438) and (527, 443)): both buttons are built at (0, 0) in windows of (0, 0, 640, 480) and nothing in the code lines them up, so the jump at the hand-over is the original's (docs 5.52); the remake drew it exactly. What was the remake's own: (1) the hit test is now the rectangle of the picture that shows (a press must hit the resting picture, every move until the release the pressed one: dead strips, the click zone is where the two overlap; the same rule for Leave, Up, Down, the in-game Return, the options Return and the results Leave); (2) the new screen's button shows hover at once when the pointer is on it (the pointer is one global; the application replays it into the new screen, and tracks it on the loading screen too); (3) the frame-rate and version plate covered the bottom rows of the setup START: it now starts at row 467 (`include/ants_app/fps_overlay.hpp`).
- **Music with several games on one machine.** The original closes the music when the program is deactivated and starts a NEW random piece when it is activated (`WM_ACTIVATEAPP`): with four windows every click on another window cut a piece. A window started with `--audio-focus` (the start scripts) now holds its music (paused and silent) and goes on with the same piece when it has the focus again; a piece that starts meanwhile starts held; a window that opened behind the others starts silent. Without `--audio-focus` the original's rule is unchanged.
- **The cursor over a dialog is the normal arrow.** Every dialog window sets cursor mode 1 when it is attached (the thunk at 0x101279a in slot +0x20 of all 15 window vtables) and nothing that runs while it is open writes another mode; the remake kept the cursor of the moment the dialog opened, so the scroll arrow showed over the team invitation and the attack crosshair over "Doing this will break your team". Fixed in `HUD::evaluate_cursor` (docs: implementation plan 47).
- **`-pnum:<team>`**: the original spells it with a colon; the remake accepts both `-pnum:` and `-pnum=`.
- **Found by the review of this release and fixed**: the host's setup screen stayed locked after a cancelled start (a guest left while the map loaded: the map and START did nothing until the screen was left); the results screen's Leave button reacted to the right and middle buttons (the original's button class knows the left one only), and did not hover under a pointer that was resting on it; the guest's hidden buttons got a pointer state on the release; a window that was started behind the others now also holds the setup screen's intro; after a network match the local game played the seat of the match instead of the configured team; the pointer is no longer reset when the setup screen comes back (it is one global); a connecting guest takes its own seat as soon as the host has assigned it. Tests added for all of it (zones of Up, Down, Leave, the results Leave and the options Return; the cursor over the options, quit and quick help dialogs).
- Documents: `docs/BOTS.md` (the design of bot players, rule 8 amended: virtual clients only), `docs/GAME_REVERSE_ENGINEERING.md` 5.44 / 5.45 / 5.50 / 5.52, `.gitignore` (the per-user `.claude/` folder and the private working plan).
- **Not changed, on purpose**: the first ant of an allied row on the results screen hangs over the left frame of its box (the original places it at x = 45 with a part offset of -11; plan 48).
- **Windows (MSVC) build: no more `C4068 unknown pragma 'GCC'`** (no behaviour change). `audio_mixer.cpp` switched GCC's warnings off around the third-party `dr_mp3` implementation with `#pragma GCC diagnostic` lines that MSVC does not know (six warnings, reported from a Windows build); the lines are now compiled for GCC and Clang only (`dr_mp3.h`'s own pragmas were guarded already). Checked by preprocessing the file with `__GNUC__` and `__clang__` undefined: no GCC pragma is left; the macOS build, the 29 suites and the web image are unchanged. `start_game.bat` also hands the game's exit code on (`!errorlevel!`; `%errorlevel%` inside a block is read before the game runs).

## v0.0.79 - 2026-09-30 - Four games on one machine, and Shift-selected groups of the original

A bug the owner found on ISLANDS (a bomber could not hit a bomb of its own team), the start scripts, the window and pointer behaviour of several games on one screen, and the web player's page. Network protocol 5.

- **Shift and the bomb: the panel is stored, not counted.** Re-read in `Ants.exe` (implementation plan section 41): the cursor and order code never read Shift; the original's panel `[54ec]` (3 = one ant, 4 = several) is whatever the last selection operation passed to `SetPanelMode`, and a Shift click that adds an ant or a Shift drag that picks any ant sets panel 4 **even when the selection holds one ant** (for instance a box dragged around the bomber that is selected already). The automatic target cursor (`panel == 3`) and the ability pedestal (slot 2 is kind 9, hidden, in panel 4) are gone then, so the click on a bomb is a plain group move: the bomber walks onto the bomb of its own team and sets it off, which is how a group gets off the island of ISLANDS. The remake recounted the panel from the selection and could not reach that state; it also offered the ability pedestal (and a target cursor with it) to several bombers, which the original never does. Both fixed; the status line follows the panel as well ("Ready!" for a panel 4 group of one). Recorded, not ported: a Shift leave from such a group of one leaves panel 4 with nothing selected.
- **A player's move order always ends on the bomb.** `FUN_010202e7` gives the tile test flag 0x20 (ignore bombs) for every player order, so a move onto a bomb of the ant's own team is not moved to a neighbour tile. The simulation's order API had an opt-in flag for this (`allow_friendly_bomb`, off by default, switched on by the command layer when a bomb lay at the tile); it is removed (from the orders, the unit and the state hash) and the rule is unconditional. No change for the game itself; an API caller that used the default got the invented behaviour.
- **Four games in a grid** (`start_game.sh` / `start_game.bat`, see the README): window i is player i and colour i (0 green, 1 red, 2 blue, 3 black), window 0 hosts on this machine only, the others join and **ask for their seat** (`--seat N`; network protocol 5: the Hello message carries the wanted seat, a taken seat gives the first free one; a Hello of another protocol version is still answered with "version mismatch": `decode_hello_prefix` reads only its version and name), random names, `--grid 2x2 --cell N` puts a window into its quarter of the usable display (the largest 4:3 client area that fits the cell with its title bar and frame), `--audio-focus` silences a window that has not the focus, `--title`, `--window-size`, `--window-pos`, `--display`.
- **The pointer is not captured.** The system cursor is hidden only while the pointer is over the game window (the game draws its own there) and shown on every other window and the desktop; leaving the window stops the edge scroll (the last position used to keep the map scrolling) and hides the game's cursor; the first click on a window that was in the background is also a click in it (`SDL_HINT_MOUSE_FOCUS_CLICKTHROUGH`).
- **The web player's page** (`web/shell.html`): the controls are the real ones (checked against the web build: the original's pointer model, keys, Ctrl shortcuts that the page keeps from the browser, touch), the version is filled in at build time, Space and the letters are left to the chat box.
- Docs: README (scripts, options), `docs/GAME_REVERSE_ENGINEERING.md` (the stored panel, the pedestal of panel 4, flag 0x20; the old Shift / bomb notes corrected), implementation plan sections 41 (this bug) and 42 (the owner's report that a right-click dig shows another cursor for a second or two: not found in the binary, the owner's description is awaited).
- Tests first: `test_pointer_model` `test_shift_group` (panel 3 by click and drag, the defuse; Shift drag around the lone bomber: one ant, panel 4, Move cursor, no pedestal, the click is a move onto the bomb and the bomb goes off, also with the right button; Shift click join / leave / last leave) and the corrected two-bomber expectation, `test_ability_actions` 4.9 / 4.10, integration 12.11 (asserted the invented redirect), `test_lobby` N4.2b / N4.2c and `test_netgame` N3.16 (seat requests, Hello layouts), `test_input_model` (window layout maths), `test_network_app` N5.1b and N5.15 - N5.19 (the options, window placement, pointer leave / enter, sound focus, `--seat` through the application), new suite 3.8 (`tests/scripts/test_start_game.sh`: 82 checks over the script's dry runs). The four-window rig was started here with the real binary: one room on 127.0.0.1, three guests connected (window positions could not be looked at: the screen was locked).

## v0.0.78 - 2026-09-30 - Games on the local network

First slice of "local networking first" (implementation plan section 38). No change to the simulation, the renderer or any state hash.

- **An open room announces itself on the local network.** `ants_net/lan.hpp`: the host sends one small UDP datagram a second (and at once when the room changes) to the directed broadcast address of every IPv4 interface that is up, to the limited broadcast and to this machine; it says who hosts, which map, how many seats are taken, which protocol and game version and on which TCP port to connect. Only while the room is open (a running match is offered to nobody, a goodbye goes out when the match starts, the room closes or the host leaves; a start that fails puts the room back on the list). A browser (`LanBrowser`) listens on UDP 4001 with address and port reuse (two copies on one machine share it), keeps at most 64 rooms, drops a room after 3.5 s of silence or at once on a goodbye, lists a room heard over several paths once (at its LAN address), and marks rooms of another protocol version.
- **The decoder reads the open network, so it is strict**: exact length, magic, format, known type, `1 <= players <= seats <= 8`, a port, printable ASCII only, every string within its limit; 20,000 random and mutated datagrams never crash it and every accepted one is exactly what the encoder writes.
- **`ants --lan-list [seconds]`** (new, no window and no assets needed) prints what the local network offers (`192.168.1.20:4001  "Queen Anne"  TINY.LVL  1/4 players  v0.0.78`), exit code 0 when a room was heard, 1 when none was, 2 when the port cannot be used; `--lan-port N` picks another UDP port (both sides must agree), `--no-lan` keeps a room off the network. Checked here with the real binary against a room announcing on the real port: it was listed with the LAN address and was gone after the host left.
- **Firewalls**: a host needs UDP and TCP port 4001 open; macOS may ask once for permission to use the local network.
- Docs: README (options, network table, suites), `docs/NETWORK_PORT.md` (LAN discovery; the WebRTC / TURN milestones are dropped: internet play is provided from outside the game), implementation plan sections 38 - 40 (next: the in-game host / join screens, the launcher interface with a documented match API, replays).
- Tests first: `test_lan` (new suite 2.16: 12 tests, 4,995 assertions: layout, limits, refusals, fuzzing, loopback announcer and browsers, changes, goodbye, expiry, telling rooms apart, garbage, the size of the list, the pace, broadcast), `test_netgame` N3.13 - N3.15 (the room announces its host, map and players, follows changes, leaves the list at the start, returns after a failed start, is gone when the host leaves, stays silent with the discovery off), `test_network_app` N5.0 (the new options) and N5.0b (`--lan-list`). The existing network tests no longer announce on the real network. Version assertions of 12.108.

## v0.0.77 - 2026-09-30 - The movement regression net, and honest test documents

Batch 8 of the audit (tests and documents; `docs/AUDIT_ONE_TO_ONE.md`, `docs/audit/ledger_movement.md` NEW-M7 / NEW-M8, `docs/audit/ledger_combat.md` NEW-5). **No behaviour change**: the simulation, the renderer and every state hash are untouched.

- **Movement differential test** (`tests/test_sim/test_movement_differential.cpp`, suite 2.15 of `run_tests.sh`): two independent models of the original, written from the disassembly for the movement audit and fed only with the raw data of `Ants.exe` (the static animation tables) and `ants.chd` (the Table-4 frames), are compared with the remake on random scenarios. They share no code with `src/ants_sim`.
